# Masterplan state — frozen archive

> Historical detail lifted out of `.claude/skills/masterplan/STATE.md` on **2026-07-23**, when the live file reached 339 KB and no longer fit in a single `Read` (Step 0 of the skill assumes it does).
>
> **This file is never the source of truth for what to do next** — that is `STATE.md`. Nothing here is live state; it is the written record of how closed lanes were built.
>
> Everything below is **verbatim** as recorded by the task that wrote it, in original order. Grep it by task id (`RU.7`, `NT.5`, `ST.4`) or by filename before touching a file a closed lane built.
>
> Contents: the superseded header block, header "prior" paragraphs for HV.1–GR.2, 96 deviation entries and 127 gotchas covering **Wave 0** (UI.1/8/9/10, ST.1–ST.9), **Wave 1** (RU.1–RU.3, N.1–N.9, ME.1–ME.7, AI.1–AI.13) and **Wave 2** (RU.4–RU.10, AX.1–AX.7, RL.1–RL.8, NT.1–NT.7), plus the HV.1–HV.5 sha-reconciliation entries superseded by D97.
>
> Still live in `STATE.md` and deliberately NOT duplicated here: D1–D11, D13, D93, D96a–c, D97, D98, D99, D100, D100a, D101; the standing test/lint/typecheck traps; the browser-verification debt; every HV/GR gotcha.

Prior: GR.3 = `feat(following): curation queue with jittered cadence caps (GR.3)`, whose parent is NOT GR.2 — the out-of-band cost-fix commit (`fix(metrics): discovery pull IS the snapshot read + reply-reread trim (cost audit)`, parent `edf3dfb` = GR.2) sits between them. Prior: GR.2 = `feat(following): harvester following-mode scrape (GR.2)`, parent `74076ad` (= GR.1). Prior: GR.1 = `feat(following): ledger schema + ingest/ratchet routes (GR.1)`, parent `7e66a22` (= HV.6). No drift at GR.1 start (HEAD was `7e66a22` with the HV.6 subject, exactly as expected). Prior: HV.6 = `docs(harvest): Harvest 2.0 docs-sync + $0 smoke-passive-harvest.ts (HV.6)`, parent `3416f14` (= HV.5). **No sha is recorded any more (D97):** three consecutive tasks (D93a/D94a/D95a) burned a Step-0 investigation reconciling an amend-orphaned sha, and the header already said the subject line was authoritative — so it is now the ONLY thing recorded. ## Archived header "prior" paragraphs (GR.2 back through HV.1)

- **prior (GR.2):** the ledger's client half — **extension-only, $0, no server/schema/migration/CSS change** (suite 1190→**1207**, +17). `HarvestMode` gains `'following'`; the scroll engine is reused untouched and `runHarvest<R>`'s `toIngest` param became **`ship(profile, store, outcome)`** (same arity — posts/replies pass a `shipToStratus` closure and ignore the outcome). The loop now records `reachedBottom` at the stable-height break — **the only exit that means "I saw everything"** — so `outcome.complete = reachedBottom && !cancelled` is what decides whether the final `/x/following/rows` batch carries `done: true`. New pure `extension/src/shared/userCell.ts` (`parseUserCell` + `handleFromAvatarTestid`, 11 fixture tests) reads a `[data-testid="UserCell"]`: avatar-testid handle first / profile-link second, display name = first innermost `[dir]` block that isn't `@…` or the badge's own text, `followsBack` = badge presence. `harvestFollowing` assigns `listPosition` on first sighting (virtualized list) and on re-sighting only ORs `followsBack` / fills a null name. New `loggedInHandle()` guard aborts `not_own_following` on a positive mismatch only.
- **prior (GR.1):** the GR lane's opener — two new tables (`following_runs`, `following`, migration **`0019`**), a new **$0** `src/x/routes/following.ts` (`POST /following/runs|rows`, `GET /following`, `PATCH /following/:handle`) mounted right after `harvest`, and a 14-test route suite (1176→**1190**). One DOM scrape of my own `/following` page yields both sides (X's "Follows you" badge), so no API sync and no `follows.write` scope. The load-bearing rule: **presence is ground truth, absence only counts when the scroll finished** — batches update what they see, only a `done:true` run **that saw ≥1 row** reconciles (unseen `active|queued`→`gone`, unseen `done`→`confirmed`, seen `done`→`queued`, the one reverse edge). Three plan-gaps closed in D99: revive-on-sighting without resetting `first_seen_at`, the zero-row run that closes but is never `complete`, and `rows: []` being legal with `done:true`.
- **prior (HV.6):** the HV sub-plan's closer — **no code, schema, route or test change** (suite stays **1176/0**): one new `scripts/smoke-passive-harvest.ts` + seven docs (`harvest-tab`, `settings-tab`, `people-tab`, `playbook-tab`, `README`, `PLAN.md` blockquote, `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md` entry) + codemap §9/§11/header. The smoke drives ingest → recapture → oversized-400 → 61-day prune → affinity → `timelineFunnel` in-process over the **real** DB and is safe there by construction (888-prefixed rows, `hv6_*` run handles, keep-and-recompute for a pre-existing passive run — D98c). Wave 3 now has only the GR lane left.
- **prior (HV.5):** the ambient corpus's second reader and the HV sub-plan's last code task — `GET /x/playbook` now carries **`timelineFunnel`** ($0, **no schema/migration/CSS/api.ts change**, 6 files, +9 tests, 1167→**1176**). Pure `buildTimelineFunnel(rows, repliedTweetIds, minN)` is playbook **§12** (the plan said §11 — AI.12 took that — D96a); `deriveTimelineBand` recomputes the band at read time through the shared `classifyBand` (§7.12, never stored) and returns `TimelineBand = Band | 'unknown'`, where **`'unknown'` (no `tweet_time`) is deliberately NOT the `null` band** (`null` = judged not worth replying to). First sighting wins twice over: deduped by earliest `capturedAtMs` in the pure fn AND by `min(captured_at)`+bare columns in `loadTimelineFunnel`'s `group by tweet_id`. `replied` = posted `reply_drafts.sourceTweetId` ∩ seen ids. 30d window, `mode='timeline'` filter, no row limit. Panel: a "Timeline funnel" section after Model effectiveness.
- **prior (HV.4):** the ambient corpus's first reader — `GET /x/harvest/affinity?days=&limit=&minDays=` (defaults 30/20/≥3, $0, **no schema/migration/CSS change**): one grouped select over `harvest_rows` filtered **`mode='timeline'`** + `capturedAt >= now−days`, `having count(distinct UTC day) >= minDays`, ordered **distinctDays desc then sightings desc** (a viral thread re-scrolled 20× in one afternoon is not affinity), then the `people`/`voice_authors` join **in TS** off the ≤`limit` handles. A **retired** person reads `stage: null` but `inRoster: true` — known, deliberately dropped, never re-nagged (D95(b)). New shared `intParam` (absent → default, out of range → clamped, garbage → **400**). Panel: a `Section`+`EmptyState` "Timeline affinity" drawer under the People roster, **collapsed and unfetched until clicked**, rows reusing the roster's own `setSelected` so an unknown handle rides the existing `NotYetKnown` create-file flow. 5 files, +5 tests (1162→**1167**).
- **prior (HV.3):** the Harvest tab's four options — a **Download CSV** checkbox (Start blocked with a warn line when it and Send-to-stratus are both off), a **Min views** floor applied at store time in `harvestPosts`/`harvestReplies` (after `noteSeen`, so filtered items still count as scrolled-past — else the day-window break dies), the whole form persisted in one `harvestForm` key through the lenient `parseHarvestForm`, and a **`Passive: N rows today`** line off `api.harvest.runs` + the new UTC-boundary `passiveRowsToday` (`Passive capture off` when the toggle is off, nothing at all while unloaded/failed). `HarvestOptions` gained `downloadCsv?`/`minViews?`, both absent-means-old-behaviour; a DB-only run reports `done.filename: ''`. New `shared/harvest.test.ts` (+15, 1147→**1162**), new `HarvestRun` type + `api.harvest.runs`, `HarvestPanel` now takes `settings`. **Drive-bys:** `useSettings`'s onChanged filter was missing `KEY_PASSIVE_HARVEST` (HV.2 gap) and `.harvest-tuning .field` needed `min-width: 0`. No server/schema/migration/route change. $0.
- **prior (HV.2):** the client half of ambient ingest — scrolling **x.com/home** streams every tweet whose metrics label parses into `POST /x/harvest/passive`. One line in `applyBand` (`if (sig) recordPassiveHarvest`), **every band including `skip`**; gate order enabled → `/home` (re-read per call) → `!isHarvestActive()` → `findPermalink` → pending/throttle → **only then** `extractArticle` (now exported from `harvester.ts` alongside `Extracted` + `isHarvestActive()`). `flushPassiveHarvest` = the `flushSightings` transport, warn-and-drop, **never retried**. NEW pure `extension/src/shared/passiveHarvest.ts` (+8 tests, 1139→**1147**) + the `passiveHarvest` Settings toggle (its own key, default ON). **No server/schema/migration/route change.** $0.
- **prior (HV.1):** `POST /x/harvest/passive` — ambient home-timeline rows into the SAME `harvest_rows` under `mode='timeline'`, hung off one **server-created** run per UTC day (`{handle:'timeline', mode:'timeline', scope:'passive'}`; `POST /harvest/runs` still 400s that mode, so clients can't forge one). Five ordered gates: 60d prune scoped to timeline runs → find-or-create today's run (exported pure `utcDayStart`) → in-batch dedupe + 30-min recapture (both → `skippedRecent`) → 2000/day cap (`skippedCap`) → one sync txn insert + `rowCount` bump. **Zero schema change, no migration** (D92). No band stored (§7.12), no person events (decision 6), unreachable from `xFetch` — **$0**. New `src/x/routes/harvest.test.ts` (9 tests; 1130→1139).

## Archived deviations (Waves 0–2, plus HV.1–HV.5 sha reconciliations)

Ordered as written. D-numbers are not contiguous here — the entries still live in STATE.md are listed in this file's header.

- **D12** (UI.8, historical): UI.8's `styles.css` and UI.1's whole settings platform were absorbed into ST.1's commit `61a04e7` (15 files, 3894 ins) by a concurrent lane's broad `git add` before either owner committed. Not reverted — `61a04e7` is shared HEAD and other lanes built on it; rewriting shared history mid-run is more dangerous than the mislabel. UI.8's `styles.css` content is correct there (0 literals outside `:root`, build green). **Process lesson: parallel masterplan lanes in ONE worktree must stage explicit paths (`git add <files>`), never `git add -A`/`commit -am`.**
- **D14** (UI.9): two things. **(a) Sha reconciliation** — the git history was rewritten (rebase/squash) after UI.1 wrote STATE, so `7e07dd5`/`40c718e`/`95b9fff` are gone; real Wave-0 shas corrected in the ledger (UI.1 `226368c`, UI.8 `1299e43`, ST.1 `61a04e7`). All ticked work verified present in the tree — status was accurate, only shas stale. **(b) Density plumbing divergence** — the plan assumed density = "override ~6 spacing vars", but UI.8 did NOT variableize spacing (`.panel` hardcoded `padding:14px;gap:12px`, `.row` `gap:8px`). UI.9 introduced three semantic vars `--ui-panel-pad`/`--ui-section-gap`/`--ui-row-gap` (cozy defaults in `:root`, compact overrides in `[data-density='compact']`) and wired `.panel`/`.row` to them; `--ui-root-size` (13px, overridden by `[data-scale='12'|'14']`) drives `body` font-size. **Binds UI.11–16 polish:** density currently only tightens `.panel`/`.row`; a polish task wanting finer compaction adds more consumers of the `--ui-*` vars (don't remap the generic `--strat-space-*` tokens — they're used everywhere).
- **D15** (ST.2, binds ST.3–ST.8): the Studio shell landed at **554 lines, not the plan's "< ~300"**. The plan's explicit split (registry.ts + fields.tsx + KitEditor.tsx) was done in full AND the AI-background section + library rail were also extracted into `fields.tsx` as `BackgroundFields`/`LibraryRail` (beyond the 4 named field components) — but the shell's irreducible logic (all state, 6 effects incl. the debounced render loop, and the async handlers generateBackground/saveToLibrary/reopenAsset/loadStatData/loadBannerData/stampVisualMade) can't be moved without a `useStudio` hook the plan didn't ask for. The **actual goal is met**: a new template = 1 `registry.ts` row + 1 field section + 1 `buildSpec` case, zero shell edits beyond those. ST.3/ST.5/ST.7/ST.8 register new templates through `TEMPLATES`/`buildSpec`; ST.3 also appends mascot layers in `templates.ts` (not the shell). **Do NOT add per-template render branches back into `Studio.tsx`** — go through the registry.

Corrected in the ledger, `last-commit`, codemap header + §11. - **D21** (ST.8): three things. **(a) Sha reconciliation** — ST.7's recorded `2104f46` was an amend-orphan (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 2104f46 HEAD` → false); real ST.7 commit is `acd95ec` (= HEAD at ST.8 start). Same sha-stamp trap as D16–D20. **(b) `heatmapCells(cells, minN=3)` takes minN** — the plan wrote `heatmapCells(cells)`, but `BestTimeCell` carries no `sufficient` field; the S0.4 gate is `BestTimesResponse.minN`, so the signature needs it. Default `HEATMAP_MIN_N=3` (the server also gates its `top` at 3). The shell passes `best.minN`. `heatmapCells` emits the FULL 7×24 grid (168 cells) so the template just places each — a cell below `minN` OR with no `avgViewsPerDay ?? avgViews` value is `sufficient:false`/`intensity:null` (renders the muted ink wash). Intensity = min→max over the sufficient set; a flat set → uniform 0.5 (guarded span, never all-invisible). **(c) Chart card is NOT bg-capable** (`supportsAiBackground:false`, like the stat card) — the plan was silent; an AI image behind a data chart would fight it, so no `patternKind`/`patternSeed` threading and no bg segmented control. `growthSeries` returns RAW follower points (the `sparkline` layer does its own min/max fit via `sparklineCoords`) — no normalization in chartData for growth. **Binds ST.9:** the chart card's two live-data fills (account series → growth, best-times → heatmap) + below-gate muting are the browser-walk items; both are $0 reads.

- **D76** (RL.1, binds RL.2/RL.3/RL.5): **engine contract additions beyond the plan text.** (a) **`parseHumanizerConfig` returns `HumanizerConfig | null`, not always-a-config** — null ONLY when the value isn't a plain object (brandKit's exact contract). The plan's Task-1 line says "lenient, field-by-field fallback to defaults", but Task 2's done-when requires `400 invalid_humanizer`, which is impossible if every input parses; a partially-bad object still falls back field-by-field. **RL.2:** `humanizer === null` in the body = clear-to-defaults, a non-null value that parses to null = 400. **RL.3:** use `resolveHumanizer(list.humanizer)` (exported, = `parse ?? DEFAULT_HUMANIZER`). (b) **`HUMANIZE_DRAWS`=10 rng draws are taken up front on EVERY `humanize` call, fired or not** (0 prefix chance · 1 prefix pick · 2 suffix chance · 3 suffix pick · 4 lowercase · 5 drop-period · 6 typo chance · 7 typo word · 8 typo kind · 9 typo position) — the plan asked for "independent rolls in fixed order so stubbed sequences are predictable"; a fixed count makes a stub array map to steps by position regardless of what fires. Don't make a step's draws conditional without updating the tests + the exported constant. (c) **Extra exports the plan didn't list** (all used by later RL tasks): `MAX_REPLY_LENGTH` (280 — RL.2 item validation should reuse it), `TEMPLATE_VARS`/`TemplateVar`, `templateVars(text)` (which vars an item needs — RL.5 can show them per item), `availableVarsFor(vars)` (build the `pickItem` arg from the target), `stripEmoji`, `resolveHumanizer`. (d) **`humanize` tracks a `bodyStart` offset** so an applied prefix doesn't shield the item's first letter from the (independent) lowercase roll. (e) **`pickItem` is generic** `<T extends PickableItem>` — the route gets its own row type back; it takes `ReadonlySet<string>`, not a `ReplyVars`. (f) **Deprioritization outranks recency**: when only var-free items are usable, the pool collapses to them and one may repeat — never blocking the use (Decision 7) — locked by a test. (g) **RL.2's migration is NOT `0013`** (the plan's number is long taken by UI.1/AI): run `bun run db:generate` against the current journal and inspect the SQL (codemap §4 seed-drop trap).

Additionally verified on a *copy* of the real `stratus.db` (36 tables after, `content_pillars` seed still 3 rows, FK cascade live under `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`) — the plan's "lands on a fresh boot without touching existing tables" done-when, proven on real data rather than only the in-memory suite.
- **D78** (RL.2, binds RL.3/RL.4/RL.5): **route-contract decisions the plan left open.** (a) **`POST /:id/items` returns the list's WHOLE current item set**, not just the written rows — the plan's `→ {items}` is ambiguous and one shape for both modes is what the RL.5 editor wants. (b) **Item `createdAt` is stamped explicitly** (`base = max(now, list's max createdAt + 1)`, then `+i` per row) inside the txn: a batch insert shares one `unixepoch()` default millisecond, and an append in the same ms as the previous batch tied with it and sorted into the middle (caught by a test). Don't revert to the column default. (c) **Items must be `{text}` objects** — a bare string 400s `invalid_items`, matching the plan's contract and RL.4's generator shape. (d) **An empty `items: []` is legal**: with `mode:'replace'` it clears the list (the panel's "start over"), with `append` it's a no-op. (e) **`source` is per-CALL, not per-item** (`'manual'`|`'ai'`, default manual) — RL.4's "Overwrite list" sends `source:'ai'` once. (f) Item PATCH/DELETE match `id` AND `listId` (wrong-list → 404); malformed uuid → 400 `invalid_id`, absent → 404. (g) `MAX_ITEMS_PER_CALL=100` is per call, **not a per-list ceiling** — repeated appends can exceed it by design. (h) No `?active=` filter on the collection GET (the plan's contract has none; RL.5 filters client-side).
- **D79** (RL.3, binds RL.4/RL.5/RL.6/RL.7): **the `/use` contract, and one correctness fix the plan didn't call for.** (a) **`lastUsedAt` is stamped MONOTONICALLY past the list's current max**, not from `Date.now()` — the plan says only "stamps last_used_at". The anti-repeat window (`pickItem` excludes the `min(n-1, floor(n/2))` most-recently-used) only guarantees no immediate repeat while the just-used item is *strictly* the most recent; uses land ~0.5ms apart, so `Date.now()` ties them and the recency sort can exclude the wrong item. **Measured, not theorized:** a throwaway 500-use stress showed **241/500 uses sharing a millisecond and 4 immediate repeats** without the stamp, and 0/0 with it — the plan's own 10-use done-when test would have been ~8%-flaky. Same trap RL.2 hit on item `createdAt` (D78b); now codemap **§7.13a**. Any future column an ordering rule reads needs this. (b) **`composeReply` has NO `protectedValues` argument** — the plan's "`composeReply` with … `protectedValues = [vars.name, vars.handle]`" is stale; RL.1's signature is `(itemText, vars, config, rng)` and it derives name/first_name/handle protection internally. (c) **The route passes ALL items to `pickItem`, not just enabled ones** — the engine owns the enabled + var-availability rules, so the filtering lives in exactly one place. (d) **`updatedAt` is NOT bumped on use** (it means "the text was edited" — RL.5's item editor can show it truthfully); only `lastUsedAt`/`useCount` move. (e) **An `active:false` list is still usable.** `active` is presentation state (D78h: RL.5 filters client-side) and the contract lists only 404/409, so no new error code was invented — RL.5/RL.6 must not offer inactive lists in the picker if that's the intent. (f) **A body-less POST is a legal "use with no target"** (`c.req.json().catch(() => undefined)` → `{}`); only a body that parses to a non-object 400s `invalid_body`. Malformed JSON therefore degrades to an un-personalized use rather than a 400 — accepted because the text is copied to the clipboard and read by a human before pasting (§7.28). (g) **`targetHandle` is normalized the repo way** (`@`-stripped, lowercased, `USERNAME_RE` = `/^[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,15}$/`) and **400s `invalid_target_handle`** when malformed — it's audit metadata that later joins people/voice rows, so storing garbage is worse than refusing. `targetTweetId` uses the plan's `/^\d{1,32}$/` (note: laxer than launch.ts's `TWEET_ID_RE` = `{1,25}`). `vars.name`/`vars.handle` are only length-bounded (≤120) — never USERNAME_RE'd, because they are rendered as display text. (h) **Error slugs:** `invalid_id` / `invalid_body` / `invalid_vars` / `invalid_target_tweet_id` / `invalid_target_handle` / `invalid_preview` / `not_found` (404) / `no_enabled_items` (409). (i) **`reply_list_uses` never cascades** (FK-free by design) — the route's test `afterAll` deletes those rows explicitly, and **RL.7's playbook suite must expect stray rows if any future suite forgets**.

The per-task history lives in the D-entries and the codemap §11 log — do not re-grow these three lines. The sha trap in one sentence: a commit cannot contain its own sha, so the stamp is always pre-amend and **HEAD's subject line is the authoritative identity**.
- **D80** (RL.4, binds RL.5/RL.6/RL.8): **the plan's Task-4 text predates the AI layer; three instructions were overridden.** (a) **`askGrok` + `promptCacheKey:'stratus-reply-list-gen'` → `askLLM` + `loadPromptSafe('reply-list')`** (D5): the generator prompt is now an editable registry key, so `PROMPT_KEYS` went **10 → 11** (appended after `icebreaker`; required placeholders `{{COUNT}}`/`{{REQUEST}}`/`{{EXISTING_ITEMS}}`, no optional ones) and the cache key is the registry sha — **bare, no niche suffix**, because the prompt carries no persona token (the `ideas`/`rewrite` precedent). `registry.test.ts`'s key list and `docs/settings-tab.md`'s "10 prompts" copy were updated in the same commit; the extension's Prompts panel is data-driven (`api.prompts.list`) and needed no change. (b) **No pre-flight `XAI_API_KEY` check → 503 `grok_not_configured`.** Following ideas.ts (AI.9, the newest new-surface precedent), the route has NO pre-flight gate: `askLLM` enforces the resolved provider's key and `llmErrorPayload` maps it to **503 `llm_not_configured`**. Two reasons this is the right call and not laziness: an OpenRouter-only setup must be able to generate, and a pre-flight check would shadow the **404 for an unknown list** (which now fires before any spend regardless of keys). **RL.5's error matcher must expect `llm_not_configured`, not `grok_not_configured`.** (c) **`count` 400s past 30 instead of clamping** (ideas.ts clamps) — the plan's own test asks for a 400 on 31, and silently returning 12 of a requested 40 reads as a broken generator. Also: `parseGeneratedItems` dedupes on a **lowercased + whitespace-collapsed** key rather than the plan's "exact texts" (casing-only twins defeat the anti-repeat pick they would feed), and it distinguishes **`null` = parse error** from **`[]` = nothing survived** — the route 502s `items_parse_error` vs `items_invalid`. Response is the plan's contract plus `count`/`requested` (ideas.ts shape). **Nothing is persisted** — RL.5's "Overwrite/Append" goes through the existing `POST /items` with `source:'ai'` (D78e).

- **D81** (RL.5, binds RL.6/RL.8): **four divergences from the plan's Task-5 text, three of them substantive.** (a) **The subtab uses the `SubTabs` UI.10 primitive, not a copied `radar-tabs` block.** The plan says "copy `Voice.tsx:19`'s `'tweets'|'pillars'` switch + its subtab header markup" — but `SubTabs` IS the formalized version of exactly that control (UI.10 shipped it saying "Voice migrates onto this in UI.14"), and **D7 binds every NEW Wave-1+ surface to the primitives**. Using it cost one import and zero CSS; copying `radar-tabs` would have added a third hand-rolled copy for UI.14 to unpick. The AI.10 Settings shell's `radar-tabs` is the older idiom — don't read it as the standard for new surfaces. `Replies.tsx`'s `<h2>` also changed "Reply Master" → "Replies" (the subtab chip now carries that name). (b) **The humanizer editor keeps NO client-side copy of `DEFAULT_HUMANIZER`.** The plan's "pools + chance sliders, Reset to defaults" implies the panel knows the defaults to seed the form; duplicating them in TS is exactly the drift the repo avoids elsewhere (and the extension MUST NOT import `src/x/replyLists/engine.ts` — §5 build isolation, and it isn't a `src/shared/` shim). Instead **"Customize for this list" PATCHes `{humanizer:{}}`**: `parseHumanizerConfig({})` is non-null and lenient-parses every absent field to its default, so the response row IS the full engine config and the form renders from server truth. "Reset to defaults" PATCHes `null` (back to the "using engine defaults" card). **Verified against the real route** with a throwaway `bun:test` (create → `humanizer:null`; PATCH `{}` → deep-equals `DEFAULT_HUMANIZER`; PATCH `null` → null; plus an items append + a `preview:true` `/use` asserting `{name}` got filled) — deleted after the run, not committed. (c) **Pools are one-per-line textareas, NOT the plan's "comma-separated inputs"** — a planning error: `DEFAULT_HUMANIZER.prefixes` is `['honestly,','man,','ngl','tbh','yeah,','ok so']`, so a comma split would shred half the defaults into fragments the first time anyone saved. The same one-per-line convention is used for the items add box (bulk paste → one item per line, which is also how a swipe file arrives). (d) **Per-item template-var chips were dropped.** D76c offered `templateVars(text)` "so RL.5 can show which vars an item needs", but reaching it needs either a `src/shared/` move of the engine (out of scope, touches RL.1–4) or a duplicated regex; the raw template text already shows `{name}` literally, and Test render's server-returned `missingVars` is the authoritative answer. Revisit only if a real user asks.

- **D82** (RL.6, binds RL.8): **three divergences from the plan's Task-6 text.** (a) **The `onUsed?` callback prop was dropped.** The plan lists `{vars, targetTweetId, targetHandle, onUsed?}`, but the same task's How forbids the only thing a caller could do with it ("don't add new status flips; the user still clicks Done after pasting"), so neither call site had a use — an optional callback nobody passes is dead weight. Re-add it the day a caller genuinely needs to react to a use. (b) **The popover keeps the composed text visible instead of only flashing "Copied ✓".** A `writeText` rejection after the round-trip is the one failure mode that *loses* work — the item is already spent server-side (`lastUsedAt` stamped, `reply_list_uses` row written), so a swallowed clipboard error would burn an item and hand back nothing. The result view therefore renders the text (selectable) with "Copied ✓ — paste it in X" or "Copy this by hand:", plus a `missingVars` hint line and a "Pick another" button. Same instinct as Studio's `copyPng` catch. (c) **Two files beyond the plan's three:** `ReplyLists.tsx` gained a 1-line unmount cleanup (`useEffect(() => invalidateReplyListsCache, [])` — that panel is the ONLY place lists are mutated, so one line beats six invalidate calls sprinkled through its child components; the ChannelTags precedent invalidates from the editing tab too), and `styles.css` gained the `.quick-reply*` block. **No UI.10 primitive fits** — there is no Menu/Popover primitive (GearPopover is settings-specific), so the picker re-uses its outside-`mousedown` close *pattern* with its own `--strat-*`-only CSS; D7 is honored on tokens, and a future UI.x can extract a Popover primitive from these two consumers.
- **D83** (RL.7, binds RL.8): **three divergences from the plan's Task-7 text, plus one drive-by.** (a) **`cannedTexts` is a REQUIRED 4th param, not optional.** The plan says "gains a `cannedTexts: Set<string>` param"; defaulting it to an empty set would have spared 5 test call sites and `scripts/smoke-radar-reply-flow.ts` — and let any future caller silently misattribute every canned reply as unattributed. Explicit beats convenient here (the same reasoning RU.9 used for the source map). (b) **The route test asserts a DELTA, not `canned.n === 1`.** The plan's test spec says "seeded use row + posts_published reply → `canned.n === 1`", but D79i is explicit that `reply_list_uses` is FK-free and a suite that forgets its cleanup leaves strays in the shared `:memory:` DB. The describe therefore snapshots `batchVsSingle` AFTER seeding the published reply but BEFORE inserting the use row, then asserts canned +1 / unattributed −1 (the median is pinned only when `before.canned === 0`). That also proves the *direction* — the use row is what moves the reply out of unattributed. (c) **A behavior-neutral refactor inside `classifyReplyOrigin`**: `normalizeReplyText(reply.text)` is now computed once at the top (both the radar branch and the canned check need it) instead of inside the radar `if`. Note the knock-on in the pure suite: the existing "wrong radar target" case (`tweetId 904`, text `'Ship it. Then fix it.'`) **now returns `'canned'` instead of `null`** because that same text is in the test's canned set — deliberate, and the clearest available demonstration that canned is a genuine fallback tier rather than dead code. (d) **Drive-by:** `src/x/playbook.ts` contained two literal NUL bytes (the `${r.pillar ?? '\0'}` key sentinel in `buildPillarRegisterScorecard`), written as raw bytes rather than escapes. `file` reported the file as `data`, so **every plain `grep` over it returned nothing** — I lost a round-trip concluding `classifyReplyOrigin` didn't exist before trying `grep -a`. Replaced with `'\0'` escapes (byte-different, runtime-identical); codemap §7.32 records the audit one-liner. `src/x/playbook.ts` was the only such file in the repo.
- **D84** (RL.8, closes the RL sub-plan): **four divergences from the plan's Task-8 text, none structural.** (a) **The phase shipped as "Surfaces S7", not the plan title's "(S5)"** — S5 is Studio 2.0 and S6 the Augmented X UI, both already in CLAUDE.md/SURFACES-PLAN; AX.7 hit the identical collision and resolved it the same way (a parenthetical noting the plan text is stale). SURFACES-PLAN gets §5d, matching §5b/§5c. (b) **The smoke covers the Playbook `canned` bucket, which the plan's Task-8 spec doesn't mention** — RL.7 landed after the plan was written and the RL.7 next-up explicitly asked for it. It asserts a **delta** (`canned.posted`/`canned.n` +1, `unattributed` unmoved) around its own seed, for D83b's reason: `reply_list_uses` is FK-free, so an absolute is never safe. It seeds **both** sides (a `posts_published` row whose text IS a use's `renderedText`, plus a `metrics_snapshots` row) rather than calling `/use` and waiting on the 03:00 discovery. (c) **Two guards beyond the plan's walk, both $0:** the empty-list `409 no_enabled_items`, and a `/generate` refusal check that **branches on `llmConfigured()`** — no key → assert `503 llm_not_configured` (D80b's error code, not the plan's `grok_not_configured`); key present → assert the unknown-list **404 fires before any spend** (so the script stays $0 on a configured machine, which is the only way it was actually run here). The plan's "vars rendered" assertion is done **per item template** via an `itemId → template` map, so it holds whichever item the shuffle picks — a blanket "the text contains Ana Pop" would fail the moment the shuffle picks the var-less item. (d) **Docs went wider than the plan's one-liner:** `docs/replies-tab.md` gained a full "The Lists subtab" section (templates + missing-var degradation, the anti-repeat shuffle, the humanizer table with its two guarantees, proposal-only generate, the `canned ▾` surfaces, and the two honest measurement caveats) plus a Launch-Room canned workflow; `docs/today-tab.md` got three touches, not two (both picker surfaces + the "which clicks cost money" tip, where canned belongs on the $0 side). **Drive-by:** three stale "two variants/two replies" lines in `docs/replies-tab.md` corrected to three (RU.1 shipped 3 variants; the body sections were already right, only the intro and the tip lagged).

- **D85** (NT.1): **the icon `d` prefixes could not be captured from the live DOM.** The plan's How says "capture the real `d` strings into the fixtures while building them" — that assumes a browser session on x.com/notifications, which this environment doesn't have. Three consequences, all deliberate: (a) `LIKE_ICON_PREFIXES` (2 entries — outline + filled heart), `REPOST_ICON_PREFIXES`, `FOLLOW_ICON_PREFIXES` hold **best-effort recollections of X's glyph paths**, exported and comment-flagged as unverified; (b) `detectKindFromIcon` returns **`null` on an unrecognised glyph** (falls through to keywords) rather than deciding `'other'`, so wrong prefixes cost accuracy-on-locale, never correctness; (c) the test fixtures **interpolate the exported constants** into their `<path d>` instead of hardcoding paths, so correcting a prefix after the live walk needs no test edit. **NT.5's browser walk MUST verify/correct these three constants** — that is the task where a real notifications page is open anyway.
- **D85a** (NT.1): **three small divergences from the plan's How, all additive.** (1) `parseNotificationCell` returns `null` when the element's `data-testid !== 'notification'` — the plan declares the `| null` return but never says when it fires; giving it that meaning lets NT.5 pass articles blindly. (2) Keyword matching runs against the **first `[dir]` block** (the header line) and only falls back to the whole cell text — the plan says "header text keywords" without saying how the header is found; whole-text-only would let a quoted post containing "liked" vote. (3) `targetText` is **forced null for `kind === 'follow'`** rather than left to the verb filter — X sometimes renders the new follower's bio in a follow cell, and NT.2's `resolveTargetTweetId` would then try to prefix-match a bio against own `posts_published`. **Binds NT.2:** it may assume `targetText === null` for every follow engagement it receives.
- **D85b** (NT.1): **`RESERVED_HANDLES` was deliberately NOT reused.** The plan's Read-first points at `content.ts:79-100` for it, but handles here come from `UserAvatar-Container-*` testid suffixes (never a URL path segment), so route names can't appear. The one real placeholder X emits is `UserAvatar-Container-unknown`, which is filtered by name; everything else is regex-validated `/^[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,15}$/`. Importing the content.ts set would have broken the §7.26 dependency-free rule for no gain.

No out-of-skill work landed; ledger no drift. The orphaned sha in the codemap header + §11 NT.1 line was corrected there too. Same one-command check as always: `git log -1 --format='%h %s'`.
- **D86** (NT.2): **four divergences from the plan's Task-2 text, all additive or forced.** (a) **`resolveTargetTweetId` was split into a pure matcher + a DB wrapper.** The plan names one export `resolveTargetTweetId(targetText)` *and* separately demands "cache the post list once per batch" — those cannot both be one function. `matchTargetTweetId(targetText, posts)` is the pure prefix match (unit-testable with zero DB), `loadRecentOwnPosts()` is module-private, and `resolveTargetTweetId(targetText)` remains as the single-shot wrapper the plan names (exercised by the test, so it is not dead code). `recordEngagements` loads the window **lazily, at most once per batch** — a follow-only batch issues zero target SQL. (b) **`recomputePerson` runs once per distinct handle (at that handle's max seenAt), not once per row** as the plan's "per row:" list reads. A handle can legitimately carry several engagements in one batch (three likes), and `recomputePerson` reloads the entire event history on every call; once-per-handle is provably identical (stage goes through `maxStage` and these types are in no stage set; the watermarks are min/max over all events) and strictly cheaper. (c) **`engagementSummary` is exported** (the plan implies an inline expression) so the timeline wording is pinned by a test — and an unresolved like reads `liked a post` rather than a quoted empty snippet. (d) **The `people.source` schema comment was corrected beyond adding `notification`** — it listed `mention | voice | reply | harvest | manual` and had never been updated for C6 `hover` or C7 `launch`, both of which have been written for months. **Not a divergence but worth stating:** the extension's `PersonEventType` union and the **exhaustive** `EVENT_ICONS: Record<PersonEvent['type'], string>` in `People.tsx` were left alone — NT.6's edit list owns them, and because the Record is exhaustive TypeScript will *force* the three labels the moment the union grows (no risk of a silent gap). NT.2 is server-only; nothing imports `engagements.ts` until NT.3.

No out-of-skill work landed; ledger no drift. The orphaned sha in the codemap header + §11 NT.2 line was corrected there too. Same one-command check as always: `git log -1 --format='%h %s'`.
- **D87** (NT.3, binds NT.5/NT.7): **three divergences from the plan's Task-3 text, all small.** (a) **Tests were colocated in `src/x/people/engagements.test.ts`, not `src/x/routes/people.test.ts`** — the plan's Edit list names the latter, but its own Read-first says "find where sightings route tests live and colocate", and `POST /x/people/sightings` is tested in `people/sightings.test.ts`. Following the precedent also keeps the C10 ingest (module + route) in one readable file. `routes/people.test.ts` was not touched. (b) **A third file:** the `isEngagementKind` guard + `ENGAGEMENT_KINDS` tuple were added to `people/engagements.ts` beside the union rather than inlined in the route, matching `isStage` (stage.ts) and `isFollowupKind` (followups.ts) — the guard belongs with the type it narrows. The plan's "the route's kind whitelist is the enforcement point" is unchanged: the route is still the only caller. (c) **The route re-forces `targetText: null` when `kind === 'follow'`**, which the plan doesn't ask for. NT.1 already guarantees it (D85a), but the guarantee lives in a *client* the server can't version-pin, and a follow cell's prose is the new follower's **bio** — feeding that to `matchTargetTweetId` is exactly the wrong-attribution the day-bucket fallback exists to avoid. This is §7.3's "recompute the gate server-side so a stale extension build can't spend/corrupt" applied to a $0 path. It is observable (two follow cells with different `targetText` collapse to `processed: 1` instead of 2) and pinned by a test. **For NT.5:** the wire contract is `{engagements: [{kind: 'like'|'repost'|'follow', handle, targetText?: string|null, seenAt: ISO}]}`, ≤50/batch; drop `'other'` cells and handle-less cells client-side (they'd 400 the whole batch on kind, or waste a slot); a bad handle inside an otherwise-valid batch is safe — it comes back in `skipped`.
- **D88** (NT.4, binds NT.5): **four divergences from the plan's Task-4 text, all additive.** (a) **Three result types were exported beyond the plan's "message + guard"** — `NotifContextEntry`, `NotifContextMap`, `NotifContextResponse` in `shared/messages.ts`. The repo convention is to document a message's response in a comment (LaunchGet, RadarVariantsGet), but here the map shape is consumed by *both* ends (the background builds it, NT.5's DOM code reads it per cell), so a shared type beats a structural cast written twice; `MentionStatus` is reused from `shared/types.ts` rather than the plan's loose `status: string`, which is what makes NT.5's `status === 'answered'` chip check type-safe. (b) **`force` drains any in-flight refresh BEFORE invalidating**, where the plan says only "if `force` → drop cache". Without the drain, a scan-triggered pull that started *before* the sync click can resolve after it, stamp `notifContextAt = Date.now()` and pin the pre-sync rows for the full 5-minute TTL — exactly what the (already spent, capped) `POST /x/mentions/refresh` was clicked to avoid. Costs at most one extra $0 GET, and only on a deliberate human click. (c) **A fetch failure keeps the last-good map instead of the plan's literal `{ok:true, mentions:{}, rankMap}`** — that empty-map response is now only the thrown-handler path. The stamp advances *only* on success, so a failed pull retries on the next ask rather than caching emptiness for 5 min; before the first successful pull the map IS `{}`, so the plan's degradation contract holds either way. (d) **Two guard tests were added** though the plan said "none required beyond typecheck" — `extension/src/shared/messages.test.ts` didn't exist when the plan was written (RU.6 created it) and now every message guard is tested there; the cache logic itself stays untested (no background harness), as the plan intends.

No out-of-skill work landed; ledger no drift. The orphaned sha in the codemap header + §11 NT.4 line was corrected there too. Same one-command check as always: `git log -1 --format='%h %s'`.
- **D89** (NT.5, binds NT.6/NT.7): **five divergences from the plan's Task-5 text, plus the D85 verification finally done.** (a) **The plan's (b) said tier chips go on "both tweet-articles and notification cells"; they go on notification cells ONLY.** The plan predates AX.3 (written 2026-07-16, AX.3 shipped 2026-07-21), which already decorates every `article[data-testid="tweet"]` — including the ones on this page — with the strictly richer glance chips (stage + `◎` target + `↩` owed + `Nd` neglect). Following the plan literally would have double-decorated the same line with a weaker vocabulary. Aggregated cells have no `User-Name` row and up to ~8 handles, so they keep the compact `personTierFor` chip the plan designed, which is also what keeps NT.4's `rankMap` half of the payload from being dead weight. (b) **The parent-context line renders on `status === 'answered'` alone, even when `parentText` is null.** The plan gates the whole line on `parentText`. A standalone @-mention that the inbox already settled is precisely the "don't reply to this twice" case the surface exists for, and it costs one chip. (c) **The context throttle stamps on settle, not on success.** The plan says only "60s throttle". Stamping on success alone would let a failing ask re-fire on every animation frame; the background already owns success-vs-failure caching (D88c keeps the last-good map), so the client throttle only governs *how often we ask*. (d) **`force` deliberately bypasses the in-flight guard.** `syncNotifContext()` returns early when a bare ask is in flight, but the sync chip calls `fetchNotifContext(true)` directly — routing it through the guard would silently drop the forced refetch the (already spent, capped) pull was clicked for, which is the exact failure D88b hardened the background against. (e) **`'other'` and empty-handle cells are left OUT of the `engagementHandled` WeakSet** rather than marked processed. The plan says "WeakSet per cell"; NT.1's gotcha says a skeleton cell can legitimately parse to `handles: []`. Marking those would burn the cell permanently on a render X hadn't finished — so only a cell that actually produced engagements is marked, exactly as `capturePassiveHoverCards` treats skeleton hover cards. **The D85 icon prefixes are now VERIFIED against live x.com/notifications** (this was the task that was supposed to do it): like `M20.884 13.19` confirmed on **191** cells (it is the *filled* heart; the second guess `M16.697 5.5` turned out to be the real *outline* heart X uses on the action-row like button, so both stay), repost `M4.5 3.88` confirmed by asset identity with the live `[data-testid="retweet"]` icon (**no repost notification was in the window — this one is verified by shared asset, not by a live repost cell**, and the like pair is what proves X reuses these assets), and follow **CORRECTED**: the guessed `M12 11.816` was wrong, the real glyph is `M17.863 13.44` (14 live cells, single + aggregated). Because NT.1's fixtures interpolate the exported constants (D85's design), the correction needed **no test edit**. X's bell glyph `M11.996 2c` is deliberately left unmapped so "New post notifications for … and 6 others" cells fall to `'other'` and get dropped — verified, and note those cells DO carry avatars, so a mis-mapping there would silently harvest bogus engagements. **Also live-validated, so NT.6/NT.7 can trust it:** `findPermalink` on a notification tweet-article yields the REPLY's own tweet id (the `NotifContextMap` key — the whole (a) path depends on it); a 21-cell page classified 19 like / 1 follow / 1 other; every follow's `targetText` was null (D85a holds on live DOM); and the load-bearing placement claim — chips injected **after** the header `[dir]` block changed **0 of 21** re-parses, so the augmentation provably cannot feed its own text back into the harvest. The DOM anchors themselves (`[data-testid="primaryColumn"] h2` for the chip, `tweetText`-afterend for the line, header-`[dir]`-afterend for the chips) were all exercised on the live page.

No out-of-skill work landed; ledger no drift. The orphaned sha in the codemap header + §11 NT.5 line was corrected there too. Same one-command check as always: `git log -1 --format='%h %s'`.
- **D90** (NT.6, binds NT.7): **three divergences from the plan's Task-6 text, all small.** (a) **The engagement query is scoped to the ranked page, not to every handle in the window.** The plan says only "a separate query keyed by handle"; running it unbounded would aggregate every engager the notifications harvest ever produced (dozens/day by design — decision 2) to then throw all but ≤`limit` rows away. `loadFanEngagementCounts(handles, cutoff)` takes the post-`rankFans`, post-`slice` handle list, and **returns an empty Map on an empty list** so drizzle can never emit `in ()`. This is only correct *because* the count is display-only — if engagement ever became a ranking input the scoping would have to move before the ranking, and at that point decision 1 has been broken anyway. (b) **The types come from `Object.values(ENGAGEMENT_EVENT_TYPE)`** (NT.2's map) rather than a new `ENGAGEMENT_TYPES` tuple in `stage.ts`. stage.ts carries the load-bearing comment "their_like/their_repost/their_follow deliberately appear in no set below" — adding a set there, even a display-only one, is exactly the ambiguity that comment exists to prevent. Derived-not-hardcoded also means the three strings live in one place. (c) **The plan's dossier-label bullet was a no-op and stays one.** `People.tsx` renders `{e.summary ?? e.type}` and NT.2's `recordEngagements` already stores `engagementSummary(kind, targetText)` (`liked: "…"` / `liked a post` / `reposted…` / `followed you`), so the timeline reads correctly with zero label mapping. What the union widening *did* force is `EVENT_ICONS` — the exhaustive `Record<PersonEvent['type'], string>` NT.2 deliberately left alone (see D86's closing note); the icons chosen are ♥ / ⟳ / ✚. **`rankFans` was not touched at all** — decision 1 is enforced structurally (the count is attached in the response `.map`, after `slice`), and the route test re-ranks the returned page through the real `rankFans` with the counts stripped to prove the order is inbound-only.

- **D91** (NT.7): **three additions beyond the plan's Task-7 checklist, all additive; nothing in the checklist was skipped.** (a) **`docs/README.md` gained an "On x.com itself" table.** The plan lists only the new `docs/notifications-surface.md` + one `today-tab.md` line, but the docs index has a tab table and an S1–S4 surfaces table and no home for a page-level surface — so `notifications-surface.md` would have shipped unreachable, exactly as **`s6-augmented-ui.md` already had** (AX.7 wrote it and never linked it). The new table links both; the S6 link is the drive-by fix. (b) **The smoke asserts four things the plan's spec doesn't name**, each earning its line: the seeded own post is written **`retired: true`** (invariant #7 — a non-retired synthetic own post left behind by an aborted run would be picked up and *billed* by the next 03:00 pass; this is the one real hazard of a real-DB smoke that seeds `posts_published`); a **fill-only** proof (the fan is pre-seeded enriched with `source='mention'` + a displayName, so the harvest has something it could wrongly clobber — and it is also why the plan's "assert `source='notification'`" is asserted on the *second*, harvest-created person); **the likes-only person never ranks** in `/x/people/fans` (a structural proof of decision 1 that costs one line and can't rot, unlike a re-ranking assertion which the route test already owns); and `kind:'other'` → **400** (the route whitelist is where the parser's client-side drop is re-enforced — D87c). (c) **A §7 codemap bullet** was added for the checkpoint trap (the plan asked for the note in §7; §4's `mentions` row already carried the two-precedent version, so the §7 bullet is the discoverable-from-the-patterns-list half). **No code, schema, route, test or extension file was touched** — `bun run test` is 1130/0, byte-identical to NT.6.
- **D92** (HV.1): **the plan's "no migration" beat STATE's "migration task" hint — and it was right.** STATE's next-up for HV.1 said to run `db:generate` and watch the dropped-seed trap; the source plan's Design block and decision 1 both say **zero schema change**. Verified against the code, not the prose: `harvest_runs.mode`/`.scope` and `harvest_rows.mode` are plain `text().notNull()` (the `MODES`/`SCOPES` whitelists are *route* validation, not column constraints), every passive field already exists (orig\*/`group_position`/`matched_draft_id` simply stay null), and `harvest_rows_tweet_captured_idx` **already existed** to serve the 30-min recapture query. **No migration was generated; the journal is still at `0018` and stays FREE for the next task that needs it.** Standing lesson: a next-up hint is a hint — the source plan's decision block plus the actual schema outrank it.
- **D92a** (HV.1, implementation choices the plan text doesn't pin down): (a) **in-batch duplicates are counted as `skippedRecent`.** The plan's test list names "batch-internal dupe" separately but the response shape it specifies has only `inserted`/`skippedRecent`/`skippedCap` — "already captured recently" is the honest bucket for "twice in the same 5s flush", so both fold there. (b) **The cap is computed OUTSIDE the txn** (from the run row read a few statements earlier), mirroring `/harvest/rows` — two tabs flushing concurrently can therefore overshoot 2000 by up to one batch, and the same race can double-write one longitudinal point. Deliberate and commented: the cap is a **volume guard, not an invariant** (no money is on this path — §8 can't be reached from here), and moving it inside the txn would buy nothing a single-user process needs. (c) `utcDayStart` is **exported and unit-tested** (the plan said "if not trivial inline") — it is the one piece of date math a wrong answer would silently fork a second run per day over. (d) Two extra in-file constants beyond the plan's five (`PASSIVE_SCOPE`, `PASSIVE_HANDLE`) so the synthetic run's identity is stated once. (e) `findOrCreatePassiveRun` **throws** `passive_run_insert_failed` on the impossible empty `.returning()` — `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` forces a branch and a throw is truthful where a cast would lie. (f) One test beyond the plan's list — **"accepts a re-capture once the window has passed"** (ages a stored row 31 min, re-posts, asserts a 2-point series): the plan's *phase* done-when #2 requires exactly this and the gate is only half-proven by the skip case.
- **D94a** (HV.3 bookkeeping): HV.2's recorded `090a8ac` was an amend-orphan; real HV.2 = **`661d039`** (= HEAD at HV.3 start, same `(HV.2)` subject `feat(extension): passive home-timeline harvest capture with settings opt-out`, parent `e49f705` = the out-of-skill docs refactor D93a already covers). **No out-of-skill work landed since; ledger no drift.** The orphaned sha in the codemap header + §11's HV.2 line stays as written (it names the same commit by subject) — only the ledger and `last-commit` were corrected. Same one-command check as always: `git log -1 --format='%h %s'`.
- **D94** (HV.3): **four divergences from the plan's Task-3 text, all additive; nothing in its checklist was skipped.** **(a) The task grew from 4 files to 9, because the status line needs the typed api client and the panel had no `Settings`.** The plan says "prefer the existing `api.ts` request path", but every `api.*` method takes `Settings` and `HarvestPanel` was the one tab component App.tsx rendered **prop-less**. So: `HarvestRun` added to `shared/types.ts` (server-response types live there — the `SettingEntry` precedent — not in `shared/harvest.ts`), `api.harvest.runs(s,{limit?})` added next to `api.voice`, `HarvestPanel({settings})` + the one-line App.tsx call site. The alternative (a bare `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` ApiRequest, as `harvester.ts::apiSend` does) would have kept the file count but put a second untyped client in a panel; not worth it. `harvestClient.ts` needed **no** edit at all (the plan's "only if" clause) — it forwards `options` verbatim over the port. **(b) Two drive-by fixes outside the edit list.** `useSettings`'s `chrome.storage.onChanged` filter in `sidepanel/storage.ts` was **missing `KEY_PASSIVE_HARVEST`** — HV.2 added the key to the `Settings` interface, `getSettings`, `saveSettings` and `patchSettings` but not to the listener's early-return, so toggling it in Settings never re-fired `getSettings` and the App-level `settings` object stayed stale until a reload. HV.3's status line is the first consumer that would have shown the bug, so it is fixed here (one line, D11-style universal correctness). And `.harvest-tuning .field` got `min-width: 0` — the tuning row went from two columns to three and flex children won't shrink below an `<input type=number>`'s intrinsic width without it. **(c) Three pure exports, not the plan's "if extracted" one.** The plan offered `passesMinViews` as a minimal option and treated the form parse as optional; both were extracted into `shared/harvest.ts` with its first-ever test suite, and `passiveRowsToday` was added beside them — the UTC-day boundary must match the server's `utcDayStart`, and a local-midnight window would silently read yesterday's run for part of every day, which is exactly the kind of wrong that never announces itself. 15 tests. **(d) The numeric form fields persist as raw digit STRINGS (`maxStr`/`minViewsStr`), not numbers.** That is the shape the panel actually holds; storing numbers would make a blank Min-views box round-trip as `0`, which `passesMinViews` treats as "no floor" today but would be a live landmine the moment anyone reads the stored form as a number. `parseHarvestForm` therefore keeps only `/^\d*$/` strings and blanks anything else.
- **D95a** (HV.4 bookkeeping): HV.3's recorded `4c2adcf` was an amend-orphan; real HV.3 = **`5cd316a`** (= HEAD at HV.4 start, same `(HV.3)` subject `feat(extension): harvest tab options — csv toggle, min-views, persisted form, passive status`, parent `661d039` = HV.2). **No out-of-skill work landed since; ledger no drift.** Corrected in the ledger, `last-commit`, and BOTH codemap places (header + §11's HV.3 line — unlike D94a, which left them; naming the same commit by a dead sha is still a sha a future session has to re-derive). Same one-command check as always: `git log -1 --format='%h %s'`.
- **D95** (HV.4): **four calls the plan's Task-4 text doesn't pin down, plus one it got backwards.** **(a) Ranking is distinctDays desc THEN sightings desc — and that ordering is the product decision, not a detail.** The plan states it, but the reason belongs in the record: one viral thread re-scrolled twenty times in an afternoon would otherwise out-rank an author the algorithm fed you on four separate days, which is the exact opposite of "affinity". The floor (`minDays`) and the sort key are the same idea applied twice. **(b) A retired person reads `stage: null` but `inRoster: true`.** The plan says "people rows by `lower(handle)` (stage, skip `retired`)" — skipping them outright would make every retired handle read as an unknown discovery and re-nag "Start their file" forever, which is precisely the outcome retiring someone expresses a preference against. So the stage is dropped (a retired person has no *current* stage) but membership is kept; the drawer then renders neither a chip nor a CTA for them. `inRoster` answers "does stratus already know this handle", and a retired row is a yes. **(c) The `lower(handle)` join wrapper was dropped as dead weight** — `parseIngestRow`, `normalizePersonHandle` and the voice scrape all lowercase on write, so both sides are already lowercase and a `lower()` would only cost the index. Commented in-file so nobody re-adds it. **(d) `intParam` is a new shared helper, not three inline blocks.** `days`/`limit`/`minDays` all want the same rule the `GET /harvest/runs` limit already implements — absent → default, out of range → **clamped**, anything that isn't a positive integer → **400** with the caller's own code. The 400 (rather than a silent fall-back to the default) is the load-bearing half: a typo'd `?days=1O` must not answer with a confident 30-day roster. **(e) No `count` field.** The plan's response shape is `{days, minDays, authors}` and it shipped exactly that — `authors.length` is the count, and the two echoed params are what make a clamped request self-describing. **Also worth stating:** own tweets are NOT filtered out of the corpus (decision 8 — filtering is analysis-time), so your own handle can appear in the affinity list if x.com/home feeds you your own posts; nothing stores a self-handle server-side today, so a filter would have cost a new lookup for a cosmetic win. Revisit only if it actually annoys.

## Archived gotchas (Waves 0–2)

- **`bun test` has 2 pre-existing failures** in `src/x/routes/brief.test.ts` ("brief quests (C9)") — full suite 624 pass / 2 fail, identical before and after UI.8. Unrelated to CSS/settings; a Wave-1 or brief-owning task should investigate. Don't read "2 fail" as a regression. **RESOLVED (2026-07-18 Wave-0 audit):** the failures were never flaky tests — bare `bun test` (without `bun run test`'s `SQLITE_PATH=:memory:`) opened the REAL `./stratus.db`, so `onConflictDoNothing` seeds kept stale dates from prior runs. `src/db/client.ts` now defaults to `:memory:` when `NODE_ENV === 'test'` (Bun sets it under `bun test`), and the local stratus.db was purged of accumulated test junk (backup `stratus.db.backup-20260718-121416`; tokens table was empty — no real data existed locally). 730/730 green both ways now.
- **UI.8 design-token conventions:** `styles.css` `:root` = `--strat-*` set + legacy short aliases (`--bg`,`--accent`,`--status-*`…, all mapping to `--strat-*`) — the 2.4k-line sheet still resolves through the aliases; don't rip them out until a full migration pass. Alpha fills use `color-mix(in srgb, var(--strat-*) N%, transparent)` (exact-equal to the old rgba, tracks the base for UI.9's light theme) — a pre-existing `color-mix` at ~line 2051 already used the pattern, so it's sanctioned. `#fff`→`white` keyword. Fonts load from absolute `/fonts/Inter-*.woff2` (public/ → dist root; same files Studio uses) — no `sidepanel.html` change needed. `color-scheme: dark` stays until UI.9 adds `:root[data-theme='light']`.
- **ST.1 / compose.ts pattern engine:** `patternCoords(pattern,w,h,spacing,seed)` returns box-local coords; `dots|grid|diagonal|plus` share one top-left-first lattice (marks at `spacing/2 + k·spacing`, `< w/h`), `blobs` returns seeded-random `{x,y,r}` (count = `floor(w/spacing)·floor(h/spacing)`, 3 PRNG draws/blob in x,y,r order). `mulberry32` is the only sanctioned RNG in the Studio — Math.random is banned (determinism = the preview-IS-artifact contract). `path` layers author SVG in a 100×100 viewbox scaled into `box`; strokes divide lineWidth by mean scale. Downstream S5 tasks (ST.3 mascot, ST.4 patterns) build on these.
- **UI.1 settings platform landed INERT.** The routes (`GET /x/settings`, `GET /x/settings/values?scope=`, `PATCH /x/settings`, `POST /x/settings/reset`) are always-mounted (`settingsRouter` next to `data`/`explorer` in `mountX`) and fully working, but **no consumer reads the store yet** — pure modules stay pure and take params (Decision 6); UI.2–UI.7 wire brief/quests/people/gates/band/budgets through `getSetting`. Only the `doctrine` group is seeded (D13). Store cache is a module-level `Map` invalidated on every write; PATCH validates ALL keys before a one-txn upsert (all-or-nothing). Value column is JSON (`text {mode:'json'}`) so numbers/booleans/strings/arrays round-trip uniformly. `app_settings` = DB table #28; migration `0013_silent_nova.sql` (no seed INSERT — defaults live in the registry).
- **Sha-stamp trap (learned at UI.9, applies to EVERY task):** the codemap header + STATE `last-commit` embed the task's commit sha, but a commit can't contain its own hash — if you `commit --amend` to inject the sha, the amend produces a *new* sha and orphans the one you just wrote (this is precisely why UI.1's recorded `7e07dd5` vanished and forced a sync). **Don't chase convergence.** Stamp the sha of the just-created commit, amend once, and STOP — accept a possible one-commit lag between the stamp and the true post-amend HEAD. The Step-0 staleness guard runs `sync` on any mismatch, so a one-off lag is harmless by design. Prefer: commit → read sha → stamp → amend → done (no re-verify loop).
- **UI.9 `uiScale` only affects inherited/`em` sizes, not px tokens.** The sheet is px-heavy (no rem), so scale is implemented as `body { font-size: var(--ui-root-size) }` — it shifts base body text and anything relative, but the many explicit-px component font-sizes don't scale. True global scaling would need a rem migration (out of scope; QA-adjust if the effect feels too weak). Theme resolution + normalizers are pure exports in `storage.ts` (`resolveTheme(pref, prefersLight)`, `normalizeTheme/Density/Scale`) — reuse them; `main.tsx` owns the `<html>` stamping (sync matchMedia best-guess to avoid flash, then storage-corrected, + storage.onChanged + matchMedia-change listeners).
- **`bun test` ran 626 pass / 0 fail at UI.1 finish** (HEAD `1299e43` + UI.1). The 2 `brief.test.ts` failures the UI.8 gotcha flags are shared-in-memory-DB **order-dependent flakiness** (§9), not a hard failure — they did not manifest here. Still worth a brief-owning task's attention, but "2 fail" ≠ regression.
- **UI.10 rail is now GROUPED.** App.tsx uses `TAB_GROUPS` (Operate/Author/Library/Learn/System eyebrow clusters), not the old flat `TABS` — a later task adding a tab adds it to a group. The vertical 104px rail's layout rules live at styles.css `.app`/`.topbar`/`.brand`/`.tab-group` **~L265+** (the plan's "rail/topbar ~L44-100" pointer is STALE — L44-100 is the UI.8 token block). `.topbar` is still the class name despite being a sidebar.
- **UI.10 primitives are INERT except `EmptyState`** (wired into App.tsx's not-configured branch — the "used by the shell" done-when). `Section`/`SubTabs`/`SettingRow`/`Slider`/`GearPopover` + `settingsClient.{loadSettingGroups,flattenSettings,patchSetting,resetGroup,resetKeys}` + `api.settings.{get,patch,reset}` are exported but UNWIRED — first real consumers are UI.11 (Settings rebuild) + the Wave-5 inline gears (UI.12–15). Per D7 this is foundational, not dead code; don't delete on an "unused" pass.
- **UI.10 brandmark reuses `/icons/icon128.png`** (byte-identical to DS `assets/logo.png`, md5 `d06f9c20…`) — no separate logo asset added; the plan's "copy logo.png → public/icons" was a no-op since icon128 already sits there and serves from dist root.
- **ST.2 registry contract (the S5 template-authoring API):** `extension/src/sidepanel/studio/registry.ts` owns `TemplateId` (union), `TEMPLATES` (metadata rows), `templateMeta(id)`/`supportsAiBackground(id)` helpers, `EMPTY_STAT`, the `TemplateState` interface (every per-template input the shell holds), and `buildSpec(id, state, kit) → RenderSpec` (a switch, exhaustive on the union). To add a template downstream: (1) extend the `TemplateId` union + add a `TEMPLATES` row (set `supportsAiBackground`), (2) add the template's inputs to `TemplateState` + a `case` in `buildSpec`, (3) add a field component to `fields.tsx` + one `{template === 'x' && <XFields .../>}` line in the shell's JSX. `supportsAiBackground(id)` **replaced the old `BG_TEMPLATES` set** — use it, it reads the metadata row. Card size is now `{w,h}` (object), not the `"1200×675"` string — display is `${size.w}×${size.h}`; save/download read `.w`/`.h` directly (always finite, so the old `Number.isFinite` guards are gone). **ST.2 is behavior-neutral & UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** — extension build+typecheck+lint green, `templates.test.ts`/`brandKit.test.ts` untouched (142 ext tests pass); pixel-identical rendering is asserted by construction, not yet eyeballed (rides ST.9's browser walk).
- **`StudioSeed` stays exported from `Studio.tsx`** (the shell) post-ST.2 — App.tsx's `import { StudioPanel, type StudioSeed } from './Studio.tsx'` is unchanged. ST.7 adds `template?: 'quote' | 'thread'` to `StudioSeed` there (still in the shell), not in registry.ts.
- **ST.3 mascot is BROWSER-VERIFIED** (unlike ST.1/ST.2 which deferred to ST.9): rendered the real `mascotLayers` output onto a canvas via a throwaway HTTP harness — all 4 poses draw a clean lobed cloud, the stat pose-flip is visible (celebrating has confetti+arm-puffs, idle is a plain happy cloud), quote/stat/banner placements are collision-free. The hand-authored `CLOUD_PATH` + face arcs are validated. ST.9's full-Studio browser walk still owns the in-panel toggle/preset re-skin check, but the geometry is no longer a risk.
- **ST.3 pattern — mascot is `path`-only + one shared box (S5.3):** every mascot feature (body, eyes, mouth, arms, confetti, thought-trail) is a `path` layer sharing the SAME `box: {x, y, w:100*scale, h:80*scale}` 100×100 viewbox — `circlePath(cx,cy,r)` builds filled dots as SVG arcs, so there's no per-feature canvas math and the whole thing is one coordinate system. Sleeping adds one `text` "zzz" (plain text — repo no-emoji rule §7.31). Colours are ALL `shade(kit.accent, …)` (body fill 0.85, face ink −0.55), so the mascot re-skins for free when ST.4's presets change the accent — **do not hardcode a mascot hex.** Templates guard with `if (kit.mascot && !data.background …)` — the byte-identity regression tests (`mascot:false` === pre-task) lock this; keep it when ST.5/ST.7/ST.8 add cards that should carry the mascot.
- **`BrandKit.mascot` is a REQUIRED boolean field now (default true).** ST.4 rewrites `brandKit.ts` for multi-preset storage (`studio:brandKits`) + built-in starter presets (`Midnight`/`Paper`/`Neon`) — each preset constant and the lenient multi-parse MUST carry `mascot` (fallback true), or a preset load drops the field and typecheck breaks. The 3 full-kit test literals (`templates.test.ts` mascot:false, `brandKit.test.ts` mascot:false, `mascot.test.ts` mascot:true) are the only other places a kit is spelled out in full.
- **ST.4 landed: `loadBrandKit`/`saveBrandKit` (single-kit) are GONE.** Storage is now the bundle API: `loadBrandKits()`/`saveBrandKits(bundle)` + `activeKit(bundle)` for the current kit. `loadBrandKits` migrates a legacy `studio:brandKit` value into `kits.default` on first load, else seeds `STARTER_KITS`, and keeps writing the legacy key for the active kit (rollback safety). The pure preset mutations (`patchActiveKit`/`setActivePreset`/`savePresetAs`/`renamePreset`/`deletePreset`/`canDeletePreset`) never touch chrome.* — the shell wraps them in `applyBundle(fn)` which persists. `parseBrandKitsFile` (import) accepts BOTH the multi bundle AND a legacy single-kit JSON.
- **ST.5 celebration cards suppress the mascot on null data.** `milestoneCardSpec`/`streakCardSpec` render a graceful placeholder ("no milestone crossed yet" / "no streak yet — show up today") when the value is null AND drop the celebrating mascot (nothing to cheer). The `templates.test.ts` blocks lock this (null → no `path` layer). Both cards' data comes resolved from the shell: **override beats auto** (a typed number wins over the account/streak read), and the milestone's `dateLabel` ("reached YYYY-MM-DD") only shows when the auto value is in effect (an override has no crossing date). `milestoneData`/`streakData` are `useMemo`-stabilized in `Studio.tsx` so their fresh-object identity doesn't retrigger the debounced render effect every render.
- **ST.5 streak start-date is derived, not stored.** `brief.quests.streak` only carries `{current, todayComplete}` — no start date — so `Studio.tsx` computes "since" as `today − (current−1) days`. If a future task adds a real streak-start to the brief, prefer it over this derivation.
- **ST.6 code card is measure-free by design — `MONO_ADVANCE = 0.6` is exact, not a guess.** JetBrains Mono is 600 advance units on a 1000 em, so a token at column `c` sits at `codeLeft + c·fontSizePx·0.6` with zero canvas `measureText`. That's WHY the mono font is bundled (the ratio must be identical across machines). `codeCardSpec` caps at 18 lines / 62 cols (`CODE_FONT_PX=22`, panel `{72,44,1056,588}`); over-cap → `⌄ trimmed` footer (U+2304, a symbol not an emoji — the repo no-emoji rule is unaffected). Token boxes are given a generous width (`text.length·fontSizePx+8`, ≫ 0.6 advance) so a single-line left-aligned token can never wrap/ellipsize even if the real glyph advance drifts slightly from 0.6. **ST.6 is UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (like ST.1/2/4/5) — the in-panel pixel/column check rides ST.9's Studio walk; the layout math itself is asserted in `templates.test.ts` (column x-diff === colDiff·size·MONO_ADVANCE).
- **ST.6 fonts came from fontsource (jsDelivr `jetbrains-mono@latest` latin-400/700 woff2, ~21KB each), not the official JetBrains release.** They are the same OFL-1.1 JetBrains Mono, latin-subset (smaller = fine for code cards). If ST.9/QA finds a needed glyph missing, swap for the full official woff2. The two files are `extension/public/fonts/JetBrainsMono-{Regular,Bold}.woff2`; Vite copies `public/` → dist root, so they serve at `/fonts/…` and `chrome.runtime.getURL('fonts/…')` resolves (same path as Inter — no manifest/`web_accessible_resources` change; the side panel is an extension page).
- **ST.7 thread cover + list card are the two NEW background-capable templates** (bringing the bg-capable set to quote/banner/thread/list). `parseListItems(raw)` lives in `templates.ts` (exported): trim → drop blanks → strip a leading `\d+[.)]` or `-*•` marker **followed by whitespace** (`/^\s*(?:\d+[.)]|[-*•])\s+/`, so "3 lessons" with no punctuation stays whole) → cap 6. The list card's accent number disc is a `ring` with `width = 2r` (r=11,width=22 → filled disc radius 22), the same trick the pfp monogram uses; the digit ink is `contrastOn(kit.accent)`. The thread cover badge is `['a thread', '1/N']` (plain text, §7.31; N = `max(1, round(count))`). Both cards call `baseLayers` so patterns/AI-bg compose; the thread's thinking mascot is guarded `kit.mascot && !data.background`. **ST.7 is UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (like ST.1/2/4/5/6) — the Composer→thread-cover seed handoff, the list render, and both cards' pixels ride ST.9's Studio walk; the pure spec math + `parseListItems` are bun-tested (55 tests in templates.test.ts). Seed routing lives in `Studio.tsx`'s effect (`seed.template === 'thread' ? 'thread' : 'quote'`), not the registry — component-level, covered by ST.9's browser check per the plan.
- **ST.8 chart card is the 9th and last S5 template — Lane C is code-complete but UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (like ST.1/2/4/5/6/7). Pure spec + `chartData` gate/normalization are bun-tested (148 studio tests, 239 ext total, typecheck+build+changed-file lint green); the live 30-day follower curve, the real best-times heatmap, and the below-gate muting all ride ST.9's Studio walk. `heatmapCells` intensity uses **min→max** normalization over the sufficient set (NOT absolute 0..max) — the least-active measured cell reads at the 0.15 alpha floor, the most-active at 0.9; a flat measured set is uniform 0.5. If ST.9's browser check finds the heatmap too washed-out on a thin single-user sample, that's the knob to revisit (raise the 0.15 floor or switch to absolute normalization), QA-adjustable like the other ST card placements.
- **Studio browser-QA harness pattern (ST.9, reusable):** a `Bun.build({target:'browser'})` of a tiny entry that imports the real `compose`/`templates`/`registry` modules, served (HTML + bundle + `extension/public/fonts`) by a `Bun.serve`, driven via claude-in-chrome. Shim `chrome.runtime.getURL = p => '/'+p` so `fonts.ts` loads the served WOFF2s. `window.STUDIO.{gallery,determinism,copyPng}` on the page. **Clipboard write needs a real click + focused document** — a `javascript_tool` eval fails "Document is not focused"; inject a button and click it with the `computer` tool (the extension's Copy PNG is a click handler, so this reproduces the real path). The `.gallery(preset)`/`.determinism(id)` helpers were enough to verify fonts/mascot/presets/pixel-determinism without the unpacked extension. Recreate the harness for future Studio visual QA; do not commit it.
- **`bun test` ran 730 pass / 0 fail at ST.9** (HEAD `289ec2c` + ST.9). The 2 `brief.test.ts` "brief quests (C9)" failures were later root-caused at the Wave-0 audit (see the RESOLVED note above): bare `bun test` vs `bun run test` DB targeting, fixed in `src/db/client.ts`.
- **ST.4 patterns only reach background-capable templates (quote, banner).** `baseLayers(kit,w,h,bg,scrimAlpha,pattern?)` is the only pattern injection point and only quote/banner use it (stat/pfp call `background(kit)` directly). A new bg-capable card (ST.5/ST.8) gets the background segmented control **for free** if its `TEMPLATES` row sets `supportsAiBackground:true` — BUT its data interface must carry `patternKind?`/`patternSeed?`, its `buildSpec` case must forward `state.patternKind`/`state.patternSeed` (conditional-spread — `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`), and its spec must call `baseLayers(..., patternArg(data.patternKind, data.patternSeed))`. The bg segmented control (`gradient|dots|grid|diagonal|plus|blobs|ai`) is inline in `Studio.tsx` and gates on `supportsAiBackground(template)`; `bgMode` is the single source of truth, `patternKind` derives from it (don't add a second patternKind state — biome exhaustive-deps flagged it).

If you hand-edit the seed, keep the value single-quoted and rely on the breakpoint markers, never introduce a bare statement-terminating `;`.

Gate with canonical `bun run test` (**971/0**), not bare `bun test` (the `inspect.test.ts` file-vs-`:memory:` trap still applies — RU.3 gotcha). **Binds RU.6/RU.7** — see D58's binds.

The background's `confirmDraft(tweetId)` POSTs `/x/radar/drafts/:id/confirm` (RU.5, idempotent) then `stampDraftId` writes the returned reply_drafts `id` onto the sighting through `enqueueRadar` (single writer, markClicked pattern — an evicted sighting is skipped). `RadarSighting.draftId?` survives `mergeSightings` like `reply`/`variants`/`clickedAt`. **Best-effort §7.8:** a failed confirm logs a warn (ignores `unconfigured`), the UX proceeds — the reply was already copied. The confirm returns the **full reply_drafts row directly** (`c.json(created,201)`/`c.json(existing,200)`), so `res.data.id` is top-level (no wrapper). `markDraftsOnServer([id],'clicked')` stays (a no-op PATCH on the already-clicked confirmed row). **First message-guard test suite** — new `extension/src/shared/messages.test.ts` (`isRadarConfirm`); none existed before. **Binds RU.7** (paste→posted): the stamped `draftId` is the row RU.7 PATCHes to `posted`; RU.7 also needs its own confirm-if-needed for deep links (D58 bind).
