The **background** is the only writer of the `stratus:openPerson` session key (`{handle, at}`, via its own `enqueueOpenPerson` chain) and does the best-effort `chrome.sidePanel.open({tabId: sender.tab?.id})` (user-gesture credit survives one hop on Chrome ≥116; `.catch()` if not — the panel is one action-click away). **`App.tsx` is the consumer + clearer:** a `useEffect` (empty deps — inlines `setPersonHandle`+`setTab('people')` so only stable setters are referenced, biome-clean) reads the key on mount + `chrome.storage.onChanged` (session area), routes to the dossier, then fires `stratus/open-person-clear` so the handoff cell can't replay on a later panel open. The chip/panel handlers `preventDefault`+`stopPropagation` so X never navigates; the collapse toggle is a header sibling of the title, so a toggle click never doubles as an open. **NO new tests** (guards trivially typed — the task block said skip; there's no message-guard suite for `isOpenPerson`/`isOpenPersonClear`, unlike RU.6's `isRadarConfirm`). **UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (not scriptable): the two click paths landing on the right dossier + no replayed opens are the live done-when items. Gate with canonical `bun run test` (**1003/0** — unchanged), not bare `bun test` (inspect.test.ts file-vs-`:memory:` trap unchanged — RU.3 gotcha). **Completes the on-page click-through**; the D9 content.ts chain continues at **RU.7** (on-page variant chips + paste→posted).
- **RU.7: on-page radar variant chips + paste→posted are LIVE in `content.ts`.** `syncVariantChips(focusedId)` runs once/scan (after `syncContextPanel`): for the focused article it asks the background `stratus/radar-variants-get` ONCE per tweetId (`variantCache` Map + `variantFetchInFlight` Set; `requestVariants` calls `scheduleScan()` on resolve so the chips inject on the next scan — a MISS is cached too, so it won't re-fetch; a page reload clears the Map). Chips inject via `actionRow.appendChild(strip)` behind a SEPARATE `variantChipsHandled` WeakSet (distinct from `replyMasterHandled`, so both inject) — **NOT** article-afterend (that slot is AX.5's panel; colliding would thrash its `nextElementSibling` anchor). The strip dies with the recycled action row (no orphan). Each chip: angle label + `variantChipPreview(text)`, `title`=full text; click → `clearReplyEditor` (selectAll+delete only when non-empty) + `typeTextInto(findReplyEditor())` (clipboard + hint span fallback), `data-active` marker, then `stratus/radar-variant-pasted`. Posting stays MANUAL (§7.28) — the chip fills the composer; the user hits Reply. Background: `getVariants` (buffer `isReplyVariants(hit.variants)` → `GET /x/radar/drafts?tweetId=` `.drafts[0]` fallback, `variants ?? [{text:replyText,angle}]`) and `pasteVariant` (POST idempotent confirm → read `id`+`replyText` off the returned reply_drafts row → PATCH `/x/replies/:id {status:'posted', ...(text!==primary?{replyTextEdited}:{})}`; 2nd chip click no-ops the status but re-PATCHes replyTextEdited; best-effort §7.8). **No server/schema/route change** — pure consumer of RU.5's confirm + tweetId filter + the `copied→posted` transition (`ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS`, replies.ts). Pure helpers in NEW `shared/variantChips.ts` (`variantChipPreview` truncation + `isReplyVariants` non-empty shape guard, angle a loose string) — 7 bun tests; message guards `isRadarVariantsGet`/`isRadarVariantPasted` +4 in `messages.test.ts`. **DOM behavior UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (content.ts convention + D64/D65 tail): the 3-chips-render + chip-click-types-and-flips-posted + deep-link-works-via-idempotent-confirm are the live done-when items. Gate with canonical `bun run test` (**1014/0**, +11), not bare `bun test` (inspect.test.ts file-vs-`:memory:` trap unchanged — RU.3 gotcha); root+extension typecheck clean, extension `vite build` green, `bun run lint` 0-error (3 changed files biome-formatted). **Binds RU.8** (next D9 content.ts task): a THIRD action-row WeakSet for the ⊕ button; widens `RadarSighting.band`→`'manual'` and the server band on `parseBatchTweets`/`RadarBatchTweet`/`RadarDraftInsert`.
- **RU.8: manual add-to-radar ⊕ is LIVE in `content.ts`, and `'manual'` is now a first-class `RadarBand`.** `attachRadarAddButton(article)` runs from `scan` (its own `radarAddHandled` WeakSet — the THIRD action-row button after save + reply-master) on EVERY tweet (timeline + status, band-null included); `onRadarAddClick(btn)` resolves the article from `btn.closest`, builds a `band:'manual'` sighting via `readTweetSignals(article) ?? synthManualSignals(article)` (zeros + `time[datetime]`-derived ageMin when there's no metrics label), flips the button ✓ for 1.5s, and sends `stratus/radar-report` with a single sighting IMMEDIATELY (NOT the 2s-batched `pendingRadar` path — one deliberate click). The background stays the single §7.24 buffer writer; `recordRadarSighting`/`flushRadar` are byte-untouched. **`'manual'` semantics across the stack:** it is QUEUE/UX METADATA, never a classifier verdict — `mergeSightings` never downgrades an existing manual band on a hot/warm re-sight (`prev.band==='manual' ? 'manual' : s.band`), eviction sorts non-manual to the front (dropped first, `am - bm`) so a human pin outlives fresher auto-captures, `rankSightings` has a leading manual-first comparison ABOVE roster tier, `isRadarSightings` accepts it. Server: `parseBatchTweets` (replies.ts) accepts `band:'manual'`, `RadarBatchTweet`/`RadarDraftInsert` (radar.ts) widened, so a pinned tweet drafted via "Draft replies" persists `radar_drafts.band='manual'`. **THE ONE TRAP (D69c):** the confirm endpoint (radar.ts ~L285) rebuilds `contextSnapshot.signals.band` from `row.band` — it now **coerces `'manual'`→`null`** (`row.band === 'manual' ? null : (row.band as PostSignals['band'])`) so a confirmed manual reply never lands in the Playbook's §7.19 hot/warm band cells; DON'T remove that coercion. `Radar.tsx` renders `.radar-band-manual` (accent-tinted) + a "manually added · Nd" whyLine for zero-signal pins; `BatchReplyTweet.band` (shared/types.ts, the 6th file) is widened so `draftReplies` can forward `s.band`. Tests +4 (radar.test.ts merge/evict/rank, src/test.test.ts parseBatchTweets, server radar.test.ts confirm-coerce). content.ts DOM behavior UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER (convention): ⊕ lands the tweet top of the Radar queue with a `manual` chip + next "Draft replies" drafts it are the live done-when items. Gate with canonical `bun run test` (**1018/0**, +4), not bare `bun test` (inspect.test.ts file-vs-`:memory:` trap — RU.3 gotcha). **Completes the D9 content.ts chain through RU.8✓**; NT.5 is next in that lane but gated behind NT.1/NT.3/NT.4 (server, none done) — RU.9 (server, dep RU.2✓) is the readiest unblocked task.
- **RU.9: Playbook batch-vs-single attribution is now source-EXACT, not text-heuristic-first.** `classifyReplyOrigin(reply, draftSourceByPostedId, radarRepliesByTarget)` — the 2nd arg is now a `ReadonlyMap<string, reply_drafts.source|null>` (was a `ReadonlySet<string>`; D71). A reply linked to a posted `reply_drafts` row is `radar` iff that row's `source==='radar'` (RU.5's confirm stamps it), else `single`; the old collapsed-whitespace radar text-match survives ONLY as the fallback for replies with NO draft link (pre-tooling history). If you add a new drafting surface, stamp `reply_drafts.source` at insert AND teach this classifier + `loadOriginRows` (routes/playbook.ts, which selects `replyDrafts.source`). **Route-test trap:** the main `describe('playbook route')` beforeAll seeds pb_r1/pb_r2 (single) + pb_r3 (radar) PERMANENTLY (no afterAll) — they persist for the whole file. A test needing a specific batch-vs-single count must run in its OWN describe with afterAll cleanup (like `radar source-exact attribution (RU.9)`), never extend the main beforeAll (a measured reply ripples into angle/band/model/latency/roster cells and breaks their exact-count asserts). Gate with canonical `bun run test` (**1020/0**, +2), not bare `bun test` (inspect.test.ts file-vs-`:memory:` trap — RU.3 gotcha). **Shell/grep trap (root cause found):** `grep` silently returns NO matches on `src/x/playbook.ts` — git also flags it `Bin` in `--stat`. Cause: **2 literal NUL bytes** (0x00) at `playbook.ts:160`, a PRE-EXISTING deliberate sentinel — `` `${r.pillar ?? '\x00'}|${r.register ?? '\x00'}` `` uses NUL as the "null-slug" separator in the pillar×register composite key (impossible in a real slug). A NUL in the first 8KB makes git+grep treat the file as binary and suppress line output. tsc/biome/bun read it fine. Use `awk`/absolute-path `Read` on this file, not `grep`. Also relative-path `ls`/`find` were flaky this session — prefer absolute paths.
- **RU.10 CLOSES the RU sub-plan (RU.1–RU.10).** The whole radar↔reply unification is shipped: 3-variant single+batch drafting, the `POST /x/radar/drafts/:tweetId/confirm` endpoint (radar draft → measured `reply_drafts` row), on-page variant chips (paste=posted), the ⊕ manual add, and source-exact Playbook attribution. `scripts/smoke-radar-reply-flow.ts` is the rerunnable $0 end-to-end proof (confirm→idempotent→PATCH-posted→origin=radar-by-source). CLAUDE.md §"Overhaul 7.7" is the phase entry. **Live done-when tails not yet observed** (need real X use): a variant chip typed into a live reply box → the confirmed `source='radar'` draft → after the next harvest it gains `postedTweetId` and shows in the Playbook radar bucket without text-matching; ⊕ on a cold tweet top-ranks it with a `manual` chip. **Wave 2 remaining:** AX.7 (AX docs-sync — the AX.1–6 augmented-X-UI surfaces), then the NT server lane (NT.1/NT.2/NT.4, none-dep, parallel-ok) and RL.1 (reply-list pure engine, none-dep). The D9 content.ts chain's next task NT.5 stays gated behind NT.1/NT.3/NT.4 (server, none done).
- **RL.1: the reply-list engine is IN but INERT** — `src/x/replyLists/engine.ts` has no consumer until RL.2 (tables + CRUD) and RL.3 (`/use` route: pick → compose → stamp `lastUsedAt`/`useCount` + insert `reply_list_uses`, all in ONE sync txn §7.13). It is pure by contract: **no DB, no clock, no `Math.random`** — the route injects `rng` (server-side `Math.random` is fine here; the Studio's PRNG ban is a determinism contract that does NOT apply to this module). The anti-repeat promise ("no immediate repeats over 10 consecutive uses") only holds if **RL.3 actually stamps `lastUsedAt` on every non-preview use** — the exclusion window is the only mechanism. Two lint/impl traps found here: biome `lint/suspicious/noMisleadingCharacterClass` **forbids a ZWJ (`\u200D`) inside a `[...]` class** — the emoji strip had to become an alternation (`/[\p{Extended_Pictographic}\p{Emoji_Modifier}\p{Regional_Indicator}]|\u200D|\uFE0F/gu`); and with `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` every pool/word index needs a `?? null` fallback (no `!` assertions — biome `noNonNullAssertion`). Verified beyond the unit tests with a throwaway 10-use simulation (not committed): no immediate repeats, vars filled, emoji/`@` stripped, jitter rates plausible.
- **RL.2: the tables are in, the anti-repeat state has a home, nothing writes it yet.** `reply_list_items.lastUsedAt`/`useCount` and the whole `reply_list_uses` table stay untouched until **RL.3's `/use`** stamps them in ONE sync txn — the anti-repeat promise is exactly that stamp plus `pickItem`'s exclusion window, nothing else. Three contracts RL.3–RL.5 should not re-derive: (1) **`humanizer` is already normalized in the DB** (parsed through `parseHumanizerConfig` at write time), so `/use` just calls `resolveHumanizer(list.humanizer)` — no re-validation, and a stored value is always a FULL config; (2) **`POST /:id/items` always answers with the list's whole current set**, append and replace alike (RL.5's UI can render the response directly); (3) **item `createdAt` is stamped explicitly** (per-row offset starting past the list's current max) because a batch insert shares one `unixepoch()` millisecond — don't "simplify" back to the column default or insertion order goes non-deterministic (this bit once: an append landing in the same ms as the prior batch sorted into the middle). Item PATCH/DELETE match on `id` AND `listId` — a 404 there means wrong list, not missing item. `reply_list_uses` has **no FK** by design (RL.7's `canned` bucket must still see uses from a deleted list); `reply_list_items` DOES cascade. Malformed uuid → 400 `invalid_id`, valid-but-absent → 404 (ideas.ts idiom) — RL.5's client should treat them differently.
- **RL.3: `/use` is the route that makes the anti-repeat promise real** — before it, `reply_list_items.lastUsedAt` was never written by anything, so `pickItem`'s exclusion window was reading a column of nulls. Any surface that composes a canned reply MUST go through `POST /reply-lists/:id/use` (or `preview:true` when it's only rendering a sample) — a client that picks locally re-introduces the exact drift Decision 1 rejects.
- **RL.3: every `/use` text assertion needs a jitter-free list.** The route calls `Math.random` for real (server-side; the no-`Math.random` rule is a Studio/extension rule, not a server one), so `humanize` can prepend "ngl", drop a period or inject a typo at any time. The test helper `seedList()` creates lists with an all-zero-chance humanizer (`{prefixes:[],suffixes:[],prefixChance:0,…}`) — copy it for RL.5/RL.6/RL.8 assertions. The jitter itself is the engine suite's job (seeded rng).
- **RL.3: `reply_list_uses` rows do NOT cascade** (the table is FK-free on purpose, D77/RL.2), so `replyLists.test.ts`'s `afterAll` deletes them explicitly by `listId`. **RL.7 reads this table for the Playbook `canned` bucket** — any new suite or smoke that calls `/use` must clean up its use rows or it will show up as phantom canned attribution in a shared-DB run.
- **RL.3 opening guesses worth revisiting with real use:** `MAX_VAR_LEN` 120 (display name + handle bound) and the decision to allow `/use` on an inactive list. Neither is load-bearing; both are one-line changes if the panel wants different behavior.
- **RL.4: the generator has never called an LLM — not once, by design.** Every test is either a pure-function assertion or a pre-network guard (the 503 case force-unsets BOTH provider keys, ideas.test.ts pattern). The first real spend happens when **RL.5** builds the "Generate items" button; budget ~$0.003–$0.01 per click (12 items, temp 0.9, 1500 max tokens, reasoning low). RL.8's smoke stays $0 by default — a `--live` flag is the only place a real call belongs.
- **RL.4: `/generate` writes NOTHING, and that is load-bearing.** The proposal is returned, previewed, and applied by the human through `POST /reply-lists/:id/items` (`mode:'replace'|'append'`, `source:'ai'` — per-call, not per-item, D78e). RL.5 must not "helpfully" persist the proposal on arrival: the destructive overwrite is the one step Decision 3 keeps as an explicit click. The response `items` array is already the exact `{text}[]` shape `/items` accepts.
- **RL.5: the panel is now the ONLY place a reply list can be managed, and `api.replyLists.use()` is the only sanctioned way to compose a canned reply.** RL.6's QuickReplyPicker must call it (with `preview` absent/false so the anti-repeat state actually advances) and copy inside the same click handler — a local pick, or a `preview:true` "real" use, both silently break the anti-repeat promise (Decision 1 / RL.3's stamp). The ReplyLists panel deliberately uses `preview:true` everywhere because it is only *sampling*.
- **RL.5: `GET /x/reply-lists` has no `?active=` filter** (D78h) — the panel shows every list and dims the inactive ones. RL.6's picker menu is the place that should *offer* only `active` lists (client-side filter), but note an inactive list is still perfectly usable server-side (D79e) — "inactive" is presentation state, not a lock.
- **RL.5 opening guesses worth revisiting with real use:** the Test-render sample vars default to `Ana Pop`/`anapop` (arbitrary, just needs to be obviously fake), the generate count defaults to 12 (the server's `DEFAULT_GENERATED_ITEMS`), and the items add box splits on newlines with no escape hatch for a genuinely multi-line canned reply — if one is ever wanted, add a "paste as single item" toggle rather than changing the split.
- **RL.6: the picker normalizes client-side ON PURPOSE, mirroring the route's own guards.** `targetHandle` and `targetTweetId` are DROPPED (not sent) unless they match `USERNAME_RE` / `/^\d{1,32}$/`, and `vars.name`/`vars.handle` are trimmed to 120 chars — because RL.3 400s on a malformed target (D79g) and losing the whole use over a scraped display quirk would be absurd, while a missing var just degrades the template. If those route regexes ever change, `QuickReplyPicker.tsx`'s copies change with them (they're commented with the route constant names).
- **RL.6: `await api…use()` THEN `navigator.clipboard.writeText()` works in the side panel** — the panel document stays focused, which is what Chrome's clipboard-write gate actually checks; transient activation is not required for `writeText` there (Studio's `copyPng` already awaits a canvas `toBlob` before `clipboard.write`). It is still wrapped in try/catch, and the composed text stays on screen on refusal (D82b). If a future surface copies from a NON-focused context, this assumption is the first thing to re-check.
- **RL.6 is UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (panel convention, no tests added). The live done-whens RL.8 should watch: a Launch Room row's picker putting a name-filled humanized reply on the clipboard in one click, and two rapid uses of the same list on different rows yielding different items (the server shuffle, observable end-to-end for the first time).
- **RL.6 cache staleness is bounded by 60 s + the Lists-subtab unmount.** A list created in the Lists subtab appears in pickers as soon as the user leaves that subtab; created any other way (MCP, curl) it takes up to a minute. If a picker ever shows a deleted list, the click 404s and says so inline — no crash path.
- **RL.7: canned attribution is TEXT-MATCH ONLY, and that is the whole risk surface.** A canned reply has no `reply_drafts` row and no id link — the only evidence it ever existed is `reply_list_uses.renderedText` matching the published reply's text under `normalizeReplyText` (whitespace collapsed, nothing else). This works *because* the humanizer's typos are stored in `renderedText` exactly as they were copied (RL.3), so the paste is byte-faithful. It breaks the moment the user edits the text after pasting — that reply silently falls back to `unattributed`, never to a wrong bucket. **Undercount, never overcount** is the intended failure mode; don't 'fix' it with fuzzy matching.
- **RL.7: the `canned` bucket will read 0 until a canned reply is both pasted AND discovered.** The chain is: picker click → `reply_list_uses` row → user pastes manually → the reply ships → the 03:00 UTC dailyMetrics discovery writes it into `posts_published` → only then does the join see it. So a same-day check of `GET /x/playbook` after the first canned use legitimately shows nothing. RL.8's smoke should seed both sides rather than wait for discovery.
- **RL.7: `grep` over `src/x/playbook.ts` silently returned nothing before this commit** (two raw NUL bytes → `file` says `data`). Fixed to `'\0'` escapes (D83d). If a future session ever again finds a symbol that 'doesn't exist' in a file it can see with `Read`, try `grep -a` and then run the audit in codemap §7.32.
- **RL.7 opening guess worth revisiting:** the canned set is loaded **unwindowed** (every use row ever), mirroring `radarRows`. Fine at single-user scale; if `reply_list_uses` ever grows into the tens of thousands, bound it by the oldest `postedAt` in the published window rather than adding a fixed row cap.
- **RL.8: `smoke-reply-lists.ts` cleans up by fixed TARGET tweet id + the three list names, NOT by the created uuid.** `reply_list_uses` is FK-free, so a `/use` call in that script that omits `targetTweetId` would leak a row the cleanup can't find (and it would then sit in the Playbook's canned set forever). If you extend the script, either keep passing `TARGET` on every real use or widen `cleanup()` to delete by `listId` as well. Same reason the empty-list 409 check uses a *second* named list rather than disabling items on the first.
- **RL.8: the smoke's "≥3 distinct items in 10 uses" is deterministic, not luck.** With n=5 the exclusion window is `min(n-1, floor(n/2)) = 2`, so picks 1–3 are necessarily three different items. Shrink the seeded list below 3 items and that assertion becomes a coin flip — keep it at 5.
- **RL.8 was run on a machine WITH an LLM key**, so the `/generate` → 503 branch of the smoke was not exercised live (the unknown-list 404 branch was). Verify the 503 path the first time you run it keyless — it is the exact code path a fresh clone hits.
- **NT.1: `extension/src/shared/notifications.ts` is PURE and INERT** — nothing imports it until NT.5. **The shape NT.2's ingest and NT.5's DOM code build against:** `parseNotificationCell(article) → {kind: 'like'|'repost'|'follow'|'other', handles: string[] (lowercased, deduped, DOM order), targetText: string|null} | null`. Callers drop `'other'` client-side and never send it (plan Decision 6); `handles` may legitimately be `[]` on a skeleton cell — a batch entry with no handle has nothing to upsert and should be dropped too.
- **NT.1: the keyword-check ORDER is load-bearing, not stylistic.** `like → repost → follow` is what makes the deliberately loose Romanian stem `urmăre` safe: a follow cell carries no post text, so by the time the follow stems are tested the like/repost phrases have already had their chance at the cell. Reordering these three `if`s (or adding a looser stem above them) reintroduces the false positives the ordering was chosen to prevent.
- **NT.1: happy-dom parses inline `<svg><path d="…">` from `innerHTML` and `querySelector('svg path')` resolves it** — verified live in this suite, so an icon-first DOM parser is fixture-testable without a real browser. Useful for NT.5's fixtures if it wants any.
- **NT.1 opening guess worth revisiting:** `targetText` picks the **longest** `[dir="auto"]` block that contains no verb stem. A post whose own text contains "liked"/"apreciat" is therefore skipped and the cell may resolve `targetText: null` — which NT.2 handles (day-bucket fallback event id), so it degrades to a coarser event rather than a wrong one. If that turns out to be common in real data, the fix is structural (exclude the header block by position) rather than a longer stop-word list.
- **NT.2 for NT.3:** the route imports `MAX_ENGAGEMENTS_PER_BATCH`, `EngagementKind`, `EngagementInput`, `recordEngagements` from `src/x/people/engagements.ts`. Server-side `EngagementKind` is **`like|repost|follow` only** — the parser's `'other'` is deliberately not representable, so the route's kind whitelist is the enforcement point for it. `recordEngagements` already skips (never throws on) an invalid handle, so the route may pass handles through and report `skipped`.
- **NT.2 opening guesses worth revisiting after real data:** `MIN_TARGET_SNIPPET_CHARS = 20` (below it a prefix is not evidence) and `TARGET_POST_WINDOW = 300` recent own posts. If dossiers fill with day-bucket events instead of tweet-anchored ones, the snippet is failing the prefix match (emoji/link rendering is the likely cause) — that is a parser-side fix, not a looser threshold.
- **NT.3: the whole-batch-400 vs per-item-skip split is deliberate, and NT.5 must respect it.** A malformed *shape* (bad kind, non-string handle, unparseable `seenAt`, oversize batch) rejects the ENTIRE batch with an indexed error — that is a client bug, and failing loudly is how it gets found. A *valid-shaped but unusable* handle (`not!a!handle`, >15 chars) is passed to `recordEngagements` and comes back in `skipped`. So a scroll that produces one weird cell still lands the other 49; a scroll that produces one `'other'` cell that the client forgot to drop lands nothing. Drop `'other'` and empty-`handles` cells client-side.
- **NT.3: `POST /x/people/engagements` is still unreachable from any UI** — NT.5 is its only planned caller. Until then the endpoint is exercised only by its test suite and (later) `scripts/smoke-notifications.ts` (NT.7). If NT.7 runs before NT.5 lands, that smoke IS the only end-to-end proof the route works against the real DB.
- **NT.4: the notifications augmentation payload is ONE message, `{type:'stratus/notif-context', force?}` → `NotifContextResponse {ok: true, mentions: NotifContextMap, rankMap}`** (both types exported from `shared/messages.ts` — import and cast, don't re-declare the shape). `NotifContextMap` is keyed by the **REPLY's tweet id** (the `mentions` row's own `tweetId`, which is what `findPermalink` yields on a notification cell), valued `{parentText: string|null, status: MentionStatus}`. One ask covers BOTH augmentations: it refreshes the 5-min mentions cache and the 10-min S0.3 rank map (the same `rankMap` the radar tier stamp uses) in parallel.
- **NT.4: the response is ALWAYS `ok: true` — empty maps are the failure mode, not an error.** An unconfigured extension (`handleApiRequest` → `unconfigured`), a network failure, a non-JSON body, or a thrown handler all resolve to `{ok:true, mentions:{}, rankMap}` / the last-good map. NT.5 must render nothing extra in that case and never surface an error to the page — this augments a page it doesn't own (same discipline as AX.3's glance cache).
- **NT.4: `force: true` has exactly one legitimate call site** — immediately after the injected sync-replies chip's `POST /x/mentions/refresh` resolves. It drains any in-flight refresh BEFORE invalidating (D88b), so a scan-triggered pull that started pre-sync can't stamp the cache and pin pre-sync rows for the full 5 min. Sending it on page load / per scan would defeat the TTL and re-fetch 200 mentions on every scroll burst.
- **NT.4: only a SUCCESSFUL pull advances `notifContextAt`.** A failed refresh leaves the stamp where it was, so the next ask retries instead of going quiet for 5 minutes — the same rule `refreshRankMap` follows. If you ever add a "cache is warm" indicator, read the stamp, not the map (an empty map is a legitimate warm state on an account with no mentions).
- **NT.5: the notifications surface is LIVE in `content.ts`, and the D85 icon prefixes are now VERIFIED (one was wrong).** `FOLLOW_ICON_PREFIXES` is **`M17.863 13.44`** — the old `M12 11.816` guess was wrong; like (`M20.884 13.19` filled + `M16.697 5.5` outline) and repost (`M4.5 3.88`) are confirmed. Fixtures interpolate the constants, so no test moved. **Everything hangs off one `onNotificationsPage()` gate computed ONCE per `scan()`** (`location.pathname.startsWith('/notifications')`) — X navigates without reloads, so it is per-scan, never once at `start()`. Four behaviors: (a) `applyNotifParentContext(article)` runs **inside** the existing article loop (so it rides the same `root.querySelectorAll`), (b)+(c) `scanNotificationCells()` parses each `article[data-testid="notification"]` **once per scan** and serves both the tier chips and the harvest, (d) `syncNotifSyncChip()`. **Three placement rules a later task must not casually change:** (1) tier chips go **after** the header `[dir]` block, never inside it and never inside a `[dir="auto"]` — the parser reads the first `[dir]` as the header and the longest `[dir="auto"]` as the target post, so nesting would feed our own chip text back into the next parse (live-verified: 0 of 21 re-parses changed); (2) the parent line anchors `tweetText`-afterend with an article-append fallback (media-only replies); (3) the sync chip anchors after `[data-testid="primaryColumn"] h2`. **Do NOT add tier chips to tweet-articles** — AX.3's `applyPersonChips` already decorates them with the strictly richer glance vocabulary (D89a). `engagementSent` is a plain **Set** keyed `kind:handle:targetSlice` (not the radar's time-windowed Map): the server's deterministic ids make a re-send a no-op, so it is a chatter guard only. A single screen produced **28** engagements from 21 cells, so the ≤50 batch cap + overflow re-arm is a real path, not theoretical. **Nothing here writes `mentions`** — that table's max tweet_id IS the since_id checkpoint (§4); the freshness gap is closed by the human sync chip only, and `POST /x/mentions/refresh` keeps its 6/day server cap with **no client bypass and no retry loop**. **No new tests by design** (plan Task-5: the parser is NT.1's, the transport mirrors `flushSightings`, content.ts DOM is untested by convention) — `bun run test` stays **1129/0**. **What is still browser-UNVERIFIED:** the harness that validated the DOM ran the logic transcribed into the live page, not the unpacked extension — so the message round-trips (`stratus/notif-context` actually answering, the engagement POST actually landing rows, the sync chip's 429 path) are the live done-when tail. Load unpacked and watch: parent lines on known replies, a tier chip on a known ally/target, `people` rows with `source='notification'` appearing while scrolling, and one refresh per chip click. **Binds NT.6** (fans engagement count — different lane, server + `People.tsx`; its `EVENT_ICONS` Record is exhaustive over the extension `PersonEventType` union, so adding the three types forces the three labels) and **NT.7** (docs+smoke — `docs/notifications-surface.md` should carry the three verified glyphs and the "chips go after the header block" rule). **Releases `extension/src/content.ts`;** the D9 chain now stands at NT.5✓ with **HV.2** next in that lane.
- **NT.6: `engagementCount` on a fan row is DISPLAY-ONLY and must stay that way.** C10 decision 1 (user-confirmed, do not re-litigate): likes/reposts/follows are not reciprocity, so they move neither `computeStage` nor `rankFans` nor C5 followup classification. The route enforces it structurally — `loadFanEngagementCounts` runs *after* `rankFans` and *after* `.slice(0, limit)`, and its result is merged in the response `.map`, so there is no code path where a count can reach the comparator. `followups.test.ts` re-ranks the returned page through the real `rankFans` with counts stripped and asserts the order is unchanged; if a future task ever wants engagement-weighted fans, it needs a new field and a new decision, not an edit to this one.
- **NT.6: widening `PersonEventType` is what forces the dossier icons — that is the designed forcing function, not an obstacle.** `EVENT_ICONS` in `extension/src/sidepanel/People.tsx` is an exhaustive `Record<PersonEvent['type'], string>`; adding a type to the union in `extension/src/shared/types.ts` breaks the build until the icon exists. Note the *text* side needs nothing: `People.tsx` renders `{e.summary ?? e.type}` and the server already writes a readable `engagementSummary`. Root `bun run typecheck` does **not** cover the extension — `cd extension && bun run typecheck` (`tsc --build --noEmit`) is the one that catches this, and it is not part of `bun run build` (vite only). Extension-touching tasks should run both.
- **NT.6: the `· N engagements` line has never been rendered against real data.** No `their_like`/`their_repost`/`their_follow` rows exist outside tests — they only appear once someone scrolls x.com/notifications with the unpacked extension loaded and `passiveCapture` on. So the count reads 0 for every fan today, and the UI hides it. A pre-NT.6 server returns `undefined` for the field, which also reads as hidden — the panel degrades cleanly against an un-deployed backend.
- **NT.7: the whole C10 phase is documented in `docs/notifications-surface.md`** — the four on-page behaviors, the deterministic-id table, the passiveCapture gate, the sync-chip caps, the verified glyph prefixes, the after-the-header chip-placement rule, and the never-write-`mentions` rule. A future task touching the notifications surface updates that file (and the CLAUDE.md C10 entry), not `docs/*-tab.md`. It is reachable from `docs/README.md`'s new "On x.com itself" table.
- **NT.7 closes Wave 2.** Everything from here is Wave 3: HV.1 (server, migration, xhigh) → HV.2 (which re-claims `extension/src/content.ts`, next in the D9 chain) → HV.3–6, then the GR lane. The NT sub-plan's remaining obligations are **live-observation tails only** (an unpacked-extension session proving the message round-trips + the first `· N engagements` against real data); no code task remains for them.


## Archived at GR.7 (2026-07-24) — header priors moved verbatim from STATE.md

- **prior (GR.3):** the following-curation queue — **server-only, $0, no schema/migration/CSS/extension change** (suite 1211→**1224**, +13). New pure `src/x/connections.ts` (followups.ts style — no DB/clock/`Math.random`): `GRACE_DAYS=7`, `UNFOLLOW_WINDOW_MS=6h`, `WINDOW_CAP_MIN/MAX=15/18`, `DAILY_CEILING=40`, `MARK_LOOKBACK_MS=24h`; `eligibleForUnfollow` (active + `!followsBack` + `!keep` + `∉whitelist` + past-grace, `rankForUnfollow` = oldest first / `listPosition` desc / handle asc), `reviewQueued` → `{held, revoked}`, `releaseBudget` (jittered cap − window marks, daily-ceiling zero). `GET /following/queue` (registered ABOVE `/:handle`) is fully read-time (§7.12): whitelist = people mutual/ally non-retired + `loadTargetHandles()`; `keep`/`followsBack` enforced as columns; releases `active→queued` and revokes `queued→active` in one sync txn; **idempotent by topping up to `budget`** (D101a). Payload `{batch, eligibleTotal, releasedNow, windowUsed, windowCap, dailyUsed, dailyCeiling, lastCompleteRunAt}`. `following.ts` is RELEASED; `connections.ts` is NEW (GR.5's churn monitor imports its `DAILY_CEILING`). **Code-verified only** — the ledger it reads is still browser-UNVERIFIED (GR.2's tail).
- **prior (out-of-band cost fix, 2026-07-23):** the 2026-07-23 cost audit shipped **fix 1 + fix 2** directly into `src/x/workers/dailyMetrics.ts` (not a numbered masterplan task — a money leak caught mid-session). **The discovery timeline pull now IS the snapshot read:** it requests `ownedPrivate`, and new pure `ingestPulledTweet(tweet, now)` writes row + `metrics_snapshots` + retire in ONE idempotent sync txn keyed off the existing row. Before this, `discover()` pulled cheap fields and `snapshotDue()` re-read the SAME ids minutes later — every own tweet billed twice/day (~$0.09/day dupe at 90 tweets/day). `snapshotDue()` is now the **leftover** backstop (rows below the checkpoint / failed batches); steady-state it reads nothing. `fullScan` keeps the old two-phase seed-then-read (30-day private-field window). Replies dropped from the winner re-read (`isReply=false`) — their snapshot rides free on the pull, but a day-7 re-read would be a real extra call and "which content compounds" is about posts. `discover()` wrapped in try/catch so a pull failure degrades to the old shape instead of skipping snapshot+reread+mentions. New `RunResult.pullSnapshotted`. Tests +4 (`ingestPulledTweet` idempotency/adopt/reply), suite 1207→**1211**. Full history in `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md`. **The two DEFERRED findings are CA.1/CA.2 above.**


## Archived at GR.10 (2026-07-24) — the HV + GR lanes, closing Wave 3

### Deviations D93–D112 (HV.2 → GR.9; D97 stayed live as the standing bookkeeping rule)

- **D93** (HV.2): **four things.** **(a) Sha reconciliation + the first out-of-skill commit since Wave 0.** HV.1's recorded `bf5e7b5` was an amend-orphan (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` → false, same `(HV.1)` subject); real HV.1 = **`5ea3495`**, whose parent `5d4a1a4` is the real NT.7 (recorded `75f15e7` was likewise an orphan). Both corrected in the ledger. **But HEAD at HV.2 start was `e49f705 "refactor claude"` — genuine out-of-skill work**: CLAUDE.md slimmed by ~215 lines with its phase block moved to the NEW `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md` (+97), plus a codemap touch (+5) and a STATE touch (+1). **No ledger drift** (no task implemented), and codemap §7.29 already reflects it: **phase entries now go to `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md`, NOT CLAUDE.md** — every remaining docs-sync task (HV.6, GR.10, A3.15, UI.17) must target that file. **(b) The pure module type-only-imports `Extracted` from `../harvester.ts`.** The plan writes `toPassiveIngestRow(x: Extracted)` without saying how the type crosses the layer. Options were duplicating a 10-field structural interface in `shared/` or importing the real one; `verbatimModuleSyntax: true` guarantees `import type` is erased, so the import costs nothing at runtime (IIFE or bun:test) and cannot drift. `Extracted` is therefore exported. **(c) Gate order is tighter than the plan's.** The plan says the record gate is `enabled && isHomeTimelinePath && !isHarvestActive()`. That is necessary but not sufficient: `applyBand` runs for EVERY article on EVERY rAF-coalesced mutation burst, so `recordPassiveHarvest` also checks the pending map and the 30-min throttle — off ONE `findPermalink` querySelector — **before** calling `extractArticle` (~8 querySelectors). After a tweet's first sighting the throttle is the hot path for the next 30 minutes. Don't reorder these to "simplify". **(d) Two permalink readers, deliberately.** The throttle/pending key is `findPermalink(article).tweetId` (content.ts) while the shipped row's `tweetId` comes from `extractArticle`'s `idFrom` (harvester.ts). Both take the article's first `a[href*="/status/"]`, so they agree; they are separate because the cheap gate must not pay for the full extract. A later task that unifies them must keep the cheap reader on the gate path.
- **D96a** (HV.5): the plan's Task-5 said "section 11" in `src/x/playbook.ts` — **§11 is AI.12's `buildModelEffectiveness`**, so the funnel landed as **§12**. Any later plan text counting playbook sections is off by one from AI.12 onward; the next `build*` is §13.
- **D96b** (HV.5): the plan's return shape was `{hot: {...}, warm: {...}}`. Shipped instead as **`{cells: FunnelCell[], totalSeen, totalReplied}`** in `TIMELINE_BAND_ORDER` (`hot, warm, skip, null, 'unknown'`), matching every other section in the file (latency/model/band cells) and, more importantly, keeping the honest denominator visible: `skip`/`null`/`unknown` rows ARE the tweets the algorithm showed me and I passed on. A `{hot, warm}` object would have hidden them and made the funnel a tautology. The panel and any future digest fact read `cells.find(c => c.band === 'hot')`.
- **D96c** (HV.5): the plan said "null tweetTime → null band = excluded". **Not excluded — bucketed as `'unknown'`**, its own key in the `TimelineBand` union, never folded into the real `null` band (which means "classified, not worth a reply"). Same discipline as `LATENCY_BUCKETS`' `unknown` and `ROSTER_BANDS`' `unknown`. Silently dropping those rows would shrink the denominator by exactly the articles whose `<time>` didn't render — a systematic, invisible bias.
- **D99** (GR.1): **three judgement calls the plan's Task-1 text left open, all resolved toward "a broken scrape must not be able to lie".** **(a) Revive.** The plan enumerates five statuses and five edges, none of which covers a handle that is `confirmed`/`gone` and then appears in a later scrape (I re-followed them). Left alone, that row is invisible to the queue forever while its `follows_back`/`last_seen_at` keep refreshing — half-live, half-dead. Shipped: presence in any batch **revives `confirmed|gone` → `active`**. But `first_seen_at` is **NOT** reset (it stays fill-only exactly as the plan says) — it is the only follow-age signal that exists and a bad reconcile must not be able to erase it; the cost is that a genuinely re-followed person gets no fresh 7-day grace, which is cheap because the queue only ever nudges and `keep` is one click. `unfollow_marked_at` is likewise **never cleared anywhere** (not on revive, not on requeue): it is churn history that the 6h release budget and the monitor's 24h churn rule both count in a trailing window, so a re-follow or a failed tick must not hand budget back. **(b) The empty-run guard.** Decision 9 says partial runs never reconcile; generalized here — a run closed with `rowsSeen === 0` is stamped `completed_at` but **`complete` stays false**, so it neither reconciles nor counts as `lastCompleteRunAt`. Without this, a DOM drift that extracts nothing (X renames `UserCell`) closes a run seeing zero handles and flips the ENTIRE ledger to `gone` in one txn. **(c) Empty batches are legal when `done:true`.** The plan lists `rows_required` as an unconditional 400; a scrape whose row count lands on an exact multiple of the batch size then has nothing left to ship and can never close its run — so the run would never reconcile. `rows: []` is refused only when `done` is absent/false. **Also (minor, not deviations):** `status: 'banana'` → 400 `invalid_status` while a real-but-forbidden status → 409 `invalid_transition` (radar.ts idiom); PATCH accepts `status` and `keep` together, neither → 400 `empty_patch`.
- **D100** (GR.2): **five divergences from the plan's Task-2 text, four additive and one a straight disagreement; nothing in its checklist was skipped.** **(a) `extractUserCell` shipped as pure `parseUserCell` in a NEW `extension/src/shared/userCell.ts`, not in `harvester.ts`.** The plan puts it in harvester.ts and then asks for happy-dom fixture tests of it — but §7.27 and NT.1's `shared/notifications.ts` already answer that shape: the scroll engine is browser-verified by convention, the DOM *parse* is the half that silently rots when X renames a testid, so it lives in `shared/` where it is testable and gets inlined into the IIFE anyway. Cost: a 5th file. `handleFromAvatarTestid` is exported from it and reused by the harvester's logged-in probe, so the `UserAvatar-Container-` suffix rule exists once. **(b) `runHarvest`'s `toIngest` param became `ship(profile, store, outcome)`.** The plan says "reuse the `runHarvest<R>` generic", but that generic hardcoded `shipToStratus` → `/x/harvest/*` with a `HarvestIngestRow[]`. Swapping the one param (arity unchanged) keeps posts/replies byte-identical while letting following mode own its own transport AND see how the run ended. The mode ternary moved into a `startRun(opts, emit, shouldCancel)` function — a 3-way nested ternary in the port handler was the alternative. **(c) `complete` is computed, never assumed.** The plan's "final batch `done: true` only when the scroll hit natural bottom, not on cancel/max" needed a fact the loop didn't record: **five** exits existed (cancel, row cap, day window, `HARD_STEP_CAP`, stable-height bottom) and only the last means "I saw everything". New `reachedBottom` flag set at that break; `complete = reachedBottom && !cancelled`. **(d) NEW: the not-my-list guard the plan doesn't mention.** A complete run over SOMEONE ELSE's `/following` would insert their followees as mine and flip my entire real ledger to `gone` in one txn — the exact failure D99(b) built the zero-row guard against, reachable here by nothing more than a stale handle in the input. Two layers: the panel targets `ctx.handle` of the OPEN following page (the handle input is frozen, never navigated away from), and `loggedInHandle()` (left-nav account switcher's `UserAvatar-Container-` suffix) aborts with `not_own_following` — **on a positive mismatch only**, since a null read is "unknown" (§7.11) and refusing a legitimate run over a collapsed sidebar is worse than the risk it removes. **(e) The Following mode chip is NOT disabled off the context probe.** The plan says "enabled only when the context probe reports `onFollowing`", but `HarvestForm` persists the mode, so a restore on a non-following page would render the chip **selected and disabled** — which reads as a broken panel and explains nothing. Shipped: the chip always selects, **Start** is what's blocked, and a warn line says exactly what to open. Same safety, discoverable.
- **D100a** (GR.2, calls the plan doesn't pin down): **(a) `followsBack` is sticky-true and `displayName` fill-only WITHIN a run, `listPosition` is first-sighting.** The list virtualizes, so the same handle is re-parsed on later screens and a cell's DOM index restarts every screen. Presence of the badge is evidence, absence on a re-render is more likely a paint gap than a same-minute unfollow — GR.1's own asymmetry at DOM scale. Across runs nothing is sticky: the server always takes the newest batch's value, so a real unfollow lands on the next scrape. **(b) `parseUserCell` requires ONLY the handle.** Every optional field is null-tolerant on purpose: a row dropped for a missing display name would count as *unseen* to a complete run and be marked `gone`. Anything that makes the scrape MISS a row is the expensive failure here, not a stale name. **(c) Display name = first *innermost* `[dir]` block that is neither `@…` nor the badge's own text.** Skipping blocks that wrap another `[dir]` is what stops a container from reading as "Alice @alice Follows you"; comparing against the badge's OWN text (never a hardcoded "Follows you") is what keeps it locale-proof, and is fixture-tested with a Romanian badge. **(d) Batch size 200, not the server's 500 cap** — matches the file's existing `INGEST_CHUNK` and keeps a failed batch small; a mid-run failure simply leaves the run open (never `complete`), so the ledger is untouched. **(e) `harvestTargetUrl`/`isAtTarget` now share a private `targetPath(handle, mode)`** — they duplicated the path spelling, which is how a new mode navigates somewhere its own already-there check disagrees with (an infinite re-navigate). **(f) `HarvestIngest`'s success arm gained optional `followsBack`/`complete`** rather than a new event field; both are absent for posts/replies, so the panel's existing rendering is untouched and an older panel just shows less.
- **D101** (GR.3): **three calls the plan's Task-3 text left open, all resolved toward "the window cap governs NEW releases; already-queued rows survive until ticked".** **(a) The batch tops up to the budget, it does not stack on it.** `releasedNow = max(0, budget − held.length)`, so `batch = held + released` never exceeds `budget` by new releases and a re-read with `held ≥ budget` releases nothing — that IS the idempotence the plan asked for. The plan's phrase "already-queued rows consume no new budget" is about the *budget number* (`releaseBudget` counts `unfollow_marked_at`, i.e. completed unfollows, never releases — a queued-never-done row can't shrink the window), NOT a licence for the batch to grow unbounded across reads. Got this backwards and every read would pile another 15–18 onto the batch until the user started ticking. **(b) `eligibleTotal = held.length + eligible.length`** — the full manual-unfollow backlog (everyone released + everyone still eligible), deliberately stable across reads because a released row just moves from the `eligible` pool into `held`. NOT "active-eligible only", which would jump down by `releasedNow` on the first read and confuse the UI's backlog line. **(c) `keep` is enforced as a COLUMN inside the pure fns, never added to the whitelist set.** The read-time whitelist is only people at stage mutual/ally (non-retired) + `loadTargetHandles()`; `keep` and `followsBack` live on the `following` row, so `eligibleForUnfollow`/`reviewQueued` check them directly. A `queued` row that becomes `keep`/mutual/ally/follows-back **after** release is **revoked** `queued→active` in the same read (GR.1 hand-off #1: kept rows must be filtered out of the re-listed queued batch, not only at first release). **Also (not a deviation, a trap for the next test author):** the marks query is GLOBAL over `following.unfollow_marked_at` (trailing 24h), so the route suite `db.delete(following)` first — the GR.1 lifecycle fixture leaves recent marks on CAROL/EVE that would otherwise perturb the jittered budget assertions.
- **D102** (GR.4): **four divergences from the plan's Task-4 text, three of them additive.** **(a) `styles.css` is a fourth file.** The plan's edit list is api.ts + People.tsx (docs deferred to Task 10), but the batch row is a flex row with a right-anchored age + two action buttons and nothing existing matched — `.fan-row` is the closest and naming an unfollow queue after Top Fans would mislead the next reader more than 30 lines of CSS cost. Shipped as a small `.following-*` block (`-list`/`-row`/`-handle`/`-meta`/`-mutual`), tokens only, D7-compliant, reusing `.person-link`/`.stage-chip`/`.status-line`/`.warn`/`.people-search`/`.radar-tabs` for everything else. **(b) `SubTabs`, not a copied Voice `radar-tab` block.** The plan says "imitate the Voice.tsx Tweets|Pillars subtab"; UI.10's `SubTabs` IS the formalized version of that control and D7/D81a already settled the question for new surfaces. Voice itself migrates at UI.14. **(c) The ledger drawer got a status filter and a two-way keep toggle**, not just "collapsed ledger search". `keep` is otherwise a one-way door from the panel — the batch's own toggle can only pin — and a pinned person would be unreachable without SQL. The toggle reloads the QUEUE as well as the list, because un-keeping re-opens eligibility. **(d) Batch rows carry both a profile anchor and a dossier button.** The plan wants the profile link (that is where the unfollow happens) *and* keeps the dossier click-through; one row can't be both, so the handle is the external anchor (the action) and a small `file` button opens the dossier. **Also (not a deviation, a trap):** `windowCap` is re-drawn from 15–18 by the server on every read (jitter, D101), so the budget line legitimately changes between refreshes — don't "fix" it by caching the first value.
- **D103** (GR.5): **four calls the plan's Task-5 text left open, plus one it got structurally wrong about the test bench.** **(a) One alert per rule.** Design §B gives `postBurst` two sub-conditions (>4 originals/24h; any two under 20 min apart) and `nearDuplicate`/`scheduleCluster` can each match many pairs — a literal reading emits several rows carrying the same `rule`. Shipped: every rule returns `MonitorAlert | null`, so `alerts` is keyable by rule and the Today card (GR.6) can never render `postBurst` twice; postBurst's message names whichever sub-condition(s) fired, and the multi-match rules carry `pairs` (capped at `MAX_LISTED_PAIRS`=5) **alongside the full `pairCount`**, so the cap can't read as "that was all of them". **(b) One originals query, not two.** The plan's loader list says "trailing 24h originals" AND "trailing 14d original texts". Shipped as one 14-day select carrying `postedAt`, with `postBurst` narrowing its own window — which also puts `POST_BURST_WINDOW_MS` inside the pure module where a test can reach it instead of inside the SQL where it can't. **(c) `replyBurst` measures the DENSEST hour inside a 3-hour lookback, not the trailing hour.** The plan's rule says ">10 in any trailing 60 min" while its loader says "trailing 3h posted reply times" — the sliding read is the only interpretation that uses both numbers, and it is the better one: a burst that ended 90 minutes ago is still exactly what X saw, so the alert keeps a tail instead of vanishing the moment the hour rolls over. `REPLY_BURST_LOOKBACK_MS` lives in the pure module and the route imports it, so the two can't drift. **(d) The churn WINDOW is imported too, not just the ceiling.** The plan (and the GR.3 hand-off) only pin `DAILY_CEILING`; `UNFOLLOW_CHURN_WINDOW_MS` **is** `connections.ts`'s `MARK_LOOKBACK_MS`, because a monitor that agreed with the queue about "40/day" but not about *which* 24 hours would fire on cadence the queue itself had permitted. **(e) "Route returns empty alerts on empty DB" is unassertable here** — every route suite shares one in-memory DB and other suites' fixtures legitimately sit inside these windows. The route test is baseline-relative instead (see the gotcha). **Also (not a deviation, a judgement recorded so a later pass doesn't "fix" it):** §7.19's sample gates are deliberately NOT applied. A gate stops a thin sample being read as a statistic about the audience; "I pasted 14 replies in an hour" is a count of what I did and is true at n=14. There is a comment in the module saying so.
- **D104** (GR.6): **the schedule-time duplicate check also scans other PENDING rows, which the plan's Task-6 text does not ask for.** The plan says "near-duplicate-of-recent-post (14d, same Jaccard)" — published originals only. But the cluster half of the same advisory already reads the pending set, so the texts come for free, and the asymmetry it would otherwise leave is the expensive one: scheduling the same post twice produces **no** warning at schedule time (neither is published yet) and then fires the monitor's `nearDuplicate` after both have gone out — i.e. exactly when it can no longer be fixed. Shipped as a **second, separately-worded** warning (`Very similar to another post already queued (…)` vs `Very similar to a post from N days ago (…)`) so the two situations never read as one. Strictly additive, cannot refuse anything.
- **D105** (GR.6): **the advisory is POST-only and `pending`-only, and both bounds are deliberate.** (a) `PATCH /posts/scheduled/:id` returns no `warnings`, so the Composer's **edit** path — including promoting a draft to pending, which is how `submitSingle` saves an existing row — is silent. The plan scopes the field to the POST response and the URL guard has the same POST/PATCH asymmetry in spirit (both re-check, only one is in the plan); extending it is a clean follow-up for whoever next owns `calendar.ts`, not a bug to discover twice. (b) A `draft` gets `warnings: []` because nothing is scheduled to happen yet — same reading as the URL guard, which also only fires on `pending`. This also keeps the MCP `x_draft_post` path (status hard-coded `draft`) doing zero extra reads. (c) `POST /posts/threads` returns no warnings either — untouched by this task.
- **D106** (GR.7): **the plan's `POST/PATCH/DELETE /x/goals` were not built — goal writes stay on `/x/me/goals`.** Task 7's edit list gives the new router full CRUD, which made sense when it owned its own `goals` table; under D4 it would have been a SECOND writer over `me_goals` with a second validation ladder, and the two would drift the first time one of them learned a new field. Shipped instead: `routes/goals.ts` owns only the derived half (current value, pacing, the lazy flip) plus the genuinely-new `commitments` table, and `POST /x/me/goals` gained baseline stamping through the exported `stampBaseline(kind, currentValue, now)`. Consequence for **GR.8**: the Today card's inline add form POSTs to `/x/me/goals`, and "abandon" is `PATCH /x/me/goals/:id {status:'dropped'}` (the ME.1 word — the plan's `abandoned` does not exist; `missed` was added, `dropped` was kept). The plan's `note` column was also dropped: `label` already carries the human sentence, and an unused column in a table the drafting prompt reads is worse than none.
- **D107** (GR.7): **the counted kinds are excluded from the injected me-block but included in the digest facts.** ME.1 renders every active goal into the prompt tail; a `posted_replies` goal there would put "I need 290 more replies" into a drafting prompt, which is process, not biography, and is exactly the kind of number that colours a draft badly. `renderFor` in `routes/me.ts` filters `isFlowGoalKind` before rendering — the pure renderers are untouched, so every ME.1 prompt test still holds. `routes/digest.ts` does the opposite (it resolves them through `loadFlowCurrents`), because a week in review is precisely where a reply quota belongs. If a later task adds a third consumer of `resolveGoals`, it has to make this same call explicitly — there is no default.
- **D108** (GR.7, three smaller calls the plan left open): **(a) `commitments.active_since` is a column the plan does not list.** Its DDL sketch has only `updated_at`, but `commitmentDebt` needs to know which days were on the hook, and using `updated_at` would silently forgive every missed day the moment the target was edited. Stamped at creation and on a false→true re-activation only. **(b) The debt window ends YESTERDAY.** Today is still in progress, so it can never be a miss — `trackedLast7` is the honest denominator (0 for a promise made today, which is why a fresh commitment reads `{0,0,0,tier 0}`). **(c) `verdict` is a 6-union, not the plan's 5.** `unknown` covers both "no current value" (§7.11) and "no deadline" — a goal with no date has no pace to be behind, and grading it 'behind' would be a claim the data doesn't support. Same discipline as HV.5's `'unknown'` band (D96c); `VERDICT_SCORE.unknown = null` keeps it out of the scorecard rather than scoring it 0.
- **D109** (GR.8): **the Today Goals card ships WITHOUT the plan's inline add form.** Task 8 asks for "inline add form: metric/target/deadline" and, three lines later, for "an empty goals list renders nothing (the PinnedWatchCard only-when-nudge discipline)" — the two together make the form reachable ONLY once a goal already exists, i.e. exactly when the user has already found the Me tab's full goal form (label/kind/target/unit/deadline). That is a second form to keep in sync with `GoalKind` for zero discoverability gain, so the gate won and the form was dropped: goal creation stays in the Me tab, which D106 already made the single writer. What DID ship is the plan's other write — a confirm-guarded `drop` → `PATCH /x/me/goals/:id {status:'dropped'}` (the ME.1 word; the plan's `abandoned` does not exist) — because a goal you have stopped chasing must not keep grading your week, and GR.9's scorecard reads exactly the active set. **If UI.12 ever wants creation on Today, it should lift `Me.tsx`'s `AddGoal`, not write a second one.**
- **D110** (GR.8): **`api.ts` gained `commitments.{get,put}` only — no `goals.*`.** The plan's edit list names both. But the Today card reads goals off the brief payload it already fetches and writes through the existing `api.me.patchGoal`, so a `goals.get` would be a second, unused way to fetch rows the panel already has (CLAUDE.md working style: don't pre-stub API surface). `GET /x/goals` itself is still live and still the MCP tool's path — only the panel client omits it. A later surface that needs goals outside a brief adds the method then.
- **D111** (GR.8): **`replyQuota.target` stays the doctrine band; only the QUEST target moves.** The plan names `repliesTarget` (the `computeQuests` input) and nothing else, and the two numbers mean different things: the band is doctrine's sustainable 10–20/day range (the sibling of `week.targetReplyPct`, read by the Replies card and by N.5's whole doctrine story), while a commitment is a personal daily minimum. Letting a commitment of 25 rewrite the band would silently restate doctrine from a number set for another purpose. Consequence the panel accepts on purpose: with an active commitment, Today's quest line ("17 quality replies") and the Replies card ("N / 10–20 today") legitimately disagree.
- **D112** (GR.9): **four divergences from Task 9's text, one of them the whole shape of the change.** **(a) The scorecard is COMPUTED inside `buildDigestFacts`, not passed through it.** The plan's edit list reads "`DigestFactInputs`/`DigestFacts` gain nullable `scorecard`" — the `goals`/`rosterCoverage` passthrough shape, with the route doing the computing. But four of `computeScorecard`'s nine inputs (`daysTracked`, `daysAllDone`, `repliesPosted`, `replyPct`) are numbers `buildDigestFacts` **already derives** from `streakDays`/`tweets`, so a route-side computation is a second count of the same rows — which is precisely how a grade drifts from the facts it is a grade of. The precedent is one line inside the same function: `conversion.rate` calls the pure `conversionRate(profileClicks, followerDelta)` on numbers just derived. Shipped: the pure layer calls `computeScorecard` itself, and the route passes only what rows can't say as `scorecardInputs` (`daysWithOriginal`, `daysInWeek`, `repliesTargetWeek`, `targetReplyPct`, `goalVerdicts`, `prevScore`). **(b) The WHOLE card is null under the gate, not just the score.** `computeScorecard` returns `{score: null, components: {…five 0–100 numbers…}, sufficient: false}` — put that object in the facts JSON and the only thing between Grok and "your quests scored 50 this week" is a `sufficient: false` flag it has no instruction about, i.e. exactly the confident-grade-over-thin-data the gate exists to prevent. Shipped: `card.sufficient && card.score !== null ? {...} : null`, which hands the existing no-fabrication rule ("if a fact is null … silently skip it") the whole job. `computeScorecard` still owns the gate — the digest just never publishes an ungradeable week. Cost accepted: the panel cannot say "3 days tracked, no grade"; it renders nothing (the PinnedWatchCard/GoalsCard only-when-there's-something discipline). **(c) `daysInWeek`, never the plan's `/7` and `×7`.** GR.7 gave `ScorecardInputs.daysInWeek` the meaning "7, or fewer for the current partial week"; grading a Wednesday read against a full week's reply target would report every mid-week digest as behind. Related: the plan's `REPLY_TARGET.min` **no longer exists** (deleted at N.5) — the floor is `doctrine.replyTargetMin`, and an ACTIVE `replies` commitment outranks it exactly as in GR.8's quests (D111 still holds — no band was touched). **(d) The Digest card is `extension/src/sidepanel/Digest.tsx`, not `Today.tsx`** (Today only hosts `<DigestSection/>`; it was not touched), and the file count is 8, not 4 — the extra four are the standing D102a extension shape: the `shared/types.ts` mirror, the `api.ts` type re-export, the component, one small CSS block.
- **D98** (HV.6): **three places the plan's Task-6 text and the current repo disagree, all resolved in the repo's favour.** **(a) The phase entry went to `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md`, not CLAUDE.md** — Task 6 literally says "CLAUDE.md: one phase-style entry", but D93a moved the whole ledger out of CLAUDE.md on 2026-07-23; CLAUDE.md now changes only when a guardrail changes, and Harvest 2.0 changed none. **(b) `PLAN.md` got a top blockquote, not a §"Phased build" edit.** The plan said "PLAN.md status updated"; §"Phased build" is the frozen Phases-1–10 ledger, and every post-Phase-10 phase since Niche N0 has landed as a `>` blockquote in the stack above §"Product, in one paragraph each" (RU.10 is the exemplar). Same place, same style, `See docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md §"Harvest 2.0"` instead of RU.10's `See CLAUDE.md §…`. **(c) The smoke does NOT "delete every seeded run/row on exit" literally.** It cannot: `POST /harvest/passive` find-or-creates *today's* passive run, which on a live box is the user's real one holding real browsing rows. Deleting it would destroy live data. Shipped instead as: every written row carries an **888-prefixed tweet id** (no real snowflake starts there) and every directly-seeded run carries an **`hv6_*` handle** (no real harvest produces one), so cleanup's `like`/`inArray` are exact; a run the route *created* for the smoke is deleted, a **pre-existing** run is kept with its `row_count` **recomputed from the surviving rows** (self-healing after an aborted run — better than restoring a remembered number). Any future smoke that writes into a table live browsing also writes must copy this shape.

### Gotchas HV.1 → GR.9

- **HV.1: `POST /x/harvest/passive` is live and Wave 3 is open — read this before HV.2/HV.3/HV.4 touch the harvest lane.** The route is server-only, **$0**, and adds **no column and no migration** (see D92 — the journal is still `0018` and FREE). Shape the rest of the phase must build against: the extension posts `{rows: HarvestIngestRow[]}` (**≤100/batch**, validated by the *existing* `parseIngestRow`, so the content script must ship the same row shape the active harvester already ships) and always gets back **`201 {runId, inserted, skippedRecent, skippedCap}`** — HV.2's flush should treat a non-201 as warn-and-drop and must NOT retry (a dropped sighting is a missing longitudinal point, never a lost user action). Four in-file constants are the contract and all four are **opening guesses**: `MAX_PASSIVE_BATCH=100`, `PASSIVE_DAILY_CAP=2000`, `PASSIVE_RECAPTURE_MS=30min`, `PASSIVE_RETENTION_DAYS=60`. The client's per-tweet resend throttle should mirror the server's 30 min so the wire isn't full of rows that will only be counted as `skippedRecent`. **Three things that will bite a later task:** (1) `MODES` is still `['posts','replies']` **on purpose** — `POST /harvest/runs` 400s `mode:'timeline'`, so nothing client-side may try to create a passive run (decision 7, asserted in the suite); (2) **`mode` is the ONLY discriminator** between ambient rows and hand-run harvests, and today the only other reader (`scripts/backfill-people.ts`) filters `mode='replies'` — **HV.4's affinity SQL and HV.5's funnel MUST filter `mode='timeline'`**, and any *future* query over `harvest_rows` has to declare a mode or it will silently mix corpora; (3) **band is never stored** (§7.12) — HV.5 recomputes it from `views`/`comments`/`tweet_time`/`captured_at`/`text` through the shared `replyBand.ts`, which is why capturing `tweetTime` matters (a null `tweet_time` makes `ageMin` and therefore vpm underivable, so the row can only ever land in an unknown bucket).
- **HV.1 test-suite traps for anyone editing `src/x/routes/harvest.test.ts`** (the file's first route suite; the pure `parseIngestRow`/`matchUnlinkedDraft` tests stay in `src/test.test.ts`): there is exactly **one passive run per UTC day**, so tests share it and **order is load-bearing** — the cap test forces `rowCount` to 1999 and **restores it** (`before + 1`) or every later test in the file gets capped to zero inserts; the prune test seeds its stale rows *after* the earlier posts because prune fires on **every** passive request; and each test needs **fresh tweet ids** (`77000xxx` here) or the 30-min recapture gate silently turns an expected insert into a `skippedRecent`. `afterAll` deletes every `mode='timeline'` run + rows plus the one seeded `posts`-mode keeper — no other suite touches these tables today, so that is exact. Suite total moved **1130 → 1139**.
- **HV.2: the passive tap is OPEN — x.com/home now writes to `harvest_rows` while you scroll, and HV.3/4/5 finally have a corpus to read.** One line in `applyBand` (`if (sig) recordPassiveHarvest(article)`) drives it, for **every band including `skip`** — HV.5's funnel needs the denominator, and ads/promoted rows carry no metrics label so `sig === null` filters them for free. **What a later task must not casually change:** (1) **gate order is the perf contract** (D93c) — enabled → `/home` → `!isHarvestActive()` → `findPermalink` → pending/throttle, and only THEN `extractArticle`; the hook runs per-article per-mutation-burst, so the throttle is the hot path after a tweet's first sighting; (2) `isHomeTimelinePath(location.pathname)` is re-read **per call**, never resolved once at `start()` — X navigates without reloading (same reason NT.5's `onNotificationsPage()` is per-scan); (3) the flush is **warn-and-drop with NO retry** (a lost sighting is a missing point on a view curve, not a lost user action) and it ignores `unconfigured`; (4) `time: x.time || null` in `toPassiveIngestRow` — shipping `''` would be an invalid_row_time 400, and a null `tweet_time` strands the row in HV.5's unknown bucket, so an article whose `<time>` didn't render is worth less than it looks. **Three constants are opening guesses** and they mirror the server's, not each other's: `PASSIVE_RESEND_MS=30min` ↔ server `PASSIVE_RECAPTURE_MS`, `PASSIVE_BATCH_MAX=100` ↔ server `MAX_PASSIVE_BATCH`; change one side and the wire fills with rows the server only counts as `skippedRecent`. `PASSIVE_FLUSH_MS=5000` and the >5000 `passiveSentAt` reset are client-only. **The toggle is a SECOND key** — `passiveHarvest`, not C6's `passiveCapture` (one grows the people roster from hover cards, one grows the corpus from the timeline); both are default-ON/opt-out with `absent key = enabled`, and both are read directly by content.ts as well as through `sidepanel/storage.ts`. Adding it to the `Settings` interface forced the object literal in `storage.test.ts` — and surfaced that **extension test files are typechecked by NEITHER tsconfig** (root `include` stops at `src/`; `tsconfig.app.json` excludes `src/**/*.test.ts`), so `bun run test` is the only thing that ever executes them and a type error in one is invisible. **Browser-UNVERIFIED by convention** (content.ts is never unit-tested): the live done-when tail is scrolling x.com/home with the unpacked extension and watching POSTs to `/x/harvest/passive` in the network tab + rows landing under one `mode='timeline'` run, toggling the setting off stopping them **without a reload** (the `onChanged` listener), and starting a Harvest-tab run suspending them. Gates: `bun run test` **1147/0** (+8, all in the new pure suite), root+extension typecheck clean, `vite build` green (the IIFE pass proves the inlining — `isHomeTimelinePath` appears as a plain function in `dist/content.js`), `bun run lint` clean. **Docs are deliberately NOT updated** — plan Task 2 defers `docs/settings-tab.md` and the new harvest doc to **HV.6**, which owes: the `passiveHarvest` toggle row, the /home-only scope, the cap/retention/recapture numbers, and (per D93a) its phase entry in **`docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md`, not CLAUDE.md**. **Releases `extension/src/content.ts`, `harvester.ts` and `Settings.tsx`.** The D9 chain now stands at HV.2✓ — **A3.3 (Wave 4) is the last claimant** and nothing before it touches content.ts. HV.3 (Harvest tab options, `Harvest.tsx` + `harvester.ts` — note it re-claims harvester.ts), HV.4 (`GET /harvest/affinity`, re-claims `src/x/routes/harvest.ts`) and HV.5 (playbook lock) are all unblocked and disjoint.
- **HV.3: the Harvest tab is done and the harvester's option surface is now four flags — read this before HV.4/HV.5/HV.6 or any later harvester work.** `HarvestOptions` = `{mode, scope, pace, max?, sendToStratus?, downloadCsv?, minViews?}`; both new flags are **absent-means-on/off-by-default** (`downloadCsv !== false` downloads, an absent/0/NaN `minViews` filters nothing), so an older side panel driving a newer content script behaves exactly as before. **Three things that will bite a later task:** (1) **the min-views filter sits after `noteSeen` and before the store write** — moving it above `noteSeen` would stop filtered items from counting as scrolled-past, which defeats the day-window exhaustion break (`win && ctx.oldestSeenMs < win.startMs`) and makes every scoped run scroll to `HARD_STEP_CAP`; it also reads the harvested item's OWN views (`reply.metrics.views` in replies mode, never `orig`'s); (2) **`done.filename === ''` now means two different things** — zero rows (as before) *or* CSV-off; the panel disambiguates by `rows > 0`, and anything else consuming the event should too; (3) **`passiveRowsToday`'s day boundary is UTC on purpose** and must stay pinned to the server's `utcDayStart` — if HV.4/HV.5 ever add a "today" read over `harvest_rows`, use the same boundary or the two surfaces will disagree for hours a day. The form now lives in **one** `chrome.storage.local` key `harvestForm` (mode/scope/pace/maxStr/minViewsStr/downloadCsv, restored through the lenient `parseHarvestForm`) while `harvestSendToStratus` deliberately keeps its own legacy slot; the restore→mirror pair is guarded by a `formLoaded` flag, so **don't add a form field to the write effect's deps without also adding it to the read** or the first render will stamp a default over stored state. Start is blocked with a warn line when CSV **and** send-to-stratus are both off. The status line renders **nothing** while unloaded or after a failed `GET /x/harvest/runs` (it never claims 0) — the `limit:20` lookback is the route's own default and could in principle miss today's passive run behind 20+ same-day hand harvests; raise the limit if that ever happens rather than filtering server-side. **Browser-UNVERIFIED** (panel React is untested by convention): the live tail is a CSV-off DB-only run landing rows with no download, `min views 1000` thinning both the CSV and the ingest, the form surviving a panel close, and the status line matching what `x.com/home` scrolling actually captured. Gates: `bun run test` **1162/0** (+15), root + extension typecheck clean, `vite build` green (`ApiError` verified **absent** from `dist/content.js` — the new `import type { HarvestRun }` in `shared/harvest.ts` is fully erased), whole-repo `bun run lint` clean. **HV.4 and HV.5 remain unblocked and disjoint; HV.6 owes the docs** — a new/updated harvest doc covering the four options + the passive tap, `docs/settings-tab.md`'s `passiveHarvest` row (deferred from HV.2), and (per D93a) its phase entry in **`docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md`, not CLAUDE.md**.
- **HV.4: the ambient corpus has its first READER — and the `mode='timeline'` rule is now load-bearing in two places, not one.** `GET /x/harvest/affinity?days=&limit=&minDays=` → `{days, minDays, authors:[{handle, distinctDays, sightings, lastSeenAt, avgViews, stage, inRoster}]}`, $0, defaults 30 / 20 / ≥3 — **all four affinity constants are opening guesses** to revisit with HV.1's four (`DEFAULT_AFFINITY_DAYS`, `MIN/MAX_AFFINITY_DAYS` 7–90, `DEFAULT_AFFINITY_LIMIT` 20 / `MAX` 50, `DEFAULT_AFFINITY_MIN_DAYS` 3). **Four things that will bite a later task:** (1) the UTC day bucket is `strftime('%Y-%m-%d', captured_at/1000, 'unixepoch')` — the THIRD place the UTC-day boundary is written (server `utcDayStart`, panel `passiveRowsToday`, now this); any fourth reader must use the same one or two surfaces disagree for hours a day; (2) the `people`/`voice_authors` join runs **in TS off the ≤`limit` handles after the group-by**, with an early return when the group-by is empty — never let it reach drizzle as `in ()` (the NT.6/D90a rule); (3) `intParam` is now shared by three params and is the place to add a fourth — its contract is clamp-on-range, **400-on-garbage**, and the 400 is deliberate; (4) the route is still the only reader that isn't `mode='timeline'`-aware by accident — **HV.5's funnel must carry the same filter**, and `scripts/backfill-people.ts` (`mode='replies'`) remains the only other consumer. **Test-suite trap:** the affinity describe must stay **LAST** in `src/x/routes/harvest.test.ts` — the ingest describe's first test asserts **zero** `mode='timeline'` runs exist, and the affinity `beforeAll` creates one. Its rows are seeded **directly** (a multi-day history is unbuildable through the 30-min recapture gate) under a run created **now** with **backdated rows**, so the 60-day prune on the passive path can't delete them mid-suite; `afterAll` now also deletes the seeded `people` + `voice_authors` rows (shared in-memory DB). **Panel side is deliberately minimal:** a `Section`+`EmptyState` drawer under the People roster, **collapsed and unfetched until clicked** (one manual Refresh, no polling), and each row calls the SAME `setSelected` the roster uses — so an unknown handle rides the existing `NotYetKnown` "start the file" path. **No new message type, no storage key, no CSS rule** (it reuses `.people-list`/`.people-row-main`/`.people-counts`/`.people-ago`/`.stage-chip`). **Browser-UNVERIFIED** (panel React is untested by convention): the live tail is opening People → Show and seeing real handles from actual x.com/home scrolling, with a genuinely unknown one clicking through to the create-file screen. - **HV.5: the ambient corpus now has TWO readers and the funnel is the one that needs `skip` rows to exist.** `GET /x/playbook` → `timelineFunnel: {cells, totalSeen, totalReplied}`, 30-day window, `mode='timeline'`, $0, **no schema/migration/CSS/api.ts change**. **Five things that will bite a later task:** (1) **HV.2 capturing every band including `skip` is load-bearing here** — the funnel's denominator IS "tweets the algorithm showed me"; if anyone ever "optimizes" passive capture to only store hot/warm rows, this cell silently becomes `replied/replied = 100%`. (2) The first-sighting rule is enforced in TWO places on purpose — `min(captured_at)` with bare columns in `loadTimelineFunnel` (SQLite's documented behavior: with exactly ONE min/max aggregate, every bare column comes from the matching input row — add a second aggregate like `max(views)` to that select and the guarantee evaporates) **and** an earliest-`capturedAtMs` dedupe inside the pure `buildTimelineFunnel`, so the pure fn is correct on any row set. (3) `'unknown'` ≠ `null` band (D96c) — and `unknown` is the bucket that grows if `content.ts` ever ships `time: ''`/omits it (HV.2 gotcha 4), so a sudden unknown spike is a capture regression, not a timeline change. (4) The window is **30d while retention is 60d** and the select is **unlimited** — deliberate, because HV.1's 2,000/day cap bounds it structurally (≤60k rows) and a `limit` would truncate the denominator without saying so. If the daily cap is ever raised, revisit the limit BEFORE the window. (5) `replied` reads posted `reply_drafts.sourceTweetId` (unwindowed; the ∩ with seen ids bounds it) — **canned replies and hand-typed replies leave no draft row**, so the capture rate reads LOW by construction. Don't "fix" it by widening to `posts_published.in_reply_to_tweet_id` without a decision: the plan's own risk note says refine the population in a follow-up, not pre-filter now. **Test-suite traps:** the funnel describe must stay **LAST** in `src/x/routes/playbook.test.ts` (it seeds a posted `reply_drafts` row, which moves the earlier describes' angle/latency/model/batch cells) and it must delete its `mode='timeline'` run + rows in `afterAll` — `src/x/routes/harvest.test.ts` asserts that **zero** timeline runs exist. Multi-sighting histories are seeded **directly**, not through `POST /harvest/passive` (its 30-min recapture gate makes them unbuildable). **Browser-UNVERIFIED** (panel React is untested by convention): the live tail is the Playbook tab showing a real seen/replied split after some days of x.com/home scrolling — and note **every cell will read `insufficient data` until 20 tweets land in a band**, which is hours of scrolling, not minutes. (2) **The `unknown`-band assertion is the funnel's canary** — the smoke seeds 6 rows with `tweetTime: null` on purpose and asserts a `≥6` `unknown` cell; if a future capture change starts shipping `time: ''` the smoke keeps passing but the *live* unknown cell balloons, which is the documented capture-regression signal (`docs/playbook-tab.md` says so in user words). (3) **`parseUserCell` returning null means "nothing seen here", never "a row with gaps"** — a dropped handle reads as *unseen* to a complete run and gets marked `gone`, so every optional field is null-tolerant and only the handle is required; within a run `followsBack` is sticky-true and `displayName` fill-only (D100a). (4) **The not-my-list guard is `loggedInHandle()`** in harvester.ts, off `[data-testid="SideNav_AccountSwitcher_Button"] [data-testid^="UserAvatar-Container-"]` — it fires `not_own_following` on a POSITIVE mismatch only, and the panel additionally targets the OPEN page's handle rather than the (frozen) handle input. Both exist because a complete run over someone else's list would insert their followees and flip my whole ledger to `gone`.
