### deviations D139 → D134 — moved verbatim from STATE.md lines 108–113

- **D139** (UI.15): **two plan instructions that were wrong at the pixel level, two that had no target, and the effect the plan did not anticipate.** **(a) "Every 'insufficient data (n=…)' becomes the standard `EmptyState`" cannot be done as written, because those are two different objects.** `insufficient data (n=7)` is a **table CELL** (`cellSummary` returns a string rendered inside a `<td>`); `EmptyState` is a block with two paragraphs. Putting one in a `<td>` destroys the table. So the instruction was split into the two treatments it was conflating: a whole empty SECTION becomes an `EmptyState` with a coach hint (eight of them — the UI.12/UI.14 precedent exactly), and a gated CELL gets a compact self-explaining form in the new `.pb-gated` tone. **(b) The plan's suggested cell copy was rejected on measurement, and the replacement is better anyway.** `"needs n≥20 — currently 7 · lower the gate in Settings"` is ~50 chars; the angle table is three columns in a ~400 px panel, and the band table is six — it would wrap to three lines per cell, twelve times per table, repeating the same affordance twelve times. `insufficient data (n=7/20)` is 26 chars, **keeps the tab's signature phrase** (which `docs/playbook-tab.md`'s entire "single most important thing" section is built on, and which the Wave-3 carry-forward gotcha lists as asserted user-visible copy) and still names the gate — plus it now says how FAR off the cell is, which the plan's version doesn't. The "lower the gate" affordance lives once, in the ⚙'s note. **(c) It fixed a four-phase-old latent bug the plan didn't know about:** `cellSummary(c: PlaybookCell, _minN: number)` had *ignored* its gate parameter since C4 — the underscore was in the source. Harmless while 20 was baked; the moment a gear moves it, a cell saying "insufficient" without saying insufficient-for-what is silent about the very number the user is watching change (UI.13(e)'s lesson, one level worse: the copy didn't quote the stale number, it quoted nothing). `ResultCell` replaced the string-returning helper so the class could be applied too. **(d) The plan's "minN stepper" needed an effect the plan never mentions.** `x.gates.minCellN` is `server`-scoped, so unlike every mirrored knob the polish passes have wired, moving it changes the RESPONSE, not the render — every `sufficient` flag was computed server-side at `minN`. A gear alone would persist the number and leave the page unchanged, which reads as a broken gear. Hence the re-read effect, and hence its **ref guard**: without `reloadedForGate` a value the registry bounds refuse (5–100) leaves gear and payload permanently disagreeing, and the effect re-fires on every `data` change — a poll. Bounded to one re-read per distinct value, it self-corrects either way, because the editor re-reads on refusal and snaps the gear back. Safe only because `GET /x/playbook` is $0 and writes nothing — four reads in this repo WRITE and could not carry this pattern. Generalized into codemap §7. **(e) Two plan items had no target.** "Replies: outcome rows tabular" — Replies has no outcome table; it has a day-grouped history of CARDS, each carrying two multi-line text blocks (source tweet + reply), which is unreadable as a table at panel width. Delivered as the chip family + the status filter as pills instead. And "pace radio as SubTabs-style pills" describes a control that was a `<select>`, not a radio — while the two rows the plan didn't mention (mode, date range) were the actual hand-rolled button groups. All three became the primitive, which is the real win: the same control had been spelled three different ways on one screen. **(f) `SubTabs` gained `disabled`** — not in the plan, but Harvest freezes its entire form during a run and the primitive had no way to express it. The `.ui-subtab:hover:not(:disabled)` rule has anticipated the prop since UI.10, which is decent evidence it was always intended. **(g) Three deliberate non-migrations:** `ReplyLists.tsx` (an RL-lane file that merely renders inside this tab), `studio/fields.tsx` (ST.2's field components, ~400 lines, out of Task 15's "KitEditor into Sections" scope), and the Studio template gallery, whose pills carry a label AND a pixel size — two lines a segmented pill can't hold. Also NOT adopted: Composer's `counterClass` ramp on the Replies counter, which would have relocated a helper into the hot `composerLogic.ts` to serve a non-goal. **Binds forward:** UI.17 inherits five updated tab docs (`playbook`/`replies`/`harvest`/`s3-studio` + `settings-tab.md`'s gear list at 11) and should demo `smoke-settings.ts`'s $0 consumer with `x.gates.minCellN` → `GET /x/playbook` (one PATCH moves both the `minN` field and a seeded cell's `sufficient` flag; `smoke-playbook.ts` already exercises that knob), keeping the `x.band.*` → `timelineFunnel` demo as the second.
- **D138** (UI.14): **one plan premise that expired, one cross-lane constraint that changed the mechanism, and three scope calls.** **(a) The plan's reason for the chip family is gone; the family is still right.** Task 14 says to unify the stage chips "because this is the chip set the 11 future plans keep reusing (notifications tier chips, augmented-UI person chips)". Those two shipped (NT/AX lanes) and they live in `content.ts`'s `--stratus-*` overlay namespace, which D137c made **permanently** a different palette from the panel — so there is no future consumer of the kind the plan imagined. The real duplication was local and present: `.stage-chip`, `.people-angle-chip` and `.dm-status` were three geometries of the same object, and Ideas was borrowing the scheduled-post `.badge-*` ramp for an idea lifecycle. So the family shipped for that reason instead: one `.chip` shape, five tones, one lookup module. **(b) It is an ALIAS, not the plan's `.chip-stage-*` rename.** `.stage-chip` is rendered by five files — `Radar.tsx`, `Fans.tsx`, `Conversations.tsx` plus the two in scope — and `.radar-tier` extends it. The plan itself marks Tasks 14 and 15 **parallel-ok**, so renaming would have edited UI.15's file mid-lane. `.chip` is therefore declared alongside `.stage-chip`/`.people-angle-chip` in one selector list; new code writes `.chip`, `stageChip()` returns the shared base plus the existing tone alias, and UI.15 can adopt the family without a rename ever happening. **The general rule: a class rendered outside your lane is not yours to rename, however mechanical the diff.** **(c) The plan put the People gear on the roster header; it went on the DOSSIER header.** `x.display.dossierListLen` caps three lists that are all in the dossier and two of which are conditional — a gear on the roster would sit a screen away from every number it moves, and a gear on one of the three would understate its reach. The `note` carries what it does not hold ("the timeline is never capped"), the D134d pattern. **(d) A fourth gear the plan did not ask for, for D135c's reason:** `x.display.voiceListLimit` had no affordance anywhere near the only list that reads it, so Voice's tweet list got a `Section` head to hang one off. Same rule that made UI.12 ship five gears instead of four. **(e) Two things the plan's edit list implies that were NOT done:** Ideas gets no gear (it has no knob), and `.badge-*`/`.radar-band-*`/the GR.8 pacing chip were left out of the family — `styles.css` already carried a GR.8 comment explaining that a CRM stage and a pacing verdict are different vocabularies, and that reasoning covers the pipeline ramp and the band verdicts too. **(f) One primitive fix rode along:** `.ui-subtabs` was `inline-flex` with no wrap. Every consumer through UI.13 had 2–4 tabs so the row always fit; People's stage filter is seven (All + six stages) and would have overflowed a ~400px panel. The deleted `.people-stage-filter` rule was the old hand-rolled row's `flex-wrap: wrap` — the need was already known, it just lived on the class the migration removed. **Binds forward:** UI.15 owns the last done-when-#5 gear (the Playbook `x.gates.minCellN` stepper) and should render its band/status chips through `chips.ts` rather than minting a sixth family; UI.17's docs-sync inherits the five tab docs already rewritten here, and its `smoke-settings.ts` can demonstrate a $0 consumer with any of the three new `x.display.*` caps.
- **D137** (UI.16): **one mechanism swap, one scope addition the plan predates, and the reason the overlay palette is NOT the panel palette.** **(a) `light-dark()` instead of a duplicated light block.** Task 16 says "light overrides under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` and a manual toggle". Written literally that is two copies of the light palette (one in the media query, one under `[data-theme='light']`) in each of two self-contained files — four copies of twelve values, with nothing to keep them in sync. `color-scheme: light dark` + `light-dark(<light>, <dark>)` expresses exactly the same thing once: the media query IS what `color-scheme` consults, and the manual toggle overrides it by stamping `data-theme` (which flips `color-scheme` to a single keyword). Chrome-only surface, and `styles.css` already leans on `color-mix()`, so the modern-CSS bar was set before this task. **(b) UI.16 also owns `public/writer.html`** — A3.13 shipped it after this plan was written, it is a byte-for-byte copy of explorer's palette, and it already shares `stratus_explorer_token`; leaving it dark while the explorer themes would have looked like a bug. It shares `explorer:theme` for the same reason, and the two `:root` blocks are now duplicated **on purpose** (no build step, per §2/codemap) with a comment in each saying so. **(c) The overlays got a token block but NOT the panel's tokens.** `OVERLAY_TOKENS` is its own `--stratus-*` namespace inside the injected sheet: the content script is an IIFE on X's page (§7.26), and X's theme is the user's X choice, not their stratus Appearance setting — importing `--strat-*` would make a badge go light while the timeline stayed black. The values are unchanged (X's companion palette + the band colors); what changed is that they exist once. Alphas were aligned in the move: fills all `.12` (were `.10`/`.12`/`.1`), colored outlines all `.5` (warm was `.55`), muted outlines `.4`, structural dividers `.3` (were `.25`/`.35`) — every delta ≤0.05, i.e. invisible, which is what "keep visuals essentially as-is, just align values" buys. **(d) Three plan items had no target:** the plan's read-first points at `public/explorer.html:1-120` for "its inline palette block" (it is lines 8–13); `scripts/smoke-explorer.ts` needed no change (it asserts `/stratus.*explorer/i` + `/x/data/tables` + `/x/articles`, all untouched — verified green); and the DS `--x-*` reference tokens the plan cites already sit in `styles.css` from UI.8, so the overlay values needed no re-derivation, only naming. **Binds forward:** a new on-page chip family (augmented-x-ui, notifications) adds a tone to `OVERLAY_TOKENS` and references it — a fresh literal in a rule is the drift this task removed; UI.17's docs-sync inherits the two public-page theme paragraphs already written into `docs/s1-data-explorer.md` + `docs/writer.md`; and the **browser-verify for both public pages + the overlay sheet is owed** (see next-up) — the Chrome extension was not connected this session, so the `light-dark()` render and the `font:` shorthand-with-`var()` were verified only by build + a dangling-`var()` audit, not by eye.
- **D136** (UI.13): **four plan-vs-repo calls plus the two bugs the first `numberArray` gear surfaced.** **(a) The gear is FOUR keys on TWO tabs, not three on one.** The plan (and STATE's next-up) scoped it to the ladder — `anchors3`/`anchors4`/`ladderSwitchAt` — on the Composer. `x.gates.bestTimeMinN` joined them because it is mirrored, it is read by the very chips beside the gear (`topCellsForWeekday(…, server.bestTimeMinN)`), and it is the number the plan's own "grey below the mirrored `x.gates.bestTimeMinN`" line is about; and the Calendar carries the SAME gear because its ghost slots ARE that ladder — a user staring at open anchors is exactly who wants to move them. The keys live once, in `composerLogic.ts::CADENCE_SETTING_KEYS`, for the reason the two modules already share `cfg` (a second literal list is how the picker and the board fork). Per-tab, only the `note` differs. **(b) Two primitive bugs, both fixed IN the primitives.** A `numberArray` row commits on BLUR; `GearPopover` closes on outside mousedown, React unmounts the subtree, and **removing a focused input never fires `blur`** — so the anchors someone just typed vanished. The outside-click handler now blurs the focused descendant first. And the gear sits inside the Composer's `<form>`, where **Enter in a text input submits the form** — i.e. saves a post while you are editing a setting — so `NumberArrayInput` swallows Enter with `preventDefault` and commits instead. Neither is Composer-specific; the next gear inherits both. **(c) Three plan items were already true or are no-ops, and were NOT built:** Calendar status badges are already the DS ramp (UI.8 tokenized `.badge-*` to `--strat-status-*`); "thread grouping indent" cannot happen — `POST /posts/thread` writes tails with `scheduledFor: null`, so a segment never lands in a day column; and "cost line as a `Message` variant" names a primitive UI.10 never shipped (the ui/ set is Section/EmptyState/SubTabs/SettingRow/Slider/GearPopover) — minting one to hold a line `.cost-preview`/`.cost-preview-warn` already carries is UI.10's job, not a polish pass's. **(d) Section ORDER unchanged (draft → schedule → drafter).** The plan's "draft → steer/idea → pillar/register → schedule" predates the drafter's position AND `docs/composer-tab.md`, which documents it as sitting *below* the scheduling controls — same call UI.12 made on Today. **(e) One genuine correctness fix rode along:** every "need ≥3 posts in a slot" string now interpolates `server.bestTimeMinN`. The gate has been configurable since UI.6, so the copy has been lying since then — and now that a gear edits it inline, it would have been lying *while the user watched them change it*. A new middle state (`thinCellsForWeekday`) shows measured-but-under-gate hours dimmed with their n, so raising the gate visibly moves a cell from the advice line to the dimmed one. **Binds forward:** UI.15's Playbook-gate gear is the last done-when-#5 gear and inherits (b); UI.14/UI.15 keep using `SettingsGear` + one `useSettingsEditor` per tab; UI.17's `smoke-settings.ts` can demonstrate the $0 consumer with a `x.doctrine.anchors3` PATCH moving `GET /x/brief`'s gaps (the server side of the same key).
- **D135** (UI.12): **four plan-vs-repo calls plus the one refactor the plan forbade by implication.** **(a) The plan's Targets gear tunes two keys that do not exist.** Task 12 says "Targets gear → band multipliers (`x.people.targetBandMin/MaxX`)" — those were never minted; D2/D128a/D129a deliberately kept the 2–10× band niche-owned. So the Targets gear tunes `x.followups.neglectedTargetDays` and its `note` points at General → Niche, the D134d ownership pattern moved next to the feature. **(b) `x.display.targetsNeglectDays` was NOT minted, exactly as the plan's own parenthetical says — but "reuse the followups key" only works if the panel can SEE it, so that key FLIPPED to `mirrored`.** That is the general lesson: a display twin is how the Targets tint and the Do-next queue start disagreeing about the same person, and the cure is to widen the mirror, not to duplicate the number. Same reasoning flipped `x.ai.batchReplyCap`: the plan writes `radarDraftCap ≤ x.ai.batchReplyCap`, which is unenforceable from the panel unless the panel knows the ceiling — so `radarBatchSize(cfg)` clamps, and an oversized batch (which the server REFUSES, never truncates) is now unreachable by clicking. **Mirrored 17 → 23; registry 14 groups / 57 knobs.** **(c) FIVE gears, not the plan's four.** `x.display.fansAmberTopN` is in the plan's own edit list but its section is not in the plan's gear list, which would have left a minted knob with no affordance next to the only feature that reads it. The Fans gear pairs it with `x.followups.fanUnacknowledgedDays` (the server-side half of the same question). **(d) The write discipline was EXTRACTED, not copied.** STATE's UI.11 handover said the gears "must reuse `TuningPanel`'s write discipline, not fork it"; the only way to obey that literally is to move it, so it is now `sidepanel/settingsEditor.ts::useSettingsEditor` and `TuningPanel` consumes it too (behaviour-identical; its two pure halves, `entriesForKeys` + `applyOptimisticValue`, moved to `settingsClient.ts` and got tests). Two rules the INLINE case adds that a single Settings tab never had to state: **one editor per TAB, handed to every gear** (a per-gear editor holds its own registry copy, so an edit in one leaves the others stale until reload), and **`resetKey` cancels a pending debounce rather than flushing it** — dropping the override is the newer intent, so flushing would immediately re-write what the user just cleared. **(e) Scope calls:** `LaunchRoom.tsx` and `ManualPostCard.tsx` were deliberately NOT migrated to `Section` — they are alert CARDS with their own heads (`.launch-room`, `.manual-card`), and an eyebrow would flatten them into the scroll they are supposed to interrupt. `Conversations.tsx` WAS migrated even though the plan's read-first list omits it — it is §6 of the Today doc and one un-migrated section reads as a rendering bug. `.radar-head`/`.radar-actions` were deleted as dead CSS (`Section`'s head is the same layout in tokens). The KPI hero needed no work: `.brief-kpi` was already 26px tabular + delta + sparkline, i.e. the DS KpiCard spec. **Binds forward:** UI.13–UI.15 own the remaining inline gears (done-when #5's Composer ladder + Playbook gate) and **must use `SettingsGear` + one `useSettingsEditor` per tab** — the third implementation of this is the one that drifts; UI.17's `smoke-settings.ts` can now demonstrate a $0 consumer with any `x.display.*` PATCH, and `docs/today-tab.md` gained an UNNUMBERED "Tuning Today from Today" section, so its §1–§18 numbering is still what an inserted card renumbers.
- **D134** (UI.11, discharges D6 + D8): **four plan-vs-repo calls, all resolved in the repo's favour, plus the layout call the plan could not have made.** **(a) Tuning is a fourth SUBTAB, not a section of one long page.** Task 11 describes one scrolling tab — Connection, Appearance, the server groups, toggles, cursors, coming-soon — which was written when the registry was a sketch. It is now **14 groups / 53 knobs**, and AI.10/AI.11 have since added a `General | AI | Prompts` switcher the plan predates. Fifty-three knobs plus Niche plus Commitments plus the connection form in one scroll of a ~400 px side panel is not a cockpit, it is a wall. So the shell became `General | Tuning | AI | Prompts` (on the UI.10 `SubTabs` primitive, replacing the ad-hoc `radar-tabs` divs) and every done-when still holds: ≥12 live groups, search, per-group reset, coming-soon. **(b) D8 discharges to THREE coming-soon entries, not eleven.** Every one of the plan's named eleven (augmented-x-ui, me-profile, niche, notifications, radar-reply-unification, reply-lists, studio-2, ai-layer, authoring-3, guardrails, harvest-enhancements) SHIPPED in Waves 0–4. The genuinely unbuilt plans are `2026-07-22-{static-coach,llm-judge,mika-growth-tactics}.md`, all three marked backlog-after-the-masterplan. A shipped feature advertised as "coming soon" is worse than no roadmap, so the manifest is pruned, not appended — the rule is in the module header and the test pins the id set. **Note the status line is NOT a usable signal:** source plans are never edited (a masterplan hard rule), so every shipped plan still reads `Status: … not started`. The test asserts the id set + that each `planFile` resolves on disk; "is it shipped" stays a human call at prune time. **(c) The plan's "numeric inputs commit on blur/Enter" is too narrow — the real hazard is the slider.** `SettingRow` renders a `Slider` for every bounded number (most of the 53) and it fires `onChange` per drag tick, so a naive wire is a PATCH storm. Writes are therefore **optimistic + debounced 400 ms per key**, with `isDefault` recomputed locally so the reset dot tracks the drag, and the debounce **flushed on unmount** — otherwise switching subtab mid-drag silently drops a knob the user already moved. On a server refusal the row shows the 400 code and the panel **re-reads `GET /x/settings`**: the registry floors/ceilings are the money guard (Decision 5), so a rejected value must never sit on screen looking saved. Generalized into codemap §7. **(d) The tab had to say where the numbers ISN'T.** Three separate tasks (D2/D128a, D129a, and the targetBand drop) deliberately removed niche-owned knobs from `app_settings` so each has one owner — but from the user's chair that reads as "the reply quota is missing". A `GROUP_NOTE` map puts one line at the top of the affected groups pointing at General → Niche, and the same mechanism carries D131a's two-tier AI story (the AI subtab is the global override, `x.ai.*` the per-surface default it falls back to) with a link between them. Ownership that is invisible is indistinguishable from a bug. **(e) Three things Task 11's edit list asks for were already done and were NOT re-done:** `api.settings` + the whole `settingsClient` (UI.10 built `loadSettingGroups`/`patchSetting`/`resetGroup`/`resetKeys`, and UI.6 made every write fire `stratus/settings-sync`), the `restart` chip, and the non-default accent dot — both already in `SettingRow`. `api.ts` was not touched at all; the only addition to `settingsClient.ts` is the pure `filterSettingGroups`. **Binds forward:** UI.12 is the only polish pass that MINTS keys (`x.display.doNextCap`/`doNextSnoozeH`, per D132a) and they will render in Tuning with no extension change; UI.12–UI.15 own the six inline `⚙` gears (done-when #5) and must copy `TuningPanel`'s write discipline rather than inventing a second one; **UI.17's `smoke-settings.ts` is unaffected by the layout** (it drives routes, not the panel) but should still honor D128c/D133e — a `x.band.*` PATCH moving `GET /x/playbook`'s `timelineFunnel` is the $0 consumer demonstration; and the coming-soon manifest is pruned by whichever task ships one of the three backlog plans.

### gotcha blocks (UI.11, UI.16, UI.13, UI.14, UI.15, UI.12) — moved verbatim from STATE.md lines 141–147

- **UI.11 — five facts the polish passes need:** **(1) Biome forbids non-null assertions (`x!`) repo-wide**, including test files — `COMING_SOON.find(...)!` failed `bun run lint` while typecheck was happy. Use a throwing lookup helper (`comingSoon.test.ts`) or `?.` with the assertion on the optional (`settingsClient.test.ts`). Neither typecheck nor `bun test` catches it; only `bun run lint` does. **(2) A knob added to the server registry needs NO extension change to appear in Settings.** Tuning renders whatever `GET /x/settings` returns — label, description, bounds, unit, `appliesOn` all come over the wire. The one hand-sync left is `SettingEntry` in `extension/src/shared/types.ts`, and only if the server's `SettingDef` shape itself gains a field the editor reads. (Adding a `mirrored` key is still the separate 2-file move — that is the blob, not the tab.) **(3) An extension test that reads a repo file must anchor on `import.meta.dir`, not cwd.** `bun run test` runs from the repo root but the same file has to pass if someone runs `bun test` inside `extension/`; `comingSoon.test.ts` resolves `${import.meta.dir}/../../..`. **(4) The settings-editing write discipline is now one place to copy** (`TuningPanel`, codemap §7): optimistic local value → 400 ms debounce per key → flush on unmount → on refusal show the code and re-read the server. The Wave-5 inline gears must reuse it; a second, different discipline is how the panel and the store start disagreeing. **(5) `docs/settings-tab.md` now documents four subtabs and, for the first time, Appearance** (UI.9 shipped it undocumented — a docs debt UI.11 inherited by touching the file, the D113b rule). The "four toggles" count was already correct in two places; what was stale was a "three toggle switches" tip. Anything UI.12–UI.16 adds to Settings updates this doc, not UI.17's backlog.

- **UI.16 — four facts the remaining passes need:** **(1) A CSS custom property is the cheapest audit tool this repo has.** After the move, a 20-line script that diffs "every `--x` defined" against "every `var(--x)` referenced" in a sheet proves both directions at once: no dangling reference (a typo'd token silently renders unstyled — no build error, no test failure) and no dead token (a defined-but-unread value is the same lie as an unregistered knob, cf. UI.12 gotcha 5). All three sheets are now 1:1 (21/21 overlay, 30/30 each public page); a future edit should re-run that check rather than trust the diff. **(2) `var()` inside the `font:` shorthand is legal and used now** (`font: 600 12px/1 var(--stratus-font)` ×8 in `content.ts`) — but a shorthand containing an invalid `var()` throws away the WHOLE declaration, not just the family, so a typo there unstyles the entire button rather than falling back. It is the one construct in that sheet that fails loudly-but-invisibly. **(3) The panel and the overlays are two palettes, permanently.** `--strat-*` = the panel, follows the user's Appearance setting; `--stratus-*` = the injected page overlays, theme-neutral because they sit on X's theme. Same repo, same designer, opposite constraint — an "obvious unification" of the two is the bug, not the cleanup. **(4) The two public pages are copy-paste twins by design.** `explorer.html` and `writer.html` have no build step (codemap §2), so the DS `:root` block, the `<head>` theme-boot IIFE and the `applyTheme`/`THEME_CYCLE` trio are duplicated verbatim in both, sharing `stratus_explorer_token` AND now `explorer:theme`. Edit them together; a change to one that isn't mirrored shows up as the two pages disagreeing about theme after a click.
- **UI.13 — four facts the remaining polish passes need:** **(1) A control that commits on BLUR is not safe inside anything that unmounts on outside click.** React removes the node, the browser moves focus to `<body>` WITHOUT firing `blur`, and the value is gone — no error, no test failure, and the optimistic UI never showed it as saved either. The `GearPopover` fix (blur the focused descendant, then close) generalizes to any popover/drawer/modal holding a commit-on-blur field. **(2) A gear inherits its host's form semantics.** Enter inside a text control in a `<form>` submits it; in the Composer that means "save the post you were writing" while the user thought they were editing a setting. `preventDefault` + commit on the control itself is the fix that keeps Enter useful; a container-level `onKeyDown` would have tripped biome's a11y rules on a static element. **(3) User-visible copy that quotes a configurable number goes stale silently.** "need ≥3 posts in a slot" survived UI.6 making the gate a mirrored knob, and would have been visibly wrong the moment a gear let someone change it in place. When a polish pass puts a gear next to a feature, grep that feature's strings for the numbers the gear now moves. **(4) Not every plan line has a target any more:** the Calendar's "thread grouping indent" is unbuildable (thread tails carry `scheduledFor: null`, so they never enter a day column) and "cost line as a `Message` variant" names a primitive UI.10 never shipped. Check the plan's UI nouns against `sidepanel/ui/` before budgeting for them.
- **UI.14 — five facts UI.15 and UI.17 need:** **(1) A mirrored key is pinned by TWO exact-list tests, not one.** `src/x/settings/registry.test.ts` asserts the mirrored key list (now **26**) and `extension/src/shared/serverSettings.test.ts` asserts `Object.keys(ServerConfig)` **and** the full-blob round-trip — so adding one knob is a 2-file move that fails 3 assertions until both halves land. That is the design (a mirrored key with no `ServerConfig` field ships in the blob and is never read), but budget for it: the first `bun run test` after touching the registry WILL be red. **(2) A dead CSS rule is invisible until you go looking.** Migrating four tabs onto the primitives orphaned `.people-stage-filter`, `.badge-tracked`, `.dm-status`/`.dm-status-sent` — no build error, no test failure, and each one is a lie the next reader believes. The UI.16 audit habit generalizes: after a migration, grep each touched class for consumers (`grep -rn "\bclassname\b" --include="*.tsx" --include="*.ts"`) and delete what hits zero. Watch for false zeros: a class built by a template literal (`stage-${stage}`, `chip chip-${tone}`) has no literal occurrence anywhere. **(3) A primitive sized for its first four consumers breaks on the fifth.** `.ui-subtabs` never wrapped because nothing had needed more than 4 tabs; a 7-tab filter row overflows the panel silently (no error, it just runs off the edge). When a polish pass migrates a hand-rolled control onto a primitive, diff what the hand-rolled class was doing — `.people-stage-filter` was exactly the `flex-wrap` the primitive lacked. **(4) `useServerSettings()` inside a list-loading `useCallback` is a dependency.** Voice's `loadTweets` sends `limit: tweetLimit`, so `tweetLimit` had to join the dep array or a settings change would only take effect on the next unrelated refetch. The three UI.12 caps were all render-time slices and never had this shape. **(5) Where the gear goes is a real decision, not a formality.** The plan named the People gear's host wrong (roster header for a knob that only bites in the dossier), and two of the three lists it caps are conditionally rendered — a gear hung on one of them would vanish for a thin dossier. Rule of thumb: a knob that caps N lists goes on the nearest ALWAYS-rendered header enclosing all of them, with the `note` naming what it does not cover.
- **UI.15 — five facts UI.17 needs:** **(1) The dead-class audit's false zeros are real and this task hit one.** After migrating the four tabs, `.badge-draft` and `.badge-posted` had **zero** literal occurrences anywhere in `extension/src` and were still fully live — `Calendar.tsx` and `Today.tsx` build them with `` `badge badge-${r.status}` ``. Deleting them would have silently unstyled the scheduled-post ramp on two other tabs with no build error and no test failure. The audit is still worth running (it correctly found `.seg-group`/`.seg-row`/`.studio-bg-modes` dead), but **grep for the constructors — `` `badge-${ ``, `chip chip-${`, `stage-${` — before deleting any class**, and treat a zero on a class whose name looks like a state as suspect by default. **(2) A `server`-scoped knob behind an inline gear is a different feature from a `mirrored` one.** Mirrored: the panel reads the new number and the next render is correct, free. Server-scoped: the number shaped the payload, so the surface must re-read itself or the gear looks broken. Every gear before UI.15 was mirrored; the Playbook's is the first server one, and the re-read has to be bounded (one per distinct value, ref-guarded) or a refused value polls. Check which kind you have before wiring the next one. **(3) User-visible copy that a doc's whole argument rests on is not free to change.** `insufficient data (n=…)` appears eight times in `docs/playbook-tab.md`, including as the subject of its "single most important thing" section. The plan asked for entirely different wording; the cheap, better move was to EXTEND the existing phrase (`n=7/20`) rather than replace it — one doc paragraph instead of a rewrite, and the Wave-3 carry-forward's "docs assert user-visible strings" stays true. Grep the docs for a string before rewording it. **(4) A primitive's missing prop is often visible in its CSS.** `SubTabs` had no `disabled`, but `.ui-subtab:hover:not(:disabled)` was already written — the affordance had been designed and not plumbed. Same shape as UI.14's `.ui-subtabs` wrap (the deleted `.people-stage-filter` had carried the `flex-wrap` the primitive lacked). When a primitive can't express something, read its CSS before adding a prop; the answer may already be half there. **(5) `bunx biome check --write` is mandatory after adding imports to a PascalCase-heavy file.** Import-sort was the only gate that failed this task (twice — `Playbook.tsx` and `Studio.tsx`), exactly UI.12 gotcha 2: biome sorts case-sensitively, so `./SettingsGear.tsx` sorts above `./api.ts` and `./ui/Section.tsx` below `./studio/registry.ts`. Both typechecks and the whole suite were green while lint was red.
- **UI.12 — six facts the remaining polish passes need:** **(1) A `Section`'s `title` is a `string`, not a node.** Every migrated header that carried inline extras had to split: counts interpolate into the title (`` `Do next (${n})` ``), everything else — streak chip, 30d/90d toggle, band span, action buttons, the ⚙ — goes in `actions`. Two rules dropped out: a `.brief-section h3` descendant selector does NOT survive the move (`ui-section-eyebrow` styles the eyebrow instead), and any CSS the extras carried for living inside an `<h3>` (`margin-left: 8px` on `.targets-band`/`.fans-window`) is now double spacing on top of `.ui-section-actions`' gap — check the rule, don't just move the element. **(2) Biome sorts imports CASE-SENSITIVELY, so a PascalCase module sorts before lowercase siblings**: `./SettingsGear.tsx` belongs up with `./Radar.tsx`/`./Targets.tsx`, NOT next to `./settingsEditor.ts`. Only `bun run lint` catches it; both typechecks are happy. **(3) `useSettingsEditor` costs one extra `GET /x/settings` per tab mount** ($0, always-mounted route, no writes) — verified safe against a refetch loop because `useSettings()`'s `onChanged` filter ignores the `settings:server` key the sync writes, so a gear PATCH cannot re-create the `settings` object the hook depends on. A future tab-level hook that DOES depend on something the mirror sync touches would loop; check the filter first. **(4) A `mirrored` key added for a panel-only reader still widens the CONTENT-SCRIPT payload** — `serverSettings.ts` is inlined into the IIFE, so every field lands in `content.js` whether the page reads it or not. Harmless at this size, but the §7.26 check still has to pass: `grep -cE '^\s*import[ {]' extension/dist/content.js` == 0 after `cd extension && bun run build`. Verified 0 at UI.12. **(5) A knob is only real once something reads it.** The reverse of D132a: UI.12 minted `x.display.fansAmberTopN` and wired it in the SAME task, and added the Fans gear the plan omitted, because a registered key with no consumer and no affordance is as dead as an unregistered one — it just fails silently in the other direction. **(6) The two brief-read `x.display.*` knobs stayed `server`** (`sparklineDays`, `leaderCount`): the panel receives those numbers already applied, inside the brief payload, so mirroring them would ship a value nothing reads. `registry.test.ts` pins that split, and `routes/settings.test.ts` separately asserts `x.display.sparklineDays` is absent from the mirrored values response — a blanket "the display group is mirrored" would break it.

### the two discharged pre-seeded adaptations (D6, D8 — both discharged at UI.11) — moved verbatim from STATE.md

- **D6** (binds all Settings.tsx tasks pre-UI.11): keep feature Settings UI minimal; UI.11 rebuilds and absorbs.
- **D8** (binds UI.11): prune the "coming soon" manifest to features still unbuilt at that time.

No gear: the whole form lives in chrome.storage-local and there is no `x.harvest.*` registry group. `shared/harvest.ts` was NOT touched; sibling `shared/userCell.ts` is free too) |
| `extension/src/sidepanel/Playbook.tsx` + `Replies.tsx` + `Studio.tsx` + `studio/KitEditor.tsx` | — (**UI.15 polished and released all four; no open task claims them.** Each holds ONE `useSettingsEditor` at its panel root (Playbook, Replies; Studio and Harvest have no knob). **The Playbook's gate-reload effect is the fragile part** — `gateFromEditor(editor.groups)` vs `data.minN`, one re-read per distinct value after `PATCH_DEBOUNCE_MS + 300`, guarded by the `reloadedForGate` ref; drop the ref and a value the registry refuses polls `GET /x/playbook` until the editor's own re-read reverts the gear. `ResultCell` is the ONE place a gated cell is rendered — a new Playbook table must use it, not a fresh `insufficient data` string, or the gate stops being named. `Replies.tsx`'s `listLimit` is a FETCH param and therefore a dep of `loadHistory`. **`ReplyLists.tsx` and `studio/fields.tsx` were deliberately NOT migrated** — separate RL/ST-lane files whose own blocks (`.studio-bg`, `LibraryRail`) are unchanged; the Studio template gallery keeps `.studio-template` because each pill carries two lines) |

Every block is `Section` + `EmptyState` now; `people-head` and `people-quicklog` stay bare cards on purpose (identity header + toolbar, D135e). **Each tab holds ONE `useSettingsEditor` at its panel root** and threads it down — People's into `DossierView` (the gear is on the dossier header because `x.display.dossierListLen` caps three lists, two conditional; the **timeline is deliberately uncapped** and the gear's `note` says so), Channels' into `Room`, Voice's straight into its *Saved tweets* `Section`. `EVENT_ICONS` is an **exhaustive** `Record<PersonEvent['type'], string>` of TEXT glyphs — widening `PersonEventType` still forces a new glyph, and it must not be an emoji. Voice's `loadTweets` sends `limit: tweetLimit` from the mirrored blob, so **`tweetLimit` is a dep of that `useCallback`** — a settings-fed fetch param is not a render-time slice. Ideas' `badgeClassFor` is gone: lifecycle chips come from `chips.ts::ideaChip`, not the scheduled-post `.badge-*` ramp it used to borrow) |

Every threshold is an exported opening guess — a recalibration is a one-line diff in the pure file and moves every surface at once) |
