The scorecard is computed **inside** `buildDigestFacts` (D112a); `QuestInputs.originalsTarget?` is optional so pre-GR.8 streaks stay valid; `GOAL_KINDS` is a 5-union with `missed` in `GOAL_STATUSES`, and `POST /me/goals` is the single goal writer) |

## Archived at GT.9 (2026-07-27) — the Wave-6 GT lane (mika growth tactics, GT.1–GT.8)

> Moved verbatim from STATE.md by line number when GT.9 closed the lane. All eighteen entries are discharged: every task they bound has shipped, and their generalizable halves live in codemap §7 rules **4a/4b/4c** and §9's `smoke-growth-tactics.ts` bullet. Grep this section by task id (`GT.3`) or filename before touching a file the GT lane built — `src/x/people/reciprocity.ts`, `src/x/routes/brief.ts`'s milestone helpers, `extension/src/shared/glance.ts`'s `isReciprocityPerson`, the `roster` band in `shared/radar.ts`, or either prompt pair.

### Header priors (GT.8 → GT.4)

- **prior (GT.8 — roster sightings into the Radar; xhigh, dep GT.6✓ + the D143 lock, **$0 — DOM capture only, no X call, no LLM, and not even a new fetch: it reuses the glance map `scan` already loads**, 13 files, extension + server + docs, no schema/migration/registry/mirror/route/MCP change; suite 1707→1719 (+12)):** a fresh (<24h) post by someone already in my circle enters the queue with `band:'roster'` even when the classifier says skip. **The headline is a measurement again — the third GT task running.** The plan's "presence in the glance map IS membership" is true about the map and false as a rule: measured live, glance presence = **1,596** handles, the plan's own decision-2 `stage ≥ noticed` = **746**, the shipped `isReciprocityPerson` (stage ≥ `engaged` ∪ roster) = **14**, identical to the rankmap set with **zero handles on either side alone** — so the Radar chip and the money gate provably cannot fork. NEW pure predicate in `extension/src/shared/glance.ts` (client twin of `loadReciprocityHandles()` unscoped; reciprocal twin comments; a test walks all six stages so the stage chip and the capture share one floor). `content.ts` `applyBand(article, glance)` gained one `else if`; `shared/radar.ts` widened `RadarBand` and collapsed three `band === 'manual'` checks into `bandStickiness` + `bandWeight`, **which is where the plan's two silent gaps surfaced** — a roster re-sight would have downgraded an earned `hot` (vpm decays with age), and the two-value rank trick sorted `warm` below `roster`. Eviction drops roster first (the plan's own queue-pressure risk, discharged). Server: `parseBatchTweets` + the two radar types accept it, confirm coerces it to null beside `'manual'`. `Radar.tsx` labels it **"your circle"**, the only band with a tooltip. See **D159** + the GT.8 gotcha; codemap §7 gained rule **4c**. Browser-verify owed (next-up item 0j).
- **prior (GT.7 — daily reciprocity quest; high, dep GT.6✓, **$0 — one indexed set load per brief, no X call, no LLM**, 8 files, server + extension + docs, no schema/migration/registry/mirror/route/MCP change; suite 1700→1707 (+7)):** `QUEST_KEYS` is **6** — `reciprocity` ("N replies to your people"), `n` = today's posted `reply_drafts` whose author is in the `people/reciprocity.ts` set, target `loadDoctrine().reciprocityTargetMin` (niche-owned; deliberately no `x.quests.*` twin — D2), vacuous-done with *"no one in your circle yet"* on an empty circle. `routes/brief.ts` loads the set ONCE in the quest `Promise.all`. **The headline is again a measurement: the plan's plain membership test grades the quest's own input.** `RECIPROCITY_MIN_STAGE = engaged` is "I have replied to them", so replying is what creates membership — and over the whole corpus **every one of 14 posted replies was a first contact (`repeat_contact` 0 on all 5 active days)**, so the literal rule reports `n === repliesPostedToday` forever. Shipped as `loadReciprocityHandles({asOf: todayStart})` — ONE rule at two instants, the gate still asking unscoped — plus a *"today went to new faces"* note so the resulting honest `0/5` doesn't read as a broken counter. See **D158** + the GT.7 gotcha; codemap §7 gained rule **4b**. Extension: the `QuestKey` mirror + the quest-gear note (the list renders generically). Browser-verify owed (next-up item 0i).
- **prior (GT.6 — band-gate roster exemption + reciprocity doctrine knob; xhigh, no deps, **$0 — one small indexed read on the gate's refusal path, no X call, no LLM**, 12 files [2 new], server + extension + docs + smoke, no schema/migration/registry/mirror change, MCP still 23; suite 1690→1700 (+10)):** NEW `src/x/people/reciprocity.ts` — `loadReciprocityHandles()` / `isReciprocityHandle()` (membership on that same Set, so the batch and single answers can't fork) / `isReciprocityHandleSafe()` (§7.8 — false keeps the old 422) / `RECIPROCITY_MIN_STAGE`. `/replies/generate`'s refusal arm consults it: exempt → stamp `ctx.gateBypass='roster'` before the insert and fall through, else the unchanged 422 — uniform across `null`/`skip`, batch untouched, and a hot/warm post never pays the lookup. `PostContext.gateBypass?: 'roster'` is server-stamped, rejected by `parseContext`'s allowlist by construction, and **verified byte-identical in the rendered prompt** — it is a cohort key, not a message to the model. Sixth doctrine knob `reciprocityTargetMin` (default 5) wired through `niche/defaults.ts` + **`DOCTRINE_KEYS`** + the extension mirror + a 6th Niche-card input; `docs/replies-tab.md` gained "Your people are exempt" and `docs/settings-tab.md` went to six numbers. **The headline is the measurement: the plan's `stage ≥ noticed` was rejected on prod data** (730/730 `noticed` rows are ambient hover captures; it would have exempted 24 of the 25 accounts ever drafted for) in favour of `stage ≥ engaged` ∪ the 2–10× roster — the set `/people/rankmap` already ships. See **D157** + the GT.6 gotcha; codemap §7 gained rule **4a**.
- **prior (GT.4 — milestone nudge; high, no deps, **$0 recurring / ~$0.006 Grok only on the button**, 8 files, no schema/migration/registry/mirror/route/MCP change; suite 1639→1649 (+10)):** `GET /x/brief` gained `milestoneWatch`, computed by the new pure `buildMilestoneWatch(series, now)` over the **already-loaded** 30d `convSnaps` rows (the plan's "widen rather than re-query" step was free). The ladder is duplicated from `extension/src/studio/milestones.ts` per plan decision 5, with a twin comment in both files and a test pinning the server copy. Two rules the studio twin lacks, because a nudge is not a label: the crossing must be **witnessed** (a below-rung snapshot must precede it — else a fresh install congratulates you on an inherited milestone) and it goes quiet after 3 days. `Today.tsx` gained `MilestoneCard` between Pinned post and Account health, whose **Draft it** button is a bare `api.drafts.generate({idea})` — no new route, no prompt edit. `docs/today-tab.md` gained §11 and renumbered 11–17 → 12–18. See **D151** + the GT.4 gotcha.

### Deviations D147, D148, D150, D151, D157, D158, D159

- **D147** (GT.1, binds JD.1 and GT.2): **the bullet went in as rule #6, not appended as #7 — the hygiene rule moved.** The plan says only "add one bullet to `## How the replies sound`". Appending would have put a grounding rule after "Zero emoji. No hashtags. No links." — and both sibling prompts (`post prompt.md` §2, `thread prompt.md`) already end their voice list on that same hygiene rule, so the reply prompt now matches them at 7 rules with the echo rule sitting next to its topical twin, #5 "Specific beats generic". **Anything reading these rules by number is now off by one past #5.** Two things the delta-check turned up that the plan text doesn't say: **(a) the edit is a single `replace_all` over one line, because the anti-drift slice makes it so.** `## How the replies sound` is inside the block `src/test.test.ts` extracts (`## Who I am` → `## The three variants`) and asserts `REPLY_BATCH_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` contains verbatim, and the old `6. **Zero emoji…` line is byte-identical in both literals — so replacing both occurrences with the same two lines satisfies byte-sync and anti-drift in one move. The batch template's *own* condensed "Hard rules for each variant" list sits below the block, is asserted by nothing, and was deliberately left alone (the echo rule reaches the batch path through the voice block already). **(b) Three template tests assert strings ABSENT** — `two variants` (RU.1), `clipboard`, `solopreneur` — so new prompt copy has to be checked against them, not just added. Wording is scoped explicitly to *the post text* so it cannot be read as contradicting `RELATIONSHIP_INSTRUCTION`'s "never recite it" (that governs the relationship block at the variable tail). No new tests, per the plan: the existing byte-sync + anti-drift + N.4 equivalence trio is the coverage, and all three were **also verified out-of-band** rather than inferred from a green suite. One prefix-cache bust on the next draft, as decision 7 expects.
- **D148** (GT.2): **one plan premise is wrong, and the block had to reconcile two rules the plan never mentions.** **(a) "§9 is builder-texture-free" is false** — §9's own hook patterns carry `386, 4MB RAM`, `4h/day`, `20 years in a Romanian public hospital`, exactly what the planning-error log already records (post §6/§9/§0 hold biography outside the N.3 §1/§5 extraction scope). The plan's *conclusion* still holds (equivalence fixtures only read §1/§5, and they stayed green), but the premise means **anything added to §9 is inherited verbatim by every future niche**. So the four skeletons ship with niche-neutral examples — "money against time, safety against upside", "show me the thing you're working on" — instead of the analysis's `$2M vs $50M` or a `5K MRR` framing, which would have deepened a known leak N.9 must eventually clean. **(b) The formats collide with two standing rules and the collisions are resolved IN the copy, not left to the model.** The prompt's intro forbids asking for a follow, and §7 forbids engagement-bait — a poll-list or audience CTA reads like both. The block therefore states that these invite a *reply*, never a follow, and that "a real question I actually want answered is not bait" (with the three counter-examples: no "like if you agree", no reply-for-reply trading, no question wrapped around a brag). Without this a §7-obedient model would keep refusing the very formats the block licenses. **(c) The "at most one such format per day" rule was made implementable**: the drafter returns three drafts in one call, so a per-day ceiling it can't observe is dead text — it became **at most ONE of the three drafts** may use a format. **(d) `POST_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` is 9,816 chars** (the "~12KB cacheable prefix" in the docs is the rendered size, not the literal); `{{MY_WINNERS}}` sits at 97.6% so the cacheable-prefix test has enormous headroom — a future §9 addition will not threaten it.
- **D150** (GT.3, binds GT.4 and anything that ever wants to say "me" to a prompt or a person row): **the plan's context spec asks for a value the system does not have, and the fix is a placeholder plus a steer.** **(a) The seed checklist item is gated on `!linkInFirstReply` too, not just the button.** The plan hides only the button; leaving the item would print "Seed the first comment yourself" directly above "Pin your first reply — the link lives there", which are the same slot. Anyone verifying the plan's done-when #1 on a link-in-first-reply launch will correctly see no seed item. **(b) `SELF_X_USER_ID` is the account's identity — the @handle is nowhere.** `tokens.x_username` exists as a column and is **never written** (`src/x/server.ts`'s OAuth callback stores the token set only, and no route exposes the row); the publisher links own posts as `/i/web/status/<id>`; `.env` has no handle; the extension only ever derives one from a `/status/` pathname for the early-replies self-filter, and never stores it. So the plan's "my handle" cannot be obtained — and the two ways to get it were both rejected: a new `GET /x/…/account` route would return null for exactly this reason, and `getMe()` would put a **billed X read behind a UI button** (refuse-before-spend, and the plan promises $0 X). The context ships `handle:'me'`, `author:'my own post'` → `@me (my own post, just now):` in the rendered prompt, which reads *better* to the model than a real handle would. **Two consequences a later task will meet:** every seed row files under `@me` in the Replies tab / `?sourceAuthor=` filter (arguably a free cohort key), and confirming a seed *posted* runs the §7.8 people hook on `sourceAuthorUsername` → a `me` person row with a `my_reply` event. Only the Replies tab's "Confirm posted" can reach that (the room's Copy PATCHes `copied`; `background.ts`'s auto-`posted` is the radar-paste path, a different flow). If a `people` self-exclusion is ever wanted, it belongs server-side next to `upsertPerson`, not in the panel. **(c) The seed needs a steer, and `idea` is the knob for it** — the reply prompt's whole frame is "reply to someone else's post", so without one the draft addresses the author of my own tweet. One sentence goes in as `idea` (stored on the row, visible in the Replies tab, no prompt edit, no byte-sync exposure). **(d) `/x/replies/outcomes` joins on `postedTweetId`, not `sourceTweetId`** (checked, because a seed's `sourceTweetId` IS one of my own published posts): a posted seed measures its own reply's metrics like any other, and does not fabricate a crosstab row from the parent post's views.
- **D151** (GT.4, binds GT.9's smoke and anything that adds a Today card or a second twin): **three plan readings resolved, one plan step that was already paid, and one plan premise that is false.** **(a) A nudge needs a rule the twin doesn't have: the crossing must be WITNESSED.** The plan says "peak-based ≥ like `latestCrossed`", and taken literally that is wrong for this surface — `latestCrossed` labels a Studio card ("you are a 1,000-follower account"), which is true whenever the peak says so, but `buildMilestoneWatch` fires a *nudge to post about a thing that just happened*. On a fresh install whose oldest snapshot is already past a rung, the literal reading makes the earliest in-window snapshot the "crossing" and congratulates the operator on a milestone they passed years ago. So the server helper additionally requires a snapshot **below** the rung before the one that reaches it (`crossIdx > 0`) — the same discipline `pinnedSince` uses when it ignores null-pin rows so the 21-day warning can't fire on backfilled history. **The cost is explicit and is the right way round: a genuine crossing that happens to be the very first snapshot after setup is never nudged.** Every test the plan lists still holds; this adds one. **(b) `followers` is the CROSSING snapshot's count, not today's.** The plan names the field and not its meaning. Today's count would make the triple incoherent on the plan's own dip-after-cross case (crossed at 1005, now 990 — "you crossed 1000, you have 990" reads like a bug); the crossing count is the reading that *proves* the crossing, so `{milestone, crossedOn, followers}` all describe one event. **(c) The plan's "reuse/widen rather than re-query" step was already paid** — `convSnaps` (the `CONVERSION_SNAPSHOT_DAYS = 30` series S0.1 loads and S0.9 rides on) already selects `snapshotAt` + `followersCount`, so GT.4 added zero SQL. 30d > the 3d nudge window means the crossing AND the below-rung snapshot that licenses it both fall inside; a future task that narrows that window breaks the nudge silently. **(d) The plan's route-test premise is false: the in-memory DB is NOT seedless.** "Route test: `milestoneWatch` key present and null on the seedless in-memory DB" — but test files share one in-memory DB and **four** of them seed `account_snapshots` (`goals.test.ts`, `people.test.ts`, `doctrine.test.ts` 100, `me.test.ts` 500, `playbook.test.ts` 10,000, several future-dated), so whether a rung looks freshly crossed inside `brief.test.ts` depends on file order. The route test asserts the **wire shape** (key present; if non-null then a real rung + parseable date + `followers >= milestone`) and behaviour is pinned in the 9 pure tests. Same call any future brief-payload test faces. **(e) Deliberately not done, flagged rather than silently skipped:** the ladder is duplicated per plan decision 5, but the repo already has a better mechanism for exactly this — `src/shared/*` + an `extension/src/*` re-export shim (`replyBand`, `channelSuggest`, `activeTimes`). No server module imports from `extension/`, and this task kept it that way; moving `milestones.ts` to `src/shared/` is a one-file cleanup a future task can take, and would delete both twin comments and the pinning test. No registry knob was added for the 3-day window (the plan asks for none; it is a plain constant beside `PIN_CANDIDATE_DAYS`), so the registry is **still 14 groups / 61 knobs, 27 mirrored, MCP 23**.
- **D157** (GT.6, binds GT.7/GT.8/GT.9 and every future gate carve-out): **the plan's membership rule was measured before it shipped and did not survive — and two of the plan's four edit targets were wrong about where the code lives.** **(a) `stage ≥ noticed` is not "my CRM", it is "their hover card rendered while I scrolled".** Decision 2 reads "a saved tweet/author/hover = noticed" as if the three were equivalent acts of curation. Pulled the prod counts $0 through `POST /x/data/query` (the S1 explorer; the SC.2/SC.5 recipe, one round-trip): **1,591 people rows, 742 at stage ≥ noticed, and 730 of the 730 `noticed` rows are hover-only — the entire DB holds exactly ONE `saved_tweet`.** `hover_sighting` is ambient AX.1 capture, not an act. The consequence is the number that decided it: **24 of the 25 accounts I have ever drafted a reply to are stage ≥ noticed** (you hover the author on the way to the tweet), so the plan's carve-out removes the band gate from ~96% of the deliberate-reply flow — not a lane, an off switch — and `contextSnapshot.gateBypass` becomes a near-constant, which is D154(a)/D156(a) arriving for the third time. **Shipped at `stage ≥ engaged` ∪ the 2–10× roster instead: 12 + 1 = 13 handles today, covering 11 of those 25 (44%)** — "someone I have actually posted a reply to, or a roster target" — so first contact still meets the gate. Two reasons that floor and not another: `engaged` is `computeStage`'s own "I replied to them" rung, and **it is byte-for-byte the membership `GET /people/rankmap` already ships** (S0.3, "handles worth tiering"), so the Radar chip GT.8 stamps and the money gate cannot fork (the UI.7 badge-vs-gate lesson). The floor is one exported constant (`RECIPROCITY_MIN_STAGE`) — widening it is a one-line change if the gate turns out to nag. Generalized into codemap §7 rule **4a**. **(b) The plan's "add `isReciprocityHandle` to `people/store.ts`" would have created an import cycle** — the roster loader is `routes/voice.ts::loadTargetHandles` and `routes/voice.ts` already imports `people/store.ts`. It went into a NEW `src/x/people/reciprocity.ts` instead: one upward import (`people/` → `routes/`, the only one in non-test code, flagged in the module header) against the alternative of re-deriving the 2–10× band and forking the doctrine knobs (D154d). **The single-handle check is implemented ON the batch set** (`(await loadReciprocityHandles()).has(h)`) rather than as a targeted query — the set is small by construction, and GT.7's batched consumer then cannot disagree with the gate about who is a member. **(c) `parseContext` lives in `routes/replies.ts`, not `replies/prompt.ts`** (the plan names the wrong file), and it needed **no code change at all**: it builds a fresh object from an explicit allowlist, so `gateBypass` is rejected by construction — the task added the test that pins that, which is the part that can rot. **(d) A doctrine knob is a THREE-place server move, and the plan lists one.** `NicheDoctrine` + `DEFAULT_DOCTRINE` + `resolveDoctrine` in `niche/defaults.ts`, **and `DOCTRINE_KEYS` in `routes/niche.ts`** — `readDoctrine` iterates that list, so a key missing from it is dropped silently while the PATCH still returns 200 and the UI still shows the value it typed (the Niche card PATCHes the whole object, D27c). `niche.test.ts` and `scripts/smoke-niche.ts` now both round-trip the newest knob so the next knob can't ship half-wired. **(e) Three things checked rather than assumed, all $0:** `buildGrokInput` output is **byte-identical** with and without `gateBypass` (it is bookkeeping, and now provably never reaches the model), the JSON round-trip keeps it in `contextSnapshot`, and the shipped rule was re-run against prod to print what the operator will actually experience (13 exempt handles). **(f) Deliberately not done, flagged rather than silently skipped:** no playbook cell splits on `gateBypass` (n will be 0 for weeks — the plan says so itself), and the batch path is untouched because it has no gate.
- **D158** (GT.7, binds GT.8/GT.9 and anything that ever counts an act against a set the act can join): **the plan's counting rule was measured before it shipped and did not survive — it would have graded the quest's own input.** **(a) `RECIPROCITY_MIN_STAGE = engaged` means "I have posted a reply to them", so posting a reply is what CREATES the membership.** Task 7 says "count today's posted drafts whose author is a reciprocity handle", full stop — and the people hook promotes an author to `engaged` the moment a reply is confirmed posted, so by the time the brief is read the stranger you just met is one of "your people". Measured $0 through `POST /x/data/query` (the GT.6 recipe, two round-trips): over the entire corpus — **14 posted replies across 5 active days — `repeat_contact` is 0 on every single day**, i.e. every reply the account has ever posted went to a handle it had never replied to before. So the literal rule reports `n === repliesPostedToday` **daily, forever**: a second copy of the replies quest wearing a relationship label. That is D154(a)/D156(a)/D157(a) arriving a fourth time, and this one is a consequence D157 itself created — at the plan's original `noticed` floor, membership was an act of curation that *preceded* the reply, and moving the floor to `engaged` (correct for the GATE, which asks "is this person mine right now") silently changed what a COUNT over the same set means. **(b) Fixed with a time qualifier on the ONE loader, not a second set:** `loadReciprocityHandles({asOf})` — the stage half additionally requires a rung reached before the cutoff, and the brief passes `todayStart`. `stageUpdatedAt` is written **only when the rung actually changes** (`store.ts::recomputePerson` line 219, checked not assumed), so it reads as "when they became this" and an ally who merely got liked today keeps their old stamp; a null stamp predates the stage machine and counts as already-mine. The roster half is deliberately unscoped — saving someone as a 2–10× target is curation that cannot be self-created by replying. Generalized into codemap §7 rule **4b**. Known undercount, safe direction and stated rather than hidden: someone promoted `engaged`→`responded` today drops out of today's quest. **(c) The honest reading of the shipped rule on this account is `0/5` every day, and that needed one line of copy to survive contact with a user.** A non-empty circle with zero hits next to a busy replies quest reads as a broken counter, so the quest prints *"today went to new faces — circle back to someone"* whenever `knownPeopleCount > 0 && n === 0 && repliesPostedToday > 0` — an addition to the plan's note contract (which only covers the vacuous case), and the one thing on this surface that turns a correct zero into the insight the quest exists to deliver. **(d) The streak objection was checked, not assumed, and is hypothetical:** all **23** tracked `streaks` days are `all_done: false` already (the `replies` quest at target 10 is never met), so a 6th quest cannot break a run that has never existed. Had there been real streaks this would have needed a different answer. **(e) No registry knob and no mirror key** — the target is `loadDoctrine().reciprocityTargetMin` (GT.6's), niche-owned like the reply band, so the Today quest gear is still the four `x.quests.*` keys and its note now names both niche-owned quests. Registry stays **14 groups / 61 knobs, 27 mirrored, MCP 23**. **(f) Deliberately not done, flagged rather than silently skipped:** no playbook cell splits on reciprocal-vs-first-contact (the corpus is 100% one side — nothing to compare), and the quest does not distinguish a roster target from a CRM person in its label.
- **D159** (GT.8, binds GT.9 and every client that re-decides a server rule): **the plan's membership premise was measured and replaced for the third GT task running, and two rules the plan didn't mention were load-bearing.** **(a) "The glance map only contains non-retired people rows + roster targets, so presence IS membership" is true about the map and false as a rule.** Glance holds **every** stage; D157 already established that ambient `hover_sighting` makes most of the timeline `noticed`. Measured $0 through the two live endpoints (`GET /people/glance` + `GET /people/rankmap`, one round-trip each, the GT.6/GT.7 recipe pointed at routes instead of SQL): **glance presence = 1,596 handles, the plan's own decision-2 `stage ≥ noticed` = 746, the shipped rule = 14** — and that 14 is *identical* to the rankmap key set, **zero handles on either side alone**, which is the property that matters: the Radar chip and the money gate provably cannot disagree. At 1,596 a cap-100 buffer stops being an opportunity queue and becomes an ambient-people feed, which is the plan's own "queue pressure" risk arriving as a certainty rather than a risk. Shipped as `isReciprocityPerson(entry)` in the pure `extension/src/shared/glance.ts` — `isTarget || stageRank ≥ engaged`, the client twin of `loadReciprocityHandles()` **unscoped** (STATE's GT.7 gotcha asked for exactly this question; the answer is the rule, not the module, because the content IIFE cannot import db+drizzle). Reciprocal twin comments in both files (the D151e ladder discipline), and a test walks all six stages asserting the predicate agrees with the stage CHIP's gate, because `glance.ts` was already carrying `ENGAGED_RANK` **and the same finding in a comment** since AX.2 — this is a derived predicate on an existing duplication, not a new twin. Generalized into codemap §7 rule **4c**. **(b) The plan's merge rule is half a rule, and the missing half is the one that loses data.** It specifies the roster→hot upgrade; it says nothing about hot→roster, and the current line (`prev.band === 'manual' ? 'manual' : s.band`) lets the incoming win. But vpm decays with age, so `hot` → `skip` is a live transition *within one browsing session* — a tweet that earned `hot` at 10:00, re-sighted at 11:00 from an author in the circle, would visibly demote to "your circle" and lose its queue position. Shipped as a private `bandStickiness` (manual 2 > hot/warm 1 > roster 0): stickier prev survives, equal → fresher incoming wins, so **every pre-GT.8 band pair is byte-identical** and the three ad-hoc `band === 'manual'` comparisons collapse into one function. **(c) `rankSightings`' band comparison was a two-value trick that a third band breaks silently** — `a.band !== b.band ? (a.band === 'hot' ? -1 : 1)` sorts `warm` BELOW `roster`. Replaced with a `bandWeight`. Anyone adding a fifth band should note both of these were type-safe and green before the fix. **(d) Eviction got the same weight, which the plan's Edit list doesn't ask for but its Tests list does** ("merge upgrade, rank ordering …, eviction") — roster rows drop first, so a chatty circle cannot push the day's loudest opportunities out of the buffer. That is the plan's Risks entry discharged inside the same function rather than left as a watch item. **(e) The server never re-checks a `'roster'` claim, deliberately:** the band never reaches Grok and is coerced to null on confirm (§7.19), so a false claim costs a label, not money or a Playbook number — and a membership query on the batch path would be a per-row lookup buying nothing. `parseBatchTweets` widened; the coercion became `row.band === 'manual' || row.band === 'roster'` so the next queue-metadata band edits one predicate. **(f) The plan's "+ whyLine" shipped as a chip tooltip plus a widened guard.** The manual whyLine exists because a ⊕ pin has *no numbers*; a roster row usually has real (small) ones, and then the numbers are the honest line — the chip one line above already says why it's there. So `roster` is the only band carrying a `title`, and the zero-metrics guard now covers both queue-metadata bands. **(g) No registry knob:** `ROSTER_MAX_AGE_MIN` (24h) is a plain constant beside the other radar constants, per the plan. Registry stays **14 groups / 61 knobs, 27 mirrored, MCP 23**. **(h) Deliberately not done, flagged rather than silently skipped:** no on-page treatment (the border/dim/`data-stratus-band` are byte-untouched — the classifier's verdict is not overridden, only the radar stream is widened), no playbook `roster` cell (n=0 for weeks, and the confirm coercion means it could only ever be keyed on `radar_drafts.band`, not on the snapshot), and no filter for a circle member's *replies* to third parties — they enter like any other article, exactly as hot/warm sightings already do.

### Gotchas (GT.1 → GT.8)

- **GT.1 — facts for JD.1 (next in the `src/x/replies/*` lane) and GT.2:** the reply templates JD.1 must check as "unchanged" are the POST-GT.1 ones — **7 numbered voice rules, the echo rule at #6**. The two-literal edit is mechanical *only because* the changed line is byte-identical in both; a JD.1 trust-marker line that differs between single and batch cannot be done with one `replace_all` and must be hand-mirrored inside the anti-drift slice (`## Who I am` → `## The three variants`) or placed outside it entirely. **How to verify a prompt edit without trusting the suite alone** (this is what the xhigh pass bought): a five-line `bun -e` that re-derives byte-sync (`TEMPLATE.trimEnd() === md.trimEnd()`), re-slices the voice block and checks `BATCH.includes(vb)`, counts `^\d\. \*\*` in the block, and asserts the absent-string set — it runs in a second and tells you *which* guarantee broke, which a red `src/test.test.ts` does not. Also worth knowing for any prompt task: `GET /x/replies/default-prompt` returns `loadPromptSafe('reply').body`, so on a DB with an override row it shows the OVERRIDE, not the new default — the done-when is about the default path (verified via `loadPromptSafe` on an empty DB), and a prod operator who customized the prompt gets the new rule only on Restore Defaults.
- **GT.2 — facts for SC.2/SC.6 consumers and any future post-prompt edit:** the post pair is a **one-file-each** edit (`post prompt.md` + the `POST_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` literal, offset by 15 lines) with none of the reply pair's anti-drift machinery — `thread prompt.md` copied post §0–§5 only, so §6–§9 edits never touch `THREAD_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` (re-verified, not assumed). The absent-string set the post tests enforce is `Alteramens` / `Naval-derived` (N.3 custom-niche assertions, plus `scripts/smoke-niche.ts` which fails on the same two) — new §9 copy must stay clear of §1/§5 vocabulary. Escaping trap: the literal escapes backticks (` \` `) and would need `\${` — the new block deliberately uses neither. **For SC.2/SC.6:** the four skeletons now in the prompt map 1:1 onto `classifyFormat`'s `would_you_rather` / `poll_list` / `confession` / `audience_cta` branches (verified against `POST_FORMATS`, not assumed), so drafted posts should start landing in those cells — the cooldown SC.6 builds will therefore see *more* format-typed posts than the historical corpus suggests (the substance-43% baseline in D146 was measured before this prompt existed). Verification recipe (same shape as GT.1's): a `bun -e` that checks byte-sync, thread byte-sync, `buildPostDraftInput` output containing the block, no leftover `{{`, and the `{{MY_WINNERS}}` position ratio — a second's work, and it names which guarantee broke.
- **GT.3 — facts for GT.4 and any new `PostContext` call site:** **how to verify a new draft-surface call site for $0** — a `bun -e` that imports `parseContext` (exported from `src/x/routes/replies.ts`) and `buildGrokInput`, feeds it the *exact literal the panel builds*, and prints the rendered `ORIGINAL TWEET` line. It proves the whole 400 ladder (tweet-id regex, `USERNAME_RE` on the handle, non-empty author/url, parseable `postedAt`, the four metrics) and shows what the model will actually read, without a Grok call or a running server; a red one names which field. Do this instead of "it typechecks, ship it" — the context object is validated by a route, not by TS. **Panel facts:** `LaunchRoom.tsx` has no test file (extension panels are manually verified by convention, and this task added none), so its automated coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, and lint — **the seed button is BROWSER-VERIFY DEBT** (see next-up). The variant-chip idiom copied here is `Conversations.tsx`'s (`variantIdx` state, `.reply-variants`/`.reply-variant`, `setCopied(false)` on switch), not `Replies.tsx`'s text-compare one — `EarlyReplierRow` right below it still renders a single `replyText` and was deliberately left alone (RU.3 variants never reached it; a future task could unify the two, one file, no server change).
- **GT.4 — facts for GT.9's smoke, SC.5, and the next Today-card task:** **`docs/today-tab.md` is now §1–§18 and GT.4 renumbered 11–17 → 12–18** (Milestone is the new §11) — the numbered-heading trap the Wave-3 carry-forward warns about is live, and the check that makes it safe is `grep -rn "today-tab.md#[0-9]"` plus the in-file `](#N-` form: **zero numbered anchor links exist anywhere in the repo**, so renumbering is a heading-only edit. Re-run that grep before the next insert rather than assuming. **The card is the cheapest possible "one-click draft":** `api.drafts.generate(settings, {idea})` with no new route, no prompt edit and no byte-sync exposure — the whole feature is a steer string, which is also how GT.3's seed works (`idea: SEED_STEER`). Prefer this shape over minting a route for any future "draft X for me" button. **Browser-verify owed (see next-up)** — `MilestoneCard` has no test file (panel convention); its automated coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, lint, and the route/pure tests behind it. **How it was verified for $0 without a browser or a server:** a scratchpad `bun -e` that mounts `brief` on a bare Hono app against `SQLITE_PATH=:memory:`, inserts four `account_snapshots` rows spanning a crossing, and prints `body.milestoneWatch` — 200 + `{"milestone":1000,"crossedOn":…,"followers":1007}`, then the same script with a cold-start pair printing `null`. That is the GT.1/GT.3 recipe applied to a route instead of a prompt, and it is what makes "the payload is right" a checked claim rather than an inference from a green suite; **note `bun run <script>` buffers stdout off-TTY (UI.17 fact 5), so the script prints through `console.error`.**
- **GT.6 — facts for GT.7 (next in the lane), GT.8, GT.9's smoke, and anything that ever gates on membership:** **the $0 way to size a membership rule before you ship it is `POST /x/data/query` against prod** — the S1 explorer takes arbitrary read-only SQL, so "how many rows would this rule match, and what share of the calls it guards" is one round-trip, not a deploy. Two queries decided this task (see D157a) and they took a minute. Run them BEFORE the rule reaches a test file: a fixture proves the arithmetic, only the corpus proves the rule means what its name says. **For GT.7:** call `loadReciprocityHandles()` ONCE per brief and test membership against the Set — `isReciprocityHandle` is already just `.has()` over it, so calling the single-handle checker per row re-runs three queries per row for no new information. `knownPeopleCount` is the Set's size (13 on the real DB today, so the vacuous-done path will NOT fire there — write the empty-CRM case as a fixture, not by hoping). The doctrine knob it reads is `loadDoctrine().reciprocityTargetMin` (default 5). **For GT.8:** import the same module; a Radar `'roster'` band that disagrees with the gate about who is on the roster is the UI.7 fork with a new coat. **Testing the exempt path costs nothing and must stay that way:** the proof is that the request reaches **503 `llm_not_configured`** with both provider keys force-unset in a `finally` (the drafter.test.ts trick) — assert the 503, never a 201, or `bun run test` starts making real Grok calls on a dev machine that has a key in `.env`. **Fixture facts:** `people` rows need nothing but `handle`+`stage` (retired defaults false); the roster half needs an `account_snapshots` row that is the LATEST one in the shared DB — `reciprocity.test.ts` dates its **3000-01-01** because `playbook.test.ts` already owns 2999, and deletes it in the same test. **Panel:** the 6th doctrine input rides the existing `DOCTRINE_FIELDS` map (flex-wrap row, no layout work), and the card sends all six because PATCH replaces — an older unbuilt extension would silently reset the new knob to its default, which is why the extension half ships in the same commit as the server half.
- **GT.7 — facts for GT.8 (next in the lane), GT.9's smoke, and any future quest or membership counter:** **the $0 measurement that decided this task is one `POST /x/data/query` and it is the one to copy — a self-join asking "did an earlier row of this same kind exist for this key".** `repeat_contact` per day (was there a prior posted reply to the same handle) is what proved the reciprocity set is created BY the thing it counts; a fixture could never have shown it, because a fixture is written by someone who already believes the rule. Run it before the rule reaches a test file. **For GT.8:** import `loadReciprocityHandles` **unscoped** — a Radar chip asks "is this person mine right now", which is the gate's question, not the quest's; passing `{asOf}` there would hide someone you just started talking to. **For GT.9's smoke:** the quest's arithmetic is assertable $0 with no X call and no LLM — seed a `people` row with an explicit **old** `stageUpdatedAt` plus a posted `reply_drafts` row and read `GET /x/brief`; what is worth asserting is the **qualifier** (re-stamp the same person to now → the same reply stops counting) rather than a raw count, since the brief's window is exactly where other suites' fixtures live. Remember `/x/brief` is a read that WRITES (it settles goals and overwrites today's streak diary — Wave-3 carry-forward rule 4), so a smoke that reads it more than once should close on a clean read like `smoke-guardrails.ts` does. **Fixture facts:** a `people` row inserted with NO `stageUpdatedAt` is treated as already-mine (null ⇒ predates), so a test that means to exclude someone must stamp them explicitly — that is why `brief.test.ts`'s GT.7 describe first asserts the hit and then re-stamps to prove the drop. That describe is declared **LAST in the file** because it is the only one writing `people`, and cleans up in a try/finally inside the test as well as `afterAll`. **Panel:** the quest list renders generically over `brief.quests.items` (no per-key branch), so a new quest needs only the `QuestKey` mirror in `extension/src/shared/types.ts` — but the mirror IS load-bearing: it is a hand-maintained literal union, and forgetting it is an extension typecheck failure, not a silent drop. **Copy:** the two niche-owned quests (`replies`, `reciprocity`) are named in the quest gear's note and in `docs/today-tab.md` §3 — a third one owned by the niche means editing both.
- **GT.8 — facts for GT.9's smoke, JD.2+, and anyone touching the radar cores:** **the $0 way to prove a client rule and a server rule are the same rule is to fetch BOTH endpoints and diff the key sets** — a 30-line `bun -e` that imports the pure predicate from `extension/src/shared/glance.ts`, applies it to `GET /people/glance`, and compares against `GET /people/rankmap`. It printed 1,596 / 746 / 14 and `only client: 0, only server: 0`, which is what turned "these should agree" into a checked claim; it also priced the two rules the plan and its decision-2 would have shipped. Keep the recipe: two GETs beat any amount of reasoning about whether two files still say the same thing. **For GT.9's smoke:** the roster half is assertable $0 with no X call and no LLM — `radar_drafts` accepts a `band:'roster'` row directly, and `POST /x/radar/drafts/:id/confirm` must return `contextSnapshot.signals.band === null` while `metrics.views` still carries the real number (that is the §7.19 half; `radar.test.ts` already pins it, so the smoke's job is the round-trip over the real DB). The *capture* half is content-script DOM and is browser-verify only — do not try to smoke it. **Test facts:** the new `radar.test.ts` fixture id is `T_ROSTER = …006` and it is in `IDS`, so `afterAll` already drains it; `glance.test.ts`'s new describe is pure and cheap. `extension/src/shared/radar.test.ts` now has four merge cases and two rank cases about band precedence — if you add a fifth band, those are the ones that tell you which rung you broke. **Panel:** `Radar.tsx` has no test file (panel convention); coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, lint, the 12 new pure/route tests, and the bundle greps that prove Vite inlined the predicate into the IIFE — `grep -o "isTarget[^;{}]*" dist/content.js` shows the predicate verbatim, `grep '"roster"' dist/content.js` shows the capture call, and the import count is still 0. **Browser-verify owed (next-up item 0j).** **Vocabulary warning:** one concept now has three names by design — `reciprocity` (the server module + the quest), `roster` (the band value and the `gateBypass` marker) and **"your circle"** (every word the user reads). Do not "unify" them: the band value is a stored cohort key, and renaming it rewrites history in `radar_drafts.band`.

## Archived at GT.9 (2026-07-27) — three released lock rows + D140

> The three hot-file-locks rows below each state in their own text that **no open task binds the file**; the UI.17 pass archived 25 rows for exactly that reason. They are file notes now — `grep STATE-ARCHIVE.md <filename>` before touching one of these files. **D140** (UI.17's closing-task deviation) is discharged: its lane is closed, and its generalizable half — *a maintenance file that has outgrown one `Read` is not "big", it is broken* — is restated in STATE's live **D160(d)** with the failure mode UI.17 itself demonstrated.

### Released lock rows (content.ts, routes/replies.ts, replies/prompt.ts)

| File | Owner |
|---|---|
| `extension/src/content.ts` | — (**the D9 chain is COMPLETE at A3.3** and **UI.16 was its last claimant — no open task binds this file**. UI.16: `injectStyles` opens with `OVERLAY_TOKENS`, one `:root{}` block of 21 `--stratus-*` values; every rule below it is `var()`-only and **a color literal anywhere else in this sheet is now a bug**. A new chip family adds a tone to the block. Do NOT reach for `--strat-*` here (D137c). Earlier — **UI.7 released it** — `initBandThresholds()` is the fourth `storage.onChanged` listener in `start()` and every one of them filters `area !== 'local'` first; the module-level `bandThresholds` starts at `SERVER_DEFAULTS.band` and feeds BOTH `classifyBand` call sites (`applyBand`'s badge + the reply-master capture stamp) — a new call site must take it too or the badge and the server gate fork. **UI.16's token polish is the only remaining claimant.** Both ambient-capture gate orders are perf contracts: passive harvest enabled → `/home` → `!isHarvestActive()` → one `findPermalink` → throttle → only THEN `extractArticle`; active-times pathname → `passiveCaptureEnabled` → done/in-flight flags → only THEN `extractActiveTimesSection`) |
| `src/x/routes/replies.ts` | — (**released by GT.8 — the D143 GT.6→GT.8 lane is COMPLETE and no open task binds this file.** GT.8 touched only `parseBatchTweets`'s band union (`+'roster'`, four literals in one `if`); the gate arm is byte-untouched. Standing facts: **the refusal arm is an if/else** — exempt → `ctx.gateBypass='roster'` + fall through, else the unchanged 422. Do not turn that into an early-return ladder; the fall-through is what keeps ONE 422 in the file. The exemption calls `isReciprocityHandleSafe` from `people/reciprocity.ts` — anything else wanting that set imports it, never re-derives it, and a CLIENT that must re-decide it reproduces the rule with a twin comment (§7 rule 4c, GT.8's `isReciprocityPerson`)) |
| `src/x/replies/prompt.ts` + `reply prompt.md` | — (**released by JD.1 — the D143 lane is COMPLETE, no open task binds these files.** Standing facts: the anti-drift slice makes any edit between `## Who I am` and `## The three variants` a two-literal edit; a render-time addition can dodge that entirely by riding on the VALUE, as `UNTRUSTED_CONTEXT_MARKER` does. GT.6→GT.8 serialize `routes/replies.ts`, a different file) |

### D140

- **D140** (UI.17, the closing task): **one plan step that was dead, three docs items that were already paid, and two files that had quietly become unreadable.** **(a) The plan's headline smoke assertion no longer exists.** Task 17 says "PATCH `x.doctrine.replyTargetMin` → `/x/brief` reflects", and the plan's own done-when #2 repeats it — but that key was **dropped from the registry at UI.2** (D2/D128, Option B: the reply quota is niche-owned, so `loadDoctrine` is its single owner). Written literally the step would 400 on `unknown_setting`. Replaced with `x.doctrine.anchors3`+`anchors4` → the brief's `today.anchors`, which is the same demonstration (a doctrine knob visibly moving the Today payload) against a key that exists. **Both rungs are set to the same array on purpose** so the assertion holds whichever the ladder picks for today's filled-slot count — a one-rung PATCH would pass or fail depending on how many slots happen to be filled when you run it. **(b) The smoke writes NO ROWS, and that turned out to be a design property worth keeping rather than a shortcut.** It was tempting to seed five measured replies so the Playbook's `sufficient` flags would visibly flip; that would have meant `posts_published` fixtures (the NT.7 `retired: true` trap) and a cleanup path, in the one script whose subject matter is *settings*. Instead the gate is asserted **monotonically** (n≥1 ≥ n≥5 ≥ n≥100) with the **gated-cell denominator printed**, so a zero reads as "this DB has no measured cell" and not as "the counter is broken" — and `smoke-playbook.ts` already owns the seeded-cell flip. The upshot: the only state it mutates is `app_settings`, which makes it the cleanest possible D113(d) example — and the `fail()` restore path was **verified by injecting a mid-run failure**, not assumed. **(c) Three of the plan's docs items were already paid and were NOT re-done.** Every tab doc UI.11–UI.16 touched was updated in that pass's own commit (verified with `git log --name-only -- docs/`: today, composer, calendar, people, channels, voice, ideas, replies, playbook, harvest, s3-studio, settings, plus s1/writer/s6). The genuinely-owed piece was the one the plan names last: `docs/settings-tab.md`'s **registry table**. Likewise most of the plan's codemap list (§2's DS + plans rows, §3.1/§3.3/§3.4, §5, §6's tool count, §7's settings pattern) had landed with the task that created each thing — the codemap's per-task discipline works, and a closing docs-sync that re-does it would just churn. **(d) The plan's codemap counts are all stale by design and were ignored:** "§4 table count 27→28, migrations →0013, §6 +2 MCP tools (16→18)" describes the repo as of 2026-07-17. It is 40 tables, migrations through `0023`, and 23 MCP tools. A closing task reads the current numbers, never the plan's. **(e) Two files had grown past what the `Read` tool can open — the exact failure mode both exist to prevent.** The codemap header (line 3) had accumulated a per-task "what shipped" chain that was a **verbatim duplicate of §11's update log**, reaching ~68 KB on a single line; `Read` refused it, so the file every session is told to read first had become the file it could not read. Compacted to a stamp + pointers. STATE.md was ~112 KB (two paged reads at UI.14 *and* UI.15); the archive pass took it to ~30 KB of body, and the hot-file locks table went with it — with 0 open tasks it has no lane to serialize, and its rows had become file notes reachable by the documented `grep STATE-ARCHIVE.md <filename>`. **The generalizable rule: a maintenance file that has outgrown one `Read` is not "big", it is broken**, because the protocol that keeps it accurate is the very thing that can no longer run.
