### D-entries

- **D146** (SC.2, binds SC.5/SC.6): three resolutions the plan text leaves open. **(a) The fallback ladder mirrors x-builder's split, so `other` is degenerate-only.** `substance` = multi-line draft that matched no shape, `one_liner` = single line that matched none, `other` = only RTs / URL-only / <4-word fragments (§7.11). Consequence the plan's "<35% other" bar hides: over the real 136-original corpus `other` is 0.7% but **`substance` is 43%** — SC.5's `formatEffectiveness` will have one dominant cell, and the discriminating comparison at this account is cta/list/story/hot_take **against** substance, not against each other. **(b) SC.6's 4-in-7-days cooldown cannot apply to `substance` (or `other`) as-is** — at ~2.4 posts/day × 43% substance the chip would be permanently lit; SC.6 must either exempt the two fallback formats or treat them as "no format detected". Record whichever it picks. **(c) `classifyFormat` normalizes at input** (X-stored HTML entities `&gt;`/`&amp;`…, curly apostrophes, t.co URLs stripped) — SC.5/SC.6 pass `posts_published.text`/`scheduled_posts.text` RAW, never pre-normalize. Also: `POST_FORMATS` is exported in cascade order for cell enumeration; the corpus sanity script was run from the scratchpad and deliberately not committed (plan Task 2 "not committed as a test").
- **D145** (SC.1, binds SC.3/SC.4/SC.7): two readings of the plan text resolved in code. **(a) The plan's quotable rule says "a final line ≤8 words ending on a verb" — the verb half was dropped.** Verb detection needs NLP or a word list neither zero-dep nor honest at this size; `quotable` passes on (single line ≤12 words) OR (final content line ≤8 words). This is also what lets the plan's own done-when fixture hold: its last line is "thoughts?", which fires `weak_closer` but must NOT also fire `quotable` or the "exactly three non-pass" bar is unmeetable. **(b) `DEFAULT_LEXICON` ships as empty arrays, not a populated "neutral" list.** A filled generic list re-creates the exact failure the plan's licensing note calls out (a nutrition niche graded on founder vocabulary); the niche-less coach runs entirely on platform/prose rules (decision 7 says those carry ~two thirds of the value) and SC.7 fills the lexicon from niche+channels. Consequence for SC.7: wiring the lexicon changes `concrete_detail` (specificTerms) and `hook_opener` (tribeTerms vocative) only — those are the two rules that read it. Also note for SC.4: `isReply: true` drops `hook_opener`+`breathing_room` from the checks array entirely (they are skipped, not auto-passed), so a reply's standard mean runs over 18 checks, not 20 — don't hardcode 20 anywhere.
- **D152** (SC.3, binds SC.5/SC.6/SC.8 and JD.6): **the plan contradicts itself on placement, one behaviour of the engine only becomes visible once you RENDER it, and one plan phrase was unimplementable as written.** **(a) Placement: the Edit list says "under the textarea", the How says "below the existing pillar/best-time controls" — those are opposite ends of the tab, and the Edit list wins.** The pillar dropdown lives in the drafter `Section` at the BOTTOM and the best-time hints inside `Section title="Schedule"`, so the How taken literally buries the coach below the Save button, or below the whole drafter. D141 (written later, and binding on three future tasks) says the coach column, cooldown chip and reach line "all sit under the textarea", and that is also the only placement that makes sense: every row the coach prints is fixed in the box directly above it. The How's stated *reason* — "so the primary flow is unchanged" — is honored instead by keeping the block SHORT: non-pass rows only, passes behind the `<details>` the plan itself specifies, which is ~4 lines on an ordinary draft. It sits after the over-limit/URL warns and before the Schedule `Section`, with the other things said about the text. **(b) The band label and the fix rows can disagree, and they do so on the plan's own fixture.** The done-when draft (em-dash + "thoughts?" + 15 raw lines) scores **90 → "top tier"** while showing a red `show_more` fix — because `standard` is the MEAN over 20 hygiene+craft rules, so one fix costs 5 points. This is SC.1's arithmetic working as specified (`min(standard, quality)`, D145) and SC.3 deliberately did NOT paper over it in the UI: the rows are the coach's actual output and the disclaimer already says the score is a floor. **But it binds SC.5 directly — a `coachScoreEffectiveness` bucket keyed on the band will put fix-carrying posts in `top`,** so if the intent is "did the coach's advice help", bucket on the *fix count* (or report both), not on the band alone. Same warning for SC.8: the band is a weak feature. **(c) "no state beyond the memo" cannot coexist with "a ~150ms debounce"** — a debounce is a timer plus a state cell. Implemented as the panel's standard `const t = setTimeout(...); return () => clearTimeout(t)` effect into one `coachInput` state, then `useMemo(() => scoreDraft(coachInput))`. The phrase's real content — *no fetch, no server state, nothing persisted* — holds exactly. Note the debounce is a React-churn guard only: `scoreDraft` is regexes over ≤1120 chars. **(d) Two things checked rather than assumed:** the block is invisible on a blank draft (`scoreDraft('')` is a legal `{score:0, checks:[]}` result — rendering "0 / rework" at an empty Composer would be the coach's worst possible first impression), and `canSubmit` was left byte-untouched, which is how decision 4's "no surface gates on the score" is actually enforced rather than merely intended. **(e) Docs were written here, not deferred to SC.9** — GT.3/GT.4 both updated their tab doc in their own commit and the Wave-3 carry-forward makes that the rule; `docs/composer-tab.md` gained a "The coach (live score and fix list)" section before "Scheduling controls". Its headings are NOT numbered (unlike `today-tab.md`), and the single inbound anchor in the repo (`today-tab.md:268` → `#schedule-time-warnings`) is untouched.
- **D154** (SC.5, binds SC.6/SC.8/JD.7 and any future Playbook cell): **the plan's own falsification test came back negative before it ever reached n≥20, and that is the most useful thing this task produced.** **(a) Both cells ship, but the band-keyed one is a near-constant on this account.** Pulled the prod corpus $0 through the S1 explorer (`GET /x/data/posts_published?limit=200&offset=N`, SC.2's repeatable recipe) and ran both builders locally over 109 originals: format = substance 47% / list 15% / cta 14% / story 10% (D146's substance-43% holds); **coach band = `top` 97%, `ship` 3%, `rework` and `almost` ZERO, lowest score in the entire corpus 83, and the only `fix` rules that fire anywhere are `url_cost` (6) and `answerable_question` (1)**. So the band axis cannot produce a spread — two bands would have to clear n≥20 and only one is populated — while the fix axis is essentially "did this post carry a URL". **This is exactly D152(b)'s warning arriving as data, which is why the fix-count split (`clean`/`flagged`) shipped BESIDE the bands rather than instead of them.** Binds **SC.8** directly: the coach score is not a weak feature, it is a **constant** — do not fit a reach model on it. Binds **JD.7**: an LLM-judge cell keyed on a score band would inherit the same degeneracy. **(b) The spread NAMES the two bands it compared, which is a deviation from the plan's "top and bottom bands".** Taken literally (`top` vs `rework`) the cell would be permanently silent on any corpus like the one above — the falsification test could never run. It compares the highest and lowest GATED bands and reports `spreadBands: {high, low}` so the UI can say which, e.g. "top tier vs ship it". Same sentence satisfies the literal reading whenever `top`/`rework` are the gated pair. **(c) `loadMediaRows` was WIDENED AND RENAMED, not duplicated** — the plan's How says "reuse the existing measured-rows loader … add the column rather than a second query", and once `text` rides along the name is a lie: it is now `loadOriginalPostRows` and one load feeds three cells, which is also what makes "the three axes see the same population" assertable in a test rather than merely intended. Its second caller is `routes/digest.ts` (§S4). **(d) The bands are NOT re-cut in the Playbook.** `scoreDraft`'s `toBand` owns the cut points; deriving `<45/45–59/60–84/85+` locally (as the plan text spells them) would grade a different number than the Composer showed the moment either side moves — the UI.7 badge-vs-gate fork, one surface later. The plan's cut points and `toBand`'s are identical today, which is exactly when the duplicate is cheapest to avoid. **(e) The `postFormat` extension shim landed here, at the first panel consumer** (the module header predicted it): `extension/src/postFormat.ts` top-level (a bare re-export — `extension/src/shared/` is only for a shim that ADDS, per the SC.3 gotcha) + the 5th `tsconfig.app.json` `include` entry. **SC.6 inherits it and needs no shim work.** `shared/types.ts` now imports `CoachBand`/`PostFormat` from the shims, which is the pre-existing `TweetSignals` precedent, not new ground.
- **D153** (SC.4, binds SC.6/SC.8/JD.6 and SC.7): **the plan's edit list is two surfaces old, and the one thing SC.3 left for this task lives in a file this task was not supposed to open.** **(a) Four variant pickers exist now, not one.** The plan says "the panel's reply editor" + `Radar.tsx`, written when only `Replies.tsx` rendered variants; since then `Conversations.tsx` (UI.12 lane) and `LaunchRoom.tsx` (GT.3) render the identical `.reply-variant` picker, and RD.2 moved Radar's angles into in-card tabs. All four got the chip. Shipping two of four would have left the same widget scored on one tab and unscored on the next, for a component that is four lines per call site once it exists — and the codemap's own chip-taxonomy rule (a shared class is a cross-lane object) says these are one surface, not four. **(b) The tone maps had to leave `Composer.tsx`, which is the D141 lane's file.** The SC.3 gotcha states the intent outright ("`COACH_TONE`/`COACH_BAND_TONE` are module-level maps precisely so SC.4's reply chips can lift them"), and the alternatives were both worse: duplicating the maps re-creates the badge-vs-gate fork UI.7 lived through, and importing them *from* `Composer.tsx` makes a leaf component depend on a tab. They moved to the new `sidepanel/CoachChip.tsx`; the Composer's edit is **two import lines and a comment — no JSX, no `.coach` container change**, so SC.6/SC.8/JD.6 still read the block exactly as SC.3 left it. If a later task wants the tones, import from `CoachChip.tsx`, do not re-declare. **(c) `isReply: true` is fixed inside the component, not a prop.** Every call site is a reply picker; the Composer's own AI.8 rewrite variants are POSTS and were deliberately left unscored (grading them needs `isReply:false` and a decision about where the chip sits in a `.draft-card` — a separate task, flagged not silently skipped). **(d) The chip renders `null` on an empty draft** — same reasoning as SC.3's hidden-when-blank block; `scoreDraft('')` is a legal `{score:0, checks:[]}`, and a red 0 next to an empty variant is a verdict on nothing. **(e) The tooltip carries `COACH_DISCLAIMER`**, because a chip has no room for a visible floor-not-target line and the tooltip is the only copy this surface owns — rendered from the export, never retyped (SC.1's instruction). **(f) Deliberately NOT done:** `content.ts`'s on-page chip strip, per the plan's own How ("a score there is noise at the moment of pasting", RU.7's light action row) — and it would have meant a fifth surface inside the IIFE bundle.
- **D155** (SC.7, binds SC.6/SC.8/JD.6 and any future lexicon work): **two plan items were already paid, one plan source does not exist, and the derivation is asymmetric on purpose.** **(a) The two term lists are built by opposite rules, because their errors cost opposite amounts.** `specificTerms` decides `concrete_detail` (via `hasAnchor`) — a term that fires on every draft silently DISABLES the check, so terms enter **whole and unsplit**, only from lists the operator wrote as terms (channel `keywords`, pillar slugs); a kebab slug like `ai-craft` then simply never matches, which is the safe direction, while a one-word slug like `pricing` does. `tribeTerms` decides one narrow branch of `hook_opener` — the draft must literally OPEN with `"<term>,"` (the `"<term>:"` form already passes on the colon alone) — so labels ARE word-split there (≥4 chars, stopword-filtered, letters only), because a generous list costs almost nothing and a missing audience noun costs a false nudge. Both capped at 200 sorted terms: `concrete_detail` runs one regex per specific term **per keystroke**. **(b) The plan's "persona nouns" source does not exist without NLP** — the persona is prose (see `DEFAULT_NICHE`), and there is no zero-dep honest way to pull nouns out of it. Labels do that job (niche + pillars + channels), which is also why "deactivating everything" leaves `specificTerms` empty but `tribeTerms` holding the niche label's words — the done-when's "still returns the default set" is satisfied in spirit (the coach falls back to the rules that need no niche), not literally. **(c) The plan's `scoreDraft` test was already shipped by SC.1** (`postCoach.test.ts:270`, 'lexicon override: a specific term counts as concrete detail'). The genuinely new coverage is the end-to-end one: a keyword inserted into `channels` reaches the route payload AND flips `concrete_detail` when that payload is fed back into `scoreDraft` — which is the only assertion that would catch the route and the engine disagreeing about term shape. **(d) The lexicon is read INSIDE `CoachChip`, not passed to it.** The plan's edit list names "the replies surface" from before D153 made it four pickers; a `lexicon` prop would have meant four call-site edits plus a `settings` prop for a leaf component, which is how a shared widget forks. So the panel cache loads its own `getSettings()` (the `Niche.tsx` precedent; `api.request` ignores the argument anyway). Consequence: `CoachChip` is now a hook-using component — it can never be called as a plain function. **(e) No registry knob, no mirror key.** The lexicon is derived data, not a setting; the TTL is the only tuning and it is a constant. Registry stays **14 groups / 61 knobs, 27 mirrored, MCP 23**.
- **D156** (SC.6, binds SC.8/SC.9/GT.9/JD.7 and every future ported constant): **the plan's headline mechanism was falsified before it was written, the fallback question D146(b) handed forward is answered, and one plan artefact was deliberately not built.** **(a) The Jaccard idea-clustering is NOT ported, and the reason is a measurement.** The plan (and x-builder) cluster each format's window by stop-worded token-set Jaccard **≥0.45** so that four *different* questions don't read as repetition. Pulled the prod corpus $0 (`GET /x/data/posts_published?limit=200&offset=N`, the SC.2/SC.5 recipe — 1,084 rows, **137 originals**, 2026-06-21→07-27) and measured every same-format pair inside a 7-day window: **795 pairs, max 0.238, p99 0.115, p95 0.077, median 0.000 — ZERO pairs at ≥0.25, let alone 0.45.** A 280-character post carries a median of **15** content tokens, so two posts making the same point share almost none of them; every cluster is a singleton and a cluster-keyed counter is **silent forever**. Worse, at any threshold this corpus does reach, the top scorers are provably *distinct* ideas ("Nobody is coming to build your app" 0.238 "Someone else's launch going viral"), so a lowered bar would fire on shared vocabulary rather than on repetition. It ships as a **format tally** instead, which is the axis nothing else measures — and idea-repetition is not lost, it already has an owner: `monitor.ts`'s `nearDuplicate` (word-SHINGLE Jaccard ≥0.8 over 14 days). **This is SC decision 1 applied to the SC plan's own imported number** ("their table is a guess; ours will be a measurement or it will be absent"), and it is now codemap §7 rule **33**. **The cost is stated rather than hidden: the plan's done-when "four dissimilar questions do not [warn]" is NOT met — four questions are four questions.** A test asserts that outcome explicitly rather than leaving it to be discovered. **(b) D146(b) is answered by exemption, and the exempt set is THREE, not the two the plan names.** `substance`, `one_liner` AND `other` all mean *no format detected* (D146a's own split), so warning about them would be a lie as well as permanent — measured lit-rate for `substance` is **97% of windows**. Their cells still ship (the tally is worth reading) carrying **`exempt: true` and a pinned `clear` status**, so no consumer has to keep a copy of the list — the panel filters on `status !== 'clear'` and can never fork from the server about what counts as a shape. **(c) There is NO extension shim and no 6th `tsconfig.app.json` include.** §7.3's shim rule is for a module the extension *uses*; the panel reads finished cells off the route and only needs the wire shape, which it mirrors in `shared/types.ts` (the `CoachLexiconResponse` precedent, not the `postFormat` one). The module header says what would change that. **(d) `count` is the plain format tally, and the thresholds stay 4/2.** With clustering gone the count is unambiguous, so the plan's "report the largest cluster's count" has no referent; the measured lit-rates are recorded in the module beside the constants (audience_cta 68% / list 43% / story 35% cooldown, hot_take 48% warming) so a future recalibration has a baseline instead of a vibe. They are high on purpose — the line is **contingent**, rendering only while you draft that shape, which is the one moment the count is worth knowing. **(e) Two things checked rather than assumed:** the route payload was printed out-of-band ($0, in-memory mount — hot_take 4→cooldown, question 2→warming, substance 4→clear+exempt, `?days=2` narrows, `?days=0` 400s) and the built bundle greps confirm Vite inlined the copy and the CSS class. **(f) Binds GT.9 and SC.9:** D142 already tells GT.9's smoke to drop its cooldown assertion — SC.9's smoke owns the arithmetic, and what it should assert is the **exempt invariant** (`exempt ⇒ clear`) and a seeded 4-in-window flip, never a raw count over a shared corpus.
- **D161** (SC.8, binds SC.9/JD.7/JD.6 and every future aggregate over `metrics_snapshots`): **the plan named two house readers to copy and both are wrong for this quantity — that is the task's real output, and it is the FIFTH time in this wave a borrowed input lost to a measurement (D156a/D157a/D158a/D159a).** **(a) `latestOutcomes`' latest-snapshot-per-tweet is a SELECTED sample.** 25 of 135 measured originals carry a second snapshot and those 25 are the winner re-reads — their median views at the FIRST snapshot is already **1,059 against 122** for the single-snapshot rows. So "latest" is two measurement protocols, and which one a post gets is decided by whether it won; using it puts that selection straight into the numerator, plus days of extra accrual (latest ages reach p90 = 10,883 min; first ages are p5 = 111 / p95 = 1,311). The route's `loadReachRows()` therefore orders `asc(snapshotAt)` and takes first-seen — the ONLY loader in the repo that inverts the house pattern, with a comment saying why. **Generalized as codemap §7 rule 34: "latest row wins" is right for displaying one row and wrong for comparing many, whenever the re-read is selective.** **(b) `buildBestTimes`' `views*1440/age` normalization is falsified by our own corpus and was NOT ported.** It assumes linear accrual; views are front-loaded. Posts snapshotted under 12 h old and at 12–27 h carry effectively the SAME raw view count (median **129 vs 121**, 6%) while their normalized rates differ by **170%** (452 vs 167/day) — so normalizing manufactures the very spread the fit is meant to detect, and one post read at 4 minutes old produced a **696×** multiplier. `buildBestTimes` survives this because it averages ≥3 posts per cell and reports a rate; dividing one rate by another compounds it. Shipped instead: the population is restricted to the daily pass's own documented 3–27h window (§8.4) and raw counts are compared directly. Rows outside are DROPPED, not rescaled. **(c) The gate is clean and the answer is zero.** 138 originals → 127 population → 107 fittable, live base **334 views**; per-format n is substance 46, audience_cta 17, list 16, story 16, hot_take 5. The only bucket with the sample is `substance` — "no format detected" (D146a) — **exempt here for exactly SC.6's reason (D156b), the same three-format set**, because a band keyed on the residual bucket is a number attached to "unknown" (§7.11). So the feature ships rendering the insufficient line for every draft and arms itself as audience_cta/list/story cross 20. That is SC decision 9 working, not a shortfall — but say it out loud to whoever reads the panel, which is why `docs/composer-tab.md` states it. **(d) Four plan-text items resolved smaller than written, each because it had no consumer or no support.** `replyRate` in the fit output was dropped (nothing renders it — no pre-stubbed surface); `buildReachBand({text, …})` takes `format` instead, since the Composer already memoizes `draftFormat` and re-classifying inside would be a second source of truth; the plan's **`escapeRange` shipped as `escapeThreshold` + `escapeProbability`** — the top of an escape is where the sample is thinnest (one format's max is 22.7× off 17 rows), so an upper bound would be the least-supported number on the surface while looking like the most exciting one; and `?format=` became a whole-table payload read once per mount, because the draft's format changes per keystroke while the fit only moves when a post is measured (the `GET /posts/cooldowns` division of labour). **(e) An exempt format renders NOTHING, not the insufficient line.** The plan's done-when says "the insufficient line otherwise", but for `substance`/`one_liner`/`other` that line would promise a band that can never arrive — they are off the axis, not below the gate. At ~47% of this account's originals it would also be the single most-shown line in the box. **(f) No registry knob and no shim.** The gate reuses `x.gates.minCellN` (registry stays **14 groups / 61 knobs, 27 mirrored, MCP 23**) and the panel reads finished cells, so there is no `extension/src/reach.ts` and no 6th `tsconfig.app.json` include — the D156c precedent, not the `postFormat` one.

### Gotchas (SC.1 → SC.8)

- **SC.7: the coach's lexicon arrives ASYNC, and two rules change under you.** Anything grading a draft in the panel must take the lexicon from `useCoachLexicon()` — never `DEFAULT_LEXICON` and never a locally fetched copy, or two surfaces will disagree about whether the same sentence has a concrete detail. The first paint of a cold panel legitimately grades with the empty default and re-renders ~1 request later; that is by design (the score sharpens, it doesn't appear), so a test or a screenshot taken at t=0 sees the pre-lexicon score. Server-side callers use `loadActiveCoachLexicon()` from `routes/coach.ts` (never re-derive). And the payload is only as good as the operator's curation: with no channel keywords and multi-word pillar slugs, `specificTerms` is legitimately EMPTY — that is not a bug, and `concrete_detail` then behaves exactly as it did before SC.7.
- **SC.2 — facts for SC.5/SC.6:** the classifier's branches carry one why-comment each and the ORDER is the contract — `postFormat.test.ts` pins 8 ordering regressions (hot_take beats question, poll_list beats binary_choice, milestone beats audience_cta, data_comparison beats story, story beats list, RT beats everything), so "fixing" a misclassification by reordering reddens those first: add/tighten a predicate instead. Fixture rows are REAL posts stored verbatim (entities escaped, truncated texts kept as stored) — don't "clean them up". The corpus pull is repeatable: `GET /x/data/posts_published?limit=200&offset=N` (S1 explorer, $0), filter `is_reply=0`, 136 originals as of 2026-07-27. See D146 for the substance-43% consequence and the cooldown exemption SC.6 owes.
- **SC.3 — facts for SC.4 (the next `postCoach` consumer), SC.6/SC.8 (the same file) and SC.9's smoke:** **the $0 way to verify a panel that renders a pure module is to push a fixture through the SHIM, not the canonical module** — a `bun -e` importing from `extension/src/postCoach.ts` and printing the exact rows the component will map over. It proves the re-export, the tsconfig `include`, and the rendered shape in one second, and it is what turned up D152(b). Pair it with three greps over the BUILT bundle (`dist/sidepanel.js` for a copy string + a tone class, `dist/assets/sidepanel-*.css` for the class) — that is the only check that proves **Vite inlined the out-of-tree module**, which neither typecheck nor `bun test` can tell you. **Shim mechanics:** the fourth out-of-tree module is a 4th `extension/tsconfig.app.json` `include` entry; forgetting it fails the extension typecheck loudly, so it cannot ship half-done. `extension/src/postCoach.ts` sits at the top level (the `replyBand.ts`/`channelSuggest.ts` form), not under `extension/src/shared/` — that directory is for a shim that ADDS to its re-export (`activeTimes.ts` bolts on the DOM extractor); a bare re-export goes top-level. **Component facts:** `Composer.tsx` has no test file (panel convention) so SC.3 added none and the suite is 1649 either way — automated coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, lint, plus SC.1's 36 engine tests; **the browser-verify is owed (see next-up).** `COACH_TONE`/`COACH_BAND_TONE` are module-level maps precisely so SC.4's reply chips can lift them instead of re-deriving a colour scale — a second tone vocabulary for the same engine is how the badge and the gate forked in UI.7's world. And `COACH_DISCLAIMER`/`COACH_BAND_LABEL` are rendered from the exports verbatim, never retyped (SC.1's instruction): reword the floor-not-target sentence in ONE place and every surface moves.
- **SC.4 — facts for SC.6/SC.7/SC.9 and any future coach surface:** **a `.tsx` in `extension/src/sidepanel/` IS importable from a `bun:test` file** — bun transpiles the JSX and resolves `react/jsx-runtime` from `extension/node_modules` by walking up from the file, so a pure helper that lives beside a component does not have to be exiled to a `.ts` to be tested (`CoachChip.test.ts` imports `./CoachChip.tsx` and the suite is green). That is new ground: every other extension test imports a `.ts`. It does NOT make the component testable — there is no DOM renderer here — so the split stays "pure helper tested, JSX manually verified", and NT.1 still applies (an extension `.test.ts` is checked by `bun run test` alone). **The $0 verification recipe, applied to a component:** import the module in `bun -e`, feed it a fixture, print the string the user will hover — that is how the fix-before-nudge ordering was checked before writing the assertion rather than after, and it is what turned up that a reply grades over **27** checks (29 minus `hook_opener`+`breathing_room`, D145b). Then three greps over the BUILT bundle (`dist/sidepanel.js` for `coach-chip` + a tone class, `dist/assets/sidepanel-*.css` for the class) — the only check that proves Vite kept it. **Copy facts:** the chip's tooltip is `N/100 · band` + at most two labels + `COACH_DISCLAIMER`; `CoachChip.test.ts` asserts the two-issue cap and `toEndWith(COACH_DISCLAIMER)`, so adding a third line or retyping the disclaimer reddens it. **For SC.7:** filling `DEFAULT_LEXICON` changes what these chips say on every reply picker at once — there is no per-surface lexicon and the chips pass no `lexicon` argument, so wiring SC.7 into `scoreDraft`'s default is the only lever, and it moves four surfaces plus the Composer in one commit.
- **SC.5 — facts for SC.6/SC.8/JD.7 and the next Playbook cell:** **the $0 way to check a new Playbook cell against reality is to pull the corpus, not to deploy** — a `bun -e` that fetches `GET /x/data/posts_published?limit=200&offset=N` from prod with the `.env` bearer (S1 explorer, $0, SC.2's recipe), filters `is_reply=0`, and runs the new `build*` locally at `minN=1`. It answered the plan's "eyeball the distribution" step in one command and produced D154(a). **Note the pull is capped by your loop bound** — 600 rows gave 109 originals vs SC.2's 136, so a share is comparable but a count is not. **Local `./stratus.db` has ZERO published originals** (prod data lives on the Hetzner host), so mounting the router against it only proves the empty-corpus path; it is still worth doing — that path is what a fresh install sees. **Route-test facts:** the two new cells are partitions over the WHOLE `posts_published` corpus, and other test files seed originals into the shared in-memory DB, so route assertions are **partition invariants** (Σposted = totalPosted, Σn = totalMeasured, clean+flagged = the same totals) plus **before/after deltas** — never absolutes, the way the media test's exact `n: 2` can be. The SC.5 describe is declared **LAST in the file on purpose**: it inserts originals and the media test above asserts exact bucket counts over the same table, and describes run in declaration order. Its seeds are `retired: true` (NT.7). **Panel facts:** the two sections have no test file (panel convention); coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, lint, and the 13 new pure/route tests — **browser-verify owed (next-up item 0e)**. The bundle greps that prove Vite inlined the new shim: `dist/sidepanel.js` for `Would-you-rather` (a `FORMAT_LABELS` value) and for the section copy.
- **SC.6 — facts for SC.8/SC.9 and anything that ships a counter:** **the $0 way to tell a useful counter from a permanently-lit one is a ROLLING-WINDOW REPLAY over the real corpus, and it is three lines on top of the SC.5 pull** — for every day of history, take the window ending that day, bucket it, and print the share of windows in each bucket that would have warned. A fixture proves arithmetic; only the replay proves *calibration*, and it is what produced both halves of D156 (the 0.238-vs-0.45 kill and the 68/43/35% lit-rates now recorded beside the constants). Run it BEFORE the thresholds reach a test file. **Route facts:** `GET /x/posts/cooldowns` lives on the calendar router but its api-client home is `api.coach.cooldowns` — namespacing there follows the FEATURE, not the path (`drafts.generate` → `/x/posts/draft` is the same shape), so don't go looking for a `posts` namespace. Its population is `posts_published`, so on `./stratus.db` locally it returns `{cells: []}` — prod data lives on the Hetzner host (the SC.5 fact, still true), and the empty-corpus path is exactly what a fresh install sees. **Test facts:** the route describe is in `calendar.test.ts`, seeds `posts_published` with `retired: true` (NT.7) and cleans up in a **try/finally inside each test** as well as the file's `afterAll` (which now drains a shared `publishedIds` array rather than the single `DUPE_ID`) — the window is "the last N days from now", which is precisely where other suites' fixtures live, so every count assertion is a **delta against a baseline read**, never an absolute. The pure tests assert their own premises first (`classifyFormat(fixture) === 'hot_take'`), so a classifier change reddens the premise instead of silently re-labelling the arithmetic under it. **Panel facts:** no test file (panel convention); coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, lint, the 14 pure + 3 route tests, and the two bundle greps (`dist/sidepanel.js` for `A count of what went out`, `dist/assets/sidepanel-*.css` for `coach-cooldown`). **Browser-verify owed (next-up item 0g).** The `.coach` box now reads, in order: head → cooldown line → fix rows → nudge rows → `<details>` passing → disclaimer. SC.8's reach line appends INSIDE it (D141), and `canSubmit` is STILL byte-untouched — three tasks in this lane have now added a number to this file without one of them being allowed to gate on it.
- **SC.1 — facts for SC.3/SC.4/SC.5:** `scoreDraft` returns checks in fixed rule order (hygiene→craft→signal) with a label on EVERY status — pass labels exist because the Composer column collapses passes but still lists them; render non-pass first by filtering, not by re-sorting ids. `COACH_DISCLAIMER` and `COACH_BAND_LABEL` are exported precisely so SC.3's UI copy is not retyped (decision 4's floor-not-target sentence must be identical everywhere). The `url_cost` fix-label contains the literal `$0.20` — `postCoach.test.ts` asserts `toContain('$0.20')`, so rewording that copy reddens the suite. `wordCount` counts only tokens containing a letter/digit (a bare "—" is not a word); tests that hand-count words must match that rule.
- **SC.8 — facts for SC.9, JD.6/JD.7 and anything that aggregates `metrics_snapshots`:** **the $0 recipe that produced this task's whole result is a FIRST-vs-LATEST pull, and it is one `POST /x/data/query`.** Select `public_metrics`/`age_at_snapshot_min` twice per row — once `ORDER BY snapshot_at ASC LIMIT 1`, once `DESC` — plus `COUNT(*)`, then look at whether the two cohorts differ. Here `snaps` came back `{1: 110, 2: 25}` and the 25 two-snapshot rows had 10× the views: that single line is what exposed the winner re-read, and no fixture would ever have shown it. **Do this before trusting ANY aggregate that reads "the latest snapshot" across posts.** Second recipe, equally cheap: to test whether a normalization is legitimate, bucket the corpus by the thing you are normalizing OUT and check the result is flat. `views*1440/age` gave 452/day under 12 h and 167/day at 12–27 h — not flat, therefore not a normalization — while the RAW counts it was 'correcting' were 129 vs 121. **Fixture design worth copying:** to assert exact quantiles against a trailing-median baseline, pad so that 15+ of every 20-row window sit at one value (here: 3 filler posts after each test post). The base is then pinned and a post's ratio is `views / base` by construction, so p25/p50/p75 can be asserted exactly instead of approximately — and the fillers are deliberately an EXEMPT format so they can never produce a competing fitted cell. **Route facts:** `GET /coach/reach` sits on SC.7's `routes/coach.ts` (second static path, §7.20 moot) but the population is `posts_published` + `metrics_snapshots`, so on local `./stratus.db` it returns all-insufficient cells — prod lives on Hetzner (the SC.5/SC.6 fact, still true), and that empty path is what a fresh install sees. The route describe is declared LAST in `coach.test.ts` because it inserts originals, and its assertion is a **delta** (`measuredPosts` moves by exactly 2), never an absolute — three other suites seed that table. **Panel facts:** no test file (panel convention); coverage is root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, lint, the 14 new tests, and three bundle greps (`dist/sidepanel.js` for `reach band: insufficient data` and `usually `, `dist/assets/sidepanel-*.css` for `coach-reach`). **Browser-verify owed.** The `.coach` box now reads: head → cooldown → **reach** → fix rows → nudge rows → `<details>` passing → disclaimer, and `canSubmit` is STILL byte-untouched after four tasks added numbers to this file. **For SC.9's smoke:** the assertion that matters here is the same shape as GT.9's — not a count, but the *contract*: seed a post whose first snapshot is in-window and whose latest is not, and prove it COUNTS; then prove no `insufficient` cell carries a number. Both are unspendable and both are false under the implementation the plan originally asked for.


### Also archived at SC.9 — two discharged bookkeeping entries

> `D113a` (GR.10's own archive-pass accounting) and the 2026-07-23 compaction gotcha. Both describe passes that are done; the standing rule they encoded now lives in STATE.md's size-discipline paragraph and in **D162**, and "grep the archive by task id or filename" is stated in three places there.

- **D113a** (GR.10, bookkeeping): the archive pass moved **20 D-entries (D93–D112, D97 excepted)**, **16 gotchas (HV.1 → GR.9)** and **7 header priors (GR.4 → GR.9)** into `STATE-ARCHIVE.md` verbatim. STATE.md went ~128 KB → **~50 KB** (one `Read` again); the archive is now ~350 KB (grep-only by design). What was deliberately kept live out of the closed lanes: the **carry-forward gotcha** below (the four HV/GR facts that still bind an open task) and nothing else.
- **STATE.md was compacted 2026-07-23 (out-of-skill housekeeping, no code touched).** It had reached **339 KB** — past the `Read` tool's 256 KB cap, so Step 0's "read STATE.md fully" silently became a chunked-read investigation every session. 96 closed-wave D-entries + 127 closed-wave gotchas + the header's HV/GR prior paragraphs moved **verbatim** into the new `STATE-ARCHIVE.md` (frozen, 269 KB, grep-only — bigger than one `Read` by design); the live file is **74 KB**. The split was generated by line number, not retyped, and verified as a set operation: every non-empty original line lands in **exactly one** of the two files (438/438, zero duplicated). **What this means for you:** a task touching a file Waves 0–2 built should `grep STATE-ARCHIVE.md` for the task id or filename before assuming the live file tells the whole story — e.g. the RL `/use` contract (D79), the NT.5 icon prefixes (D89), the ST registry contract. SKILL.md gained the matching archive rule in Step 6 (a lane's docs-sync task archives that lane) and its Step-0 staleness guard now compares the **subject line**, matching D97 — it still said `--format=%h` five sessions after shas stopped being recorded.

### Also archived at SC.9 — the three oldest browser-debt gotchas, verbatim

> Their *unpaid* half was distilled into `VERIFY-DEBT.md` item **(4)** (a debt parked in a facts list reads as a fact). Kept verbatim here so nothing is lost in the distillation.

- **UI.9 light theme is an OPENING-GUESS palette, unverified in a browser.** Automated gates pass (632 pass / 0 fail incl. 6 new storage tests, typecheck + build + changed-file lint green) but the 12-tabs × {dark,light} × {cozy,compact} visual QA matrix is explicitly manual and deferred to the Wave-5 polish passes (UI.12–16 done-whens). Known trap (plan risk note): AA contrast on warn/ok/band **text over tinted fills** in light — the fill tokens and semantic ink were re-authored dark-enough to double as text (`--strat-warn: #9a6a10`, band-hot `rgb(0,150,100)`, band-warm-text `#8a6100`), but spot-check before trusting. Adjust `:root[data-theme='light']` values, not the consuming rules.
- **UI.10 rail regrouping is UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER** (like UI.9's palette). Extension typecheck + Vite build + changed-file lint are green and the CSS compiles, but the visual QA (grouped rail spacing, brandmark, EmptyState, popover) rides the Wave-5 polish passes (UI.12–16 done-whens), per D7/D14.
- **RL.5 is UNVERIFIED IN A BROWSER**, like every panel component since UI.9 — no tests were added (repo convention: panels are untested, logic lives server-side). Automated gates only: root+extension typecheck, `cd extension && bun run build`, changed-file lint, full suite 1087/0. The plan's live done-whens (create → add → test-render → generate → Overwrite) ride RL.8's smoke + the Wave-5 QA pass. The one server-behavior assumption the UI leans on was **not** left to faith — the `{humanizer:{}}` → defaults round-trip was proven with a throwaway route test (D81b).

## Archived at JD.2 (2026-07-27) — four blocks that stopped binding an open task

Moved verbatim from STATE.md so the file stays a one-`Read` job while the JD lane
grows it. Nothing here was rewritten. Grep by task id (UI.2–UI.7, UI.17, SC.9) or by
filename (`registry.ts`, `serverSettings.ts`).

### The Wave-5 registry carry-forward (UI.2–UI.7, ten items) — moved from STATE.md line 134
- **Wave-5 registry carry-forward (the UI.2–UI.7 facts that still bind an open task — the five per-task gotcha blocks + D128–D133 are in `STATE-ARCHIVE.md` §"Archived at UI.11", grep by task id):** **(1) The store is process-global across test files.** One in-memory `app_settings`, one module cache — any test that `setSettings(...)` MUST reset in `afterEach`/`afterAll` (`resetSettings({group})` or `{keys}`), and keeping a suite's PATCH assertions inside ONE registry group means a single group-reset cleans them. **(2) A knob's blast radius can exceed its caller.** PATCHing a knob whose consumer is a global read-that-writes (the `/radar/drafts` TTL expires *every* stale row) means seeding your own fixture and only ever tightening the window; a smoke does it by snapshot-restoring `resolveSetting(key)` by `isDefault`, never `resetSettings` (which DELETES an override the operator set on purpose) — and hangs the restore off `fail()` too, since `fail()` exits. **(3) An env-backed registry default is read ONCE, at registry import** — a runtime `process.env.X = …` in a test or script is a silent no-op that still looks like it works. Steer through `setSettings`. **(4) `appliesOn` is behavior, not copy:** `'restart'` **only** where reading later can't take effect (the knob armed a timer — just `x.workers.dailyMetricsHourUtc`/`publisherIntervalSec`); everything else reads per request/run even inside a worker, and **no money knob is ever restart-scoped**. Getting this backwards makes a knob look broken. **(5) The `?param=` vs knob contract:** a query param overrides the configured baseline **per read**, in both directions (`?minN=20` re-tightens a store-loosened gate) — assert both, as `playbook.test.ts` does. **(6) A knob whose LOOSENING direction spends can only be route-tested on the refusal side** — `/x/replies/generate` mounts whenever any LLM key is configured and `.env` has one, so "PATCH → it gets through" runs a real paid Grok call inside `bun run test`. Assert a tightening that 422s, and prove the store was read by picking a context that refuses under BOTH settings so the reported `band` changes; the permissive direction belongs in the pure suite. **(7) Adding a `scope:'mirrored'` key is a 2-file move** — the registry def, then a `ServerConfig` field + `SERVER_DEFAULTS` fallback in `extension/src/shared/serverSettings.ts`; `registry.test.ts` asserts the mirrored list EXACTLY (**17 keys when this was written; 27 as of UI.15** — and `extension/src/shared/serverSettings.test.ts` separately asserts `Object.keys(ServerConfig)` plus the full-blob round-trip, so one knob is a 2-file move that reddens 3 assertions until both halves land) so the second file can't be forgotten (without it the key ships in the blob and is never read — no type error, no test failure). **(8) A cap arriving asynchronously must be applied to a SPENT counter, not snapshotted as REMAINING** — any mirrored knob feeding an initial `useState` has the `Conversations.tsx` trap. **(9) The AI layer has TWO legitimate tiers and they are not duplicates:** `x.ai.*` (per-surface house default) and the `ai` blob from Settings → AI (global override, `null` = defer). "Unifying" them silently drops the per-surface knobs out of the merge. **(10) Two infra facts:** the `.claude/skills/stratus/scripts/` files are linted but NOT typechecked (root `tsconfig.json` covers the repo-root `scripts/`, not the skill's) — verified only by running them; and Bun auto-loads `.env` into `process.env`, so `env -u API_TOKEN bun run …` does NOT unset it (the skill script's `STRATUS_API_TOKEN ?? API_TOKEN` mapping exists for that).

### UI.17 five facts — moved from STATE.md line 136 (items 1 and 2 are restated in D160(b)/D162(b); 3-5 stayed live)
- **UI.17 — five facts for whoever opens the next plan.** **(1) A closing docs-sync is mostly a VERIFICATION pass, and the way to scope it is `git log --name-only -- docs/`.** Six polish tasks had already rewritten twelve tab docs in their own commits; guessing would have meant either re-reading all twelve or trusting a plan written eight days earlier. One command answered it, and the residue was a single owed section. **(2) Assert a plan's BAR, not today's number.** The smoke checks "≥12 groups / ≥55 knobs" (the plan's done-when) and prints the real 14/61. Had it asserted 61, the next knob would redden a script that has nothing to do with knobs — the same trap the exact-count tests in `registry.test.ts` accept **on purpose** (there the exactness IS the point: a mirrored key with no `ServerConfig` field must fail). Know which kind of count you are writing. **(3) A monotone assertion plus a printed denominator beats a fixture, when the fixture would drag invariants along.** `sufficient` counts across three gates prove the gate is applied without seeding a single row — and printing "0 of 21 gated cells" is what keeps a zero honest. Reach for this shape whenever the thing under test is a *parameter* and the data belongs to someone else's smoke. **(4) A settings knob whose loosening direction spends can still be smoked safely from the tightening side** — `x.budgets.imageDailyUsd: 0` makes `spent >= budget` true at $0 spend, so the 429 fires with no `cost_events` fixture at all (`smoke-studio.ts` seeds one because it needs a *nonzero* cap for a different assertion). The XAI key is faked **only when absent** and restored either way; biome forbids `delete process.env.X` in `scripts/`, so the restore assigns `?? ''`. **(5) `bun run <script>` buffers stdout when it is not a TTY.** A run that hangs writes an EMPTY output file, so "no output" tells you nothing about where it stopped — `console.error` (or `2>&1 | head`) is how you bisect a stuck script here. Cost twenty minutes on a hang that turned out to be transient.

### The SC.9 archive-pass write-up — moved from STATE.md line 13 (its lesson survives as size-discipline lesson 3)
**✓ Archive passes paid: GR.10, UI.11, UI.17, GT.9, and SC.9 (2026-07-27).** SC.9 took **105 KB → 61 KB** (57 KB after the move itself; this task's own D-entry, two gotchas and prior add ~5 KB back, and five discharged entries came out) in one move, by line number, never retyped, verified as a set operation (the D113a precedent — 135 non-empty lines in = 108 kept + 27 moved, 0 missing, 0 duplicated): the SC lane's **11 header priors** + **D145/D146/D152–D156/D161** + the **8 per-task gotchas** → `STATE-ARCHIVE.md` §"Archived at SC.9". **The interesting part is what almost stopped the pass:** six of those eight D-entries name an OPEN JD task in their own header line, so the literal reading of the archive rule ("leave what still binds an open task") would have kept the whole lane live and saved nothing. The binding is real but it is *small* — a handful of facts inside 19 KB of narration — so it was **distilled** into one `SC carry-forward` gotcha below plus the two hot-file-lock rows, and the entries went to the archive whole. **That is the generalizable move: a closing pass distils, it does not choose between keeping everything and losing something.** This file is a one-`Read` job again for the first time since GT.2.

The `include` list is now **six**; that entry is what makes the out-of-tree path typecheck at all, and omitting it fails `cd extension && bun run typecheck` with a confusing rootDir error. **Two test-shape facts, both of which cost a red gate here:** (1) `expect(a).toEqual(b)` types `b` from `a`, so comparing `Object.keys(x)` (`string[]`) against a `readonly JudgeDimension[]` must put the **string side first** — the reverse is a TS2769 overload error, not a test failure; (2) biome's `organizeImports` sorts SCREAMING_CASE before `type` imports before camelCase inside one brace group, which no eyeballing gets right — `bunx biome check --write <file>`, and it is an error not a warning. **A drift check TS cannot do:** `JUDGE_DIMENSIONS` and the `JudgeScores` interface are two hand-maintained lists of the same 13 keys and the compiler checks one direction only — sorted-`Object.keys` equality over a fully-populated fixture is what makes adding a key to one and not the other a red test. Reuse that shape for any list-plus-interface pair.

Topping the block back up with machine rows would re-create exactly the loop the task exists to break. **(c) `posts_published.source` is a trap** — it is `'scheduled'`\|`'manual'` (the publish path), not authorship; the only column that answers "who wrote this" is `scheduled_posts.source` (`'api'` default vs `'drafter'`, the only two values any writer produces), reached through `posted_tweet_id`, which the publisher AND the manual reconcile both stamp. **(d) The plan's "verify `ideas.ts` still satisfied" step was free** — `WinnerPost[]` is unchanged, and there is now a **third** external caller the plan predates: `articles.ts:301` (A3.12). The JD.1 block was scoped to JD.3/JD.6 and JD.3 is done — its DB-fixture half is distilled inline because JD.4 and JD.8 both seed rows.*

The extension MUST NOT import `src/x/settings/registry.ts` (§5 build-isolation). **Binds UI.2/UI.11:** if the server `SettingDef` shape gains a field the editor needs, hand-sync it into `SettingEntry`.
