Where a foreign row could legitimately take a freed slot, the assertion filters to the seeds (`t.startsWith('JD1')`) instead of comparing whole arrays; where the seeds provably fill every slot, exact equality is safe. **Also:** `bun -e` out-of-band verification (the GT.1 recipe) caught nothing this time but took ten seconds and is what makes "byte-sync is untouched" a checked claim rather than an inference from a green suite — keep doing it on any task that touches a prompt module.

## Archived at JD.4 (2026-07-27) — two blocks whose standing halves are stated closer to their use

**Why these two stopped needing to be in STATE.md.** D160 is half of JD.8's closing-task spec and it still binds — but it is 3.4 KB read by exactly ONE future task, which is now told to grep for it by name (the SC.9 lesson: "it still binds an open task" is a reason to DISTIL, not to keep 19 KB). The GR.10 gotcha is the Wave-3 smoke template; its standing rule already lives in **D113(d)/(e)** in STATE.md, and the Wave-3 carry-forward item (4) already lists the five reads-that-write, so what moved here is the Guardrails-fixture detail underneath it. **JD.8 must read D160 in full before writing the closing docs-sync.**

- **D160** (GT.9, binds SC.9/JD.8 and every future closing docs-sync): **three things the plan's Task 9 asks for that the repo had already answered differently, and one structural debt a closing pass is the only one positioned to see.** **(a) The smoke's spec was written for a feature that was never built and for a `CLAUDE.md` that no longer takes phase entries.** Task 9's step (b) is the register/pillar cooldown — GT.5, `[s]` since the wave opened (superseded by SC.6, D142), so asserting it would test a module that does not exist; SC.9's smoke owns the format arithmetic, and what it should assert there is the **exempt invariant** and a seeded flip, not a raw count over a shared corpus (D156f). Its "CLAUDE.md: one phase-style entry" means `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md` since the 2026-07-23 refactor. Both are recorded rather than silently skipped. **(b) The docs half was a VERIFICATION pass and `git log --name-only -- docs/` is how you scope one in a single command** — five GT tasks had each paid their own tab doc, exactly as the Wave-3 carry-forward requires, so guessing would have meant re-reading five docs or trusting a plan written five days earlier. **What that command surfaced is the useful part: the two tasks with NO tab doc to update were the two prompt tasks, and they had therefore shipped entirely undocumented.** GT.1's echo anchor and GT.2's four skeletons change what every draft says and appear nowhere a user reads. A prompt task has no natural docs home, so it silently exits the per-task discipline — **a future prompt-only task should write its user-facing paragraph in its own commit**; `docs/replies-tab.md` §"Generating and the three variants" and `docs/composer-tab.md` §"Generating and picking a draft" are now the homes. The same pass caught a stale "two variants in one call" in the cost paragraph, three waves after RU.1 made it three. **(c) `evals/i_mika_el_analysis.md` got more than the plan's one-line pointer, on purpose.** The plan asks for "shipped as GT.1–GT.8". But **three of the study's own recommendations did not survive our corpus** (the `noticed` membership floor, the quest's plain membership test, x-builder's 0.45 Jaccard bar — D157a/D158a/D156a), and an eval doc whose recommendations were silently overruled is worse than no eval doc: the next person to mine it re-proposes the same numbers. The pointer section records what was replaced and by which measurement. **The generalizable rule: when a plan's borrowed number loses to a measurement, the correction belongs in the SOURCE the number came from, not only in the deviation register.** **(d) A closing pass archives the lane in front of it and misses the weight behind it.** UI.17 archived the polish lane and left **Wave 5's own 13 ledger rows** in STATE.md; every session since Step-0'd past them. GT.9's pass therefore moved four things, not one: the GT lane, those rows, the discharged narration in the header, and the verification-debt matrix — which is *unpaid* debt and so could not be archived, but had become the single largest block in the file and binds no open task, so it moved to `VERIFY-DEBT.md` with a pointer. 156 KB → ~76 KB. **So the closing question is not "what did my lane leave" but "what stopped binding anything, ever".**

- **GR.10: the smoke that closes Wave 3 is the template for smoking a surface whose READS write.** `scripts/smoke-guardrails.ts` ($0, no `--live`, rerunnable, verified to leave 0 rows) mounts following + monitor + goals + me + calendar + brief + digest over the real `./stratus.db`. **Copy its safety shape, not `smoke-passive-harvest.ts`'s, whenever a route mutates rows it did not create:** namespaced ids cover only what you wrote, so it *additionally* snapshots every foreign `following.status` before the first write and restores it from the success path **and** from `fail()` (a complete-run reconcile marks every unseen live handle `gone`; a queue read releases rows), snapshot-restores `commitments` and the seeded `streaks`/`digests` keys rather than deleting them, and **closes with one more `GET /x/brief`** so today's C9 streak row is recomputed from clean data instead of describing the burst fixtures it just deleted. **Three things it discovered that no plan text says:** (a) reconcile "unseen" is per **run** (`last_run_id`), not per batch — proving `gone`/`confirmed` needs TWO runs; (b) the queue needs ≥3 slots of budget for its assertions, so the script fails loudly naming `windowUsed/windowCap` + `dailyUsed/dailyCeiling` rather than through a confusing count mismatch; (c) `?factsOnly=true` returns **before** the digest cache write, so a scorecard read caches nothing — but it still settles goals. Assertions over shared/real data are **baseline-relative** where they must be (`nearDuplicate.pairCount`, `scheduleCluster.clusterCount`) and exact where the fixture owns the whole population (`questAdherence`, the queue batch, the ledger statuses).

**Added by the same JD.4 pass — the rest of JD.8's closing-task spec.** D160 (above) was only half of it; D162 and the GT.9/SC.9 gotchas are the other half, and all four together were ~11 KB of STATE.md read by every session and needed by exactly ONE task. They are consolidated here rather than distilled because a closing docs-sync is the one task that genuinely wants the full text. **JD.8: read this whole section before writing anything.**

- **D162** (SC.9, binds JD.8 and every future closing task): **three things a closing pass discovers only by doing it, and one plan done-when that was wrong about its own engine.** **(a) The plan's done-when did not survive contact with the code it specified.** SC's own bar says the em-dash + "thoughts?" + 15-line draft shows "exactly three **Fix** rows"; the engine ships `em_dash` and `weak_closer` as **nudges** (severity is per-rule — D145), so the draft scores **80 / ship it** with exactly ONE fix. The smoke asserts the contract that is actually true — all three rules FIRE, `show_more` is the only fix — with a comment saying why it is not the plan's sentence. A closing task is the last chance to notice that a plan's acceptance criterion describes a different implementation than the one that shipped; **assert the code, record the divergence, never quietly restate the plan.** **(b) The assertion-shape ranking** (invariant > delta > the plan's bar > an exact number, and the last one only in a *pure* step on a synthetic corpus) is stated in full in the **SC.9 gotcha** below — read it there, it is the same paragraph and it belongs with the fixture facts. **(c) Both new files answered a question no per-task doc could.** `git log --name-only -- docs/` showed SC.3–SC.8 each paid their tab doc (D160b), so the owed piece was the one **no tab owns**: the cross-cutting story, including the table of what was deliberately NOT adopted. **A lane whose whole thesis is "a number is a measurement or it is absent" needs a doc that says what it refused to guess** — otherwise the refusals read as missing features. Same reasoning drove the `evals/x-builder-static-engine-analysis.md` postscript (D160c): four of that study's numbers and two of stratus's own house readers lost to the corpus, and an eval doc whose recommendations were silently overruled invites the next reader to re-propose them. **(d) One stale pointer fixed:** `smoke-growth-tactics.ts` named a `smoke-static-coach.ts` that never existed (the plan and STATE both say `smoke-coach.ts`). A forward reference written by the *previous* task is unverifiable when it is written and invisible afterwards — **when you name a file a future task will create, the task that creates it owns the check.**

- **GT.9 — facts for SC.9/JD.8 (the next two closing smokes) and anyone writing a smoke that must not spend:** **the strongest $0 assertion for a gate is how FAR a request gets, not what comes back.** `smoke-growth-tactics.ts` force-unsets both LLM keys in a `finally` and asserts **503 `llm_not_configured`** — reaching the LLM layer IS the proof the gate opened, and it is unspendable by construction rather than by remembering not to pass `--live`. It is why the script has no `--live` flag at all: the only paid thing in reach is the call it exists to prove was not made. Copy that shape for any surface whose success path costs money. **Second: assert the QUALIFIER, not the count.** The reciprocity step's load-bearing assertion is not `n === 1` — it is that re-stamping the same person's `stageUpdatedAt` to *now* makes the same posted reply stop counting. A count over a shared real DB is baseline-relative noise; the qualifier is the feature, and it is the one thing a fixture written by someone who already believes the rule would never have checked. **Fixture facts:** handles are `gt9*` (≤15 chars or `normalizePersonHandle` drops them silently and every assertion goes vacuous), tweet ids 887-prefixed; a `people` row needs only `handle`+`stage`; the milestone step is PURE and deliberately seeds no `account_snapshots` (that table is contested by four suites and `smoke-pinned-watch.ts` refuses the real DB over it); and `/x/brief` WRITES — it settles goals and rewrites today's C9 diary — so the run closes on a clean brief read asserting the baseline came back (the GR.10 rule). **Local `./stratus.db` is nearly empty** (prod lives on Hetzner), so the reciprocity set reads as just the script's own seeds; that is the fresh-install path and worth seeing, but it means the smoke proves wiring, never calibration — calibration is the prod `POST /x/data/query` recipe in the archived GT.6/GT.7 gotchas. **Docs discipline this pass established:** a prompt-only task has no tab doc, so it silently exits the per-task docs rule and ships invisible — GT.1 and GT.2 both did. Write the user-facing paragraph in the prompt task's own commit; `replies-tab.md` §"Generating and the three variants" and `composer-tab.md` §"Generating and picking a draft" are where prompt behaviour is explained now.

- **SC.9 — facts for JD.8 (the next and last closing task) and anyone writing a smoke over a shared corpus:** **the assertion shapes are ranked, and the ranking is the whole craft of this file** — invariant > delta > the plan's bar > (in a *pure* step only) an exact number. Concretely: `exempt ⇒ clear` and "no insufficient cell carries a number" hold on any corpus including an empty one; cooldown counts and `measuredPosts` are deltas against baselines read **before the first seed**; the gate is proven at `minN=1000`, which no account can ever redden; and the exact `[40,120]` stall range lives in a synthetic-corpus step. **The padding trick that makes an exact quantile assertable:** pad so 15+ of every 20-row trailing window sit at one value — the base is then pinned by construction and a row's ratio is `views / base` — and make the padding an **exempt** format so it can never mint a competing fitted cell. **Fixture facts:** ids are 889-prefixed; every seeded `posts_published` row is `retired: true` (NT.7 — an un-retired row joins the *billed* daily pass, which is the one way this $0 script could cost money); the cooldown/Playbook fixtures deliberately carry snapshots with a **null** `ageAtSnapshotMin` so they stay out of the reach population and its delta stays exactly the two rows it is about; the lexicon step inserts a channel with `niche: null` so it is in scope under whichever niche is active. **Nothing mounted here is a read-that-writes** (unlike `/x/brief` in GT.9's), so the closing re-read is a *cleanup proof*, not a repair. **Docs facts:** `git log --name-only -- docs/ evals/` scopes a closing docs pass in one command (D160b); what it cannot tell you is the piece **no tab owns** — for a lane spanning four tabs that cross-cutting doc IS the owed work, and for a lane whose thesis is "a number is a measurement or it is absent" it has to state the refusals or they read as missing features. Its home is `docs/sc-static-coach.md`, linked from the README term list plus the Composer and Playbook sections.

## Archived at JD.5 (2026-07-27) — two JD-lane blocks whose readers have all shipped

Both were written for tasks that are now `[x]`. **D164** was JD.3's four plan-vs-repo
resolutions and named JD.4/JD.5/JD.6/JD.8 as readers: JD.4 and JD.5 have landed and consumed
(a)–(d), the two generalizable halves are codemap §7 rule **35** (what the schema cannot
express, the parser enforces, and the parser is what a test pins) and the standing
"a plan quoting a count is quoting the day it was written" lesson that JD.5's update-log entry
restates. The **JD.3 gotcha block** was facts for JD.4/JD.5/JD.8; JD.4 and JD.5 paid them, and
the two that outlive their readers are stated inline in STATE (assert a hard-wrapped template
through `normalizeJudgeText`; whitespace-only edits do NOT invalidate a verdict).

**Fetch them before touching the judge prompt, the schema, or `parseVerdict`** — D164(b)'s
structured-outputs keyword list and the `1e999` non-finite recipe are the kind of thing that is
cheap to read and expensive to rediscover.

- **D164** (JD.3, binds JD.4/JD.5/JD.6/JD.8): **four plan readings resolved, three of them because the plan predates thirteen tasks of its own repo.** **(a) The registry key is the 14th, not the plan's "11th", and `registry.test.ts` DID need changing.** The plan's Task 3 says "add the `judge` key (11th)" and "nothing in `registry.test.ts` needs changing (no key-count assertion exists)" — both were written before `dm` and `article` landed, and the suite has asserted the **exact key list** with `toEqual` since AI.3. So this was the full RL.4 4-file move: `registry.ts` + the pure module + `registry.test.ts` (list + `isPromptKey` + `defaultBody`) + `docs/settings-tab.md` 13→14. **A plan that quotes a count is quoting the day it was written; re-derive it.** The done-when's "`GET /x/prompts` lists 11 prompts" reads as 14. **(b) The schema's numeric bounds were dropped and that is a correctness decision, not a style one.** The plan asks for `scores` as "13 integer 0–100"; `minimum`/`maximum` are on the same strict-structured-outputs UNSUPPORTED list as the `minItems`/`maxItems` the threadPrompt/RU.3/AI.8 schemas already refuse, so shipping them risks a provider rejection on every judge call. The **parser** enforces 0–100 (and the 12/5 caps), which is where the plan already puts the caps anyway. `['integer','null']` on `audienceMatch` is the opposite case — a union with null is the *documented* way to make a required field nullable, so it is not the same risk and it stays. **The generalizable half is codemap §7 rule 35:** what the schema cannot express, the parser enforces, and the parser is what a test pins. **(c) `renderJudgeGrounding` takes STRINGS, not the plan's `(niche, pillarLabels)`.** The plan's Edit list and its How-section disagree ("the route loads the niche and passes strings, so the prompt module stays pure and testable"); the How won. `renderJudgeGrounding({persona?, beliefs?, pillarLabels?}) → string|null`, null when there is nothing real — mirroring `renderIcebreakerGrounding`, and JD.4 supplies the loaded values. It also means the grounding is testable with no DB, which is why 36 tests fit in a $0 pure suite. **(d) `judgeTextHash` uses `Bun.CryptoHasher`, not the plan's `node:crypto`** — same sha256, and it is the house idiom (`promptCacheKey`). It **normalizes internally**, so JD.4's insert and JD.7's Playbook join both hash raw text and cannot disagree about which side was normalized. **Deliberately NOT done, flagged rather than skipped:** the plan's `parseVerdict` line "annotations clamped to 12 **and individually dropped when the quote is empty or over-long**" is implemented, but nothing here validates a quote against the draft — that check is `locateAnnotations` at render time (JD.2), and duplicating it in the parser would make a hallucinated quote a 502 instead of a dropped row.

- **JD.3 — facts for JD.4/JD.5/JD.8 and anyone parsing a PAID response:** **(1) The route inherits a parser that is strict on 13 fields and lenient on six.** `parseVerdict` returns `null` only for an unparseable body or a bad `scores` object — so JD.4's 502 `judge_parse_error` fires on *those* cases and nothing else; a verdict with an empty `headline`, `confidence: null` and zero annotations is a SUCCESS, not a degraded one, and the route must not add a second refusal on top. **(2) The `1e999` recipe:** a non-finite guard is untestable through `JSON.stringify` (it emits `null` for `Infinity`) but perfectly testable through the raw text `JSON.parse` receives — build the fixture with a string `.replace`, which is also closer to what the route actually gets. **(3) Assert a hard-wrapped template through `normalizeJudgeText`,** never against the raw literal: my first contract assertion went red purely because "an exact substring of the draft" straddled a source line break. **(4) Two biome traps this file hit:** `delete obj.staticKey` is an error (`noDelete`) while `delete obj[computed]` is not — use rest-destructuring (`const {audienceMatch: _omitted, ...absent} = scores()`) when you need a key gone; and a bare `1e999` literal is `noPrecisionLoss`. **(5) `judgeTextHash('a  b') === judgeTextHash('a b')` by design** — whitespace-only edits do NOT invalidate a verdict, which is the intended reading of the reset-on-edit rule (the manualReconcile/harvest text-match convention) and is what makes the JD.7 join work against pasted-from-X text. **(6) The out-of-band check is still worth ten seconds** (the GT.1 recipe): `bun -e` printing `PROMPT_KEYS.length`, the rendered prompt length and a `JSON.stringify` round-trip of the schema confirmed 14 / 4347 chars / serializable before commit — the schema is built at module load from a `.map`, so "it compiles" is not the same claim as "it serializes".


## Also archived at JD.5 (2026-07-27) — D163 and the JD.4 gotcha block

**D163** (JD.2) named JD.3/JD.6/JD.7 as readers; JD.3 consumed (c), so what is left binds the
Composer panel — **JD.6 must fetch this before rendering a single score**, because (a) is the
three shared exports the panel would otherwise hand-type (`JUDGE_SUB_DIMENSIONS`,
`JUDGE_DIMENSION_LABEL`, and `JUDGE_HIGHER_IS_WORSE` — colouring `negativeRisk` 94 green says
the opposite of what the rubric means) and (b) is the pinned, deliberate limitation of
`locateAnnotations`.

The **JD.4 gotcha block** was facts for JD.5/JD.7/JD.8. JD.5 paid its share; the two that still
bind JD.7 are distilled inline in STATE, and the rest — the migration-applies-via-the-suite
observation, the `bun -e` out-of-band recipe, and the biome-reformats-what-it-does-not-reject
note — is here.

- **D163** (JD.2, binds JD.3/JD.6/JD.7): **three exports beyond the plan's list, one algorithm deliberately NOT improved, two types JD.3 inherits.** **(a) All three additions are the same move — a fact the panel would otherwise invent.** `JUDGE_SUB_DIMENSIONS` exists because the plan's own done-when has the panel rendering "twelve collapsed dimensions" while `JUDGE_DIMENSIONS` is thirteen; without it JD.6 hand-types a 12-element twin list (§7 rule 4c). `JUDGE_DIMENSION_LABEL` follows three precedents (`FORMAT_LABELS`, `COACH_BAND_LABEL`, `BAND_LABEL` all live in the shared module). **`JUDGE_HIGHER_IS_WORSE` is the load-bearing one:** `negativeRisk` 94 and `statusDependency` 94 are *bad*, and a panel colouring scores green-high says the opposite — polarity is a rubric fact and belongs beside the rubric, while thresholds/tones stay presentation (the `CoachChip.tsx` split). **Its omissions ARE the decision:** `answerEffort`/`strangerAnswerability` are absent because read as targets they are the reply-farm gradient (JD decision 4) — neutral is the honest polarity, and the test pins the set at exactly two with that reason inline. **(b) `locateAnnotations` ships the plan's consumed-offset walk unimproved: annotations arriving out of DOCUMENT order lose the earlier one.** A claimed-range matcher fixes it in ~15 lines and passes every test the plan asks for — but it buys overlap semantics against a failure mode nobody has observed, and the SC-lane doctrine is that a behaviour change is a measurement or it is absent. **The limitation is pinned by a test**, which is what makes changing it deliberate rather than a silent regression. Revisit against real judgments. **(c)** `JudgeVerdict` is the MODEL-derived half only — **no `overall` sibling** (it is `scores.overall`; two copies is how a label and a number start disagreeing) and none of the transport fields (`id`/`textHash`/`model`/`provider`/`costUsd`/`requestId` are the route's). JD.3 kept the `JudgeConfidence` enum as-is, so that half is settled.

- **JD.4 — facts for JD.5/JD.7/JD.8 and anyone adding a table to this repo:** **(1) The `bun run test` suite proves a migration applies, and nothing else does.** `draft_judgments` rows inserted fine in `:memory:` on the first run, which IS the migration check — there is no separate step, and if the table were missing the suite would red on the insert, not on a boot error. **(2) A new table with no other reader needs no baseline discipline, and saying so is worth a line** — `judge.test.ts` cleans up by collected id in `afterAll` and can assert exact counts, unlike every suite that touches `posts_published`. **The moment JD.7 joins this table from the Playbook that stops being true**, and its route tests inherit the SC.5 lock row's rule (partition invariants or before/after deltas, never absolutes). **(3) Two `text_hash` spellings must agree or JD.7 silently measures nothing**, so the test pins all three: `judgeTextHash(text)` == `judgeTextHash(normalizeJudgeText(text))` == the hash of the same text with a double space collapsed. `judgeTextHash` normalizing internally (D164d) is what makes that free — but a future caller reaching for `normalizeJudgeText` first is not a bug, and the test is what keeps it not-a-bug. **(4) The out-of-band check earned its ten seconds again** (the GT.1/JD.3 recipe, now three tasks running): a `bun -e` mounting the router on a bare Hono printed the rendered prompt length (4365), that the grounding block is present and the draft is LAST, that `JUDGE_SCHEMA` serializes (2713 bytes), and that a bad provider really 400s through `app.request` — "typecheck passes" claims none of those, and the schema is built at module load from a `.map`. **(5) biome reformatted two lines it did not reject** (a long `expect(...)` and a chained `db.select()`), so `bunx biome check --write` on the changed files before `bun run lint` saves a round-trip; the lint failure text is a diff, not an error list.



## Archived at JD.6 (2026-07-27) — D165, D166, the JD.5 gotchas, the JD.2 gotchas, and the Composer.tsx lock row

**Why these stopped binding.** JD.6 closed the **D141 Composer lane** (SC.3 → SC.6 → SC.8 → JD.6):
`extension/src/sidepanel/Composer.tsx` has no remaining claimant in any open task, so its
lock row — 1.2 KB of notes for a lane that is finished — moved here whole. **D165** and
**D166** were the JD.4/JD.5 resolution registers; four of D165's five readings and four of
D166's five were consumed by JD.5 and JD.6, both `[x]`, and what still binds JD.7/JD.8
survives as the two pointer lines left in STATE. Same for the **JD.5** and **JD.2** gotcha
blocks. Grep this section by `D165` / `D166` / `JD.5 —` / `JD.2 —` / `Composer.tsx`.

**The one clause worth reading before ANY judge work, restated so a grep for it lands here:**
the route derives nothing (`parseVerdict` already banded the score), only a WINNING rewrite is
persisted, and a re-judge is pinned to the stored judgment's model+provider. All three are
codemap §7 (rules 4c and 36) as well.

### The `Composer.tsx` hot-file lock row (retired at JD.6 — the D141 lane is closed)

| `extension/src/sidepanel/Composer.tsx` | — (**released by SC.8 — the D141 lane's third link is done; `JD.6` is the LAST claimant and reads the file as SC.8 left it.** **SC.8 left the `.coach` box reading head -> cooldown line -> reach line -> fix rows -> nudge rows -> `<details>` passing -> disclaimer, and added a second per-mount fetch (`api.coach.reach`) beside the cooldown one plus two module-level pure helpers `reachNote`/`reachTitle`. `draftFormat` is now read by BOTH corpus lines — reuse it, don't re-classify. JD.6's judge panel goes BELOW the AI.8 rewrite block per D141, i.e. NOT inside `.coach`: everything in that box is free and instant, and a panel that costs an LLM call does not belong in a container the user reads as live.** Standing facts JD.6 inherits: the box sits between the URL/over-limit warns and the Schedule `Section`; `coachDraft`/`coachInput`/`coach` are computed right after `threadSegments`; the tones live in `CoachChip.tsx` (import, never re-declare); and **`canSubmit` must stay ignorant of every score this lane adds** — four tasks have now honoured that, which is how SC decision 4 is enforced rather than merely intended)

### D165 and D166 (verbatim)

- **D165** (JD.4, binds JD.5/JD.6/JD.7/JD.8): **five plan readings resolved, and the through-line is that the plan's Task 4 was written before its own Task 3 existed.** **(a) The route derives NOTHING, and that is the opposite of what the plan's ladder says.** Task 4's How reads "`parseVerdict` null → 502 → **derive band** → best-effort insert", and decision 3 says the route "computes it from `scores.overall` and spreads it last". But JD.3 put `deriveVerdictBand`/`deriveApproved` INSIDE `parseVerdict`, so the band arrives already attached: the route spreads `...verdict` whole. Re-deriving here would satisfy the plan's sentence while creating exactly the twin decision 3 exists to prevent (§7 rule 4c). **When a plan says "derive it at layer N" and layer N-1 already did, the invariant is *one derivation*, not *derivation at N*.** **(b) `judge_invalid` was NOT shipped, and the omission is the correctness call.** The plan's endpoint table lists 502 `judge_parse_error`/`judge_invalid`/`judge_failed`, modelled on `/posts/rewrite` where `rewrite_invalid` fires when zero variants survive the parser's drops. The judge has no analogue: `parseVerdict` is all-or-nothing on the 13 scores, so anything it returns is complete, and JD.3's own contract (D164, gotcha 1) says a verdict with an empty headline, null confidence and zero annotations is a SUCCESS. A second refusal would 502 a call that was already billed, for a model that simply had nothing to flag. **Shipping a dead error code is worse than dropping it: the next reader assumes there is a case that reaches it.** **(c) `headline` was ADDED to the Design DDL.** The sketch has `confidence`, `scores`, `annotations`, `strengths`, `improvements` — but not `headline`, and JD.5's never-worse path returns the ORIGINAL verdict on a losing rewrite. Without the column that path hands back a verdict with a blank headline, which breaks decision 7's one invariant ("the returned verdict always describes the returned text") in the most confusing possible way: a verdict that looks partially loaded rather than kept. **(d) The migration is `0024_soft_leopardon`, not the plan's `0018`** — `0018` is RL.2's `reply_lists`. Already flagged in STATE, restated because it is the fourth plan in a row to quote a count or a number from its writing day (D164a's "11th registry key", GT.9's tab-doc guesses). **(e) `approved` is deliberately not a column** — it is `deriveApproved(verdict)`, and `verdict`+`overall` are already the same fact twice (justified: `overall` is what JD.7 aggregates, and they cannot disagree because `insertJudgment` takes the parsed verdict whole and nothing writes them separately). A third copy is where a label starts lying. A test pins the absence. **Two sizing decisions worth not re-litigating:** `maxOutputTokens` is **1600** against a worst case computed at ≈1150 (13 scores ~150 + headline ~50 + 5+5 one-liners ~250 + 12 annotations ~660), because the failure is asymmetric — a truncated body makes `parseVerdict` return null and the call is billed for a 502, while unused headroom bills nothing; and `temperature` is **0.2** because JD.5's `<=` compare between two judgments is only meaningful if the grader is near-deterministic.
- **D166** (JD.5, binds JD.6/JD.7/JD.8): **five resolutions, and the through-line is that a guard is only as good as what it holds constant.** **(a) The shared helper is call → parse, NOT the plan's "call → parse → derive → best-effort insert".** Two independent reasons, and they point the same way: D165a already settled that the route derives nothing (`parseVerdict` banded the score), and keeping the INSERT out is what makes the plan's own *conditional* persist ("else persist the new judgment with `parent_judgment_id` set") expressible at all — a helper that always writes cannot express "only if it won". **What must be identical between a first judge and a re-judge is the prompt body, the server-stamped grounding, the schema and the sampling. The side write is not part of that claim**, and folding it in would have forced a flag argument, which is the thing that makes two call sites stop being the same call site. `judgeOnce` returns a discriminated union already shaped as the HTTP response, so both routes' error handling is one `if (!ok) return c.json(body, status)`. **(b) The re-judge is pinned to the STORED judgment's `model`+`provider`; the caller's override steers the WRITER only.** This is the load-bearing one and the plan does not mention it: `apply`'s body carries `model?`/`provider?`, `askLLM` puts a per-call provider at the top of the precedence chain, and `rewriteOverall <= originalOverall` across a grok verdict and an openrouter one compares two graders rather than two drafts. Decision 11 is the argument *for* pinning, not against: a judge on the drafting model likes its own prose, and that bias is only tolerable when it is the SAME bias on both sides. Generalized as codemap §7 rule **36**. **(c) 409 `nothing_to_apply` was ADDED, which is the opposite call from D165b and consistent with it.** `judge_invalid` was dropped because `parseVerdict` makes it unreachable; this one ships because JD.3's contract makes it *reachable* — a verdict with an empty headline, null confidence and zero annotations is a documented SUCCESS, and paying a rewrite call to apply zero fixes is exactly the spend §7.4 refuses. **The test to write for a guard like this is not "does the error fire" but "does the NEAR case get through"**: improvements-with-no-annotations reaches 503, which is what proves the gate is `annotations.length === 0 && improvements.length === 0` and not `annotations.length === 0`. **(d) Only a winning rewrite is persisted.** A discarded rewrite is text the user never saw; JD.7 buckets *published* posts by the band their text was judged at, so an unjoinable row is noise in the one table built to be measured. The money is not lost either way — `cost_events` bills both calls through the middleware, and `draft_judgments.cost_usd` was never the cost ledger. **(e) Three text-identity calls, all of them consequences of one rule (the returned verdict describes the returned text):** the rewriter gets **`row.text`** as `{{DRAFT}}` because the annotation quotes were copied out of *it* character for character; a kept draft comes back as the **CALLER** spelled it, so the composer is left untouched (the two are hash-equal, they can differ only in whitespace runs); and a rewrite that hashes back to the original **short-circuits before the re-judge** — the stored verdict IS its verdict by the reset-on-edit reading, so re-judging would pay ~$0.003 to reproduce a number we hold, and at temperature 0.2 would usually reproduce it as a tie and discard it anyway. All three exits go through `applyPayload`, which stamps `textHash` from the text it is returning, so the invariant is structural rather than three careful hands. **Two smaller ones:** `MAX_JUDGE_TEXT` moved from the route into `judge/prompt.ts` (the rewrite parser caps its OUTPUT at the same ceiling — a rewrite too long to judge is not one this route can compare, and two spellings would be the §7-rule-4c twin); and the plan's "`judge-rewrite` (12th)" is the **15th** — the fifth plan in a row to quote a count from its writing day (D164a).

### The JD.5 and JD.2 gotcha blocks (verbatim)

- **JD.5 — facts for JD.6/JD.7/JD.8, and for anyone testing a route whose success path costs $0.007:** **(1) Both live paths of a guard can be unreachable at $0, and the answer is to export the RULE, not to mock the call.** `rewriteWins(a,b) = a > b` and `applyPayload(...)` are three-line exports whose tests pin the two decisions a later reader would otherwise "fix" silently — the tie direction, and that `textHash` always describes the RETURNED text. Neither test needs a provider key, a fixture, or a mock. **Reach for this whenever the interesting behaviour sits after two paid calls**: the alternative is a route-level mock that proves the mock works. **(2) The GT.9 how-far-does-it-get recipe extends to proving a NEGATIVE.** `judge.test.ts` force-unsets both keys three times, not once: a whitespace-only edit reaching 503 proves the 409 hash check is normalization-aware, improvements-with-no-annotations reaching 503 proves `nothing_to_apply` is an AND and not an OR, and the full ladder reaching 503 **with a `parentJudgmentId` probe returning zero rows** proves a refused apply writes nothing. A 503 is a much better assertion than it looks: it is the only status that says *everything before the money passed*. **(3) Sequential `{{TOKEN}}` substitution is unsafe the moment one substituted value is copied out of another.** The house split/join idiom exists for `$&`-safety and chains fine when the values are independent — but a judge annotation's `quote` is copied verbatim out of the draft, so a draft containing the literal `{{IMPROVEMENTS}}` reaches the template through `{{FIXES}}` and the next step expands it. One `.replace(/\{\{(?:A|B|C)\}\}/g, t => values[t] ?? t)` is single-pass AND `$`-safe (a FUNCTION replacer never interprets `$&`), and the test that proves it is a token count, not a snapshot: exactly two survivors, `split(token).length === 3`. **(4) The rewrite parser's shapes came from the study, and the depth guard's test asserts a PROPERTY, not a value** — a 6-deep nest must terminate and must never hand back a JSON blob as a post, which is `out === null || out === 'too deep'`. Pinning the exact answer there would pin an implementation detail nobody has an opinion about. **(5) `bun run lint` was clean but `bunx biome check --write` still reformatted one file** — the JD.4 gotcha, now four tasks running: run the autofix on the changed files first, the failure text is a diff rather than an error list. **(6) The out-of-band `bun -e` check earned its ten seconds a fourth time**: mounting `judgeRouter` on a bare Hono printed `PROMPT_KEYS.length` 15, `validatePromptBody('judge-rewrite')` clean, the rendered prompt at 1719 chars with `{{DRAFT}}` genuinely last, the schema serializing at 183 bytes, the double-encoded unwrap returning the inner text, and a real 404/409/503 through `app.request`. Typecheck claims none of those.
- **JD.2 — the facts JD.3 did not consume** (full block in `STATE-ARCHIVE.md`, grep JD.2): **a shim with no importer is a real deliverable.** `extension/src/judge.ts` + its `tsconfig.app.json` `include` entry (now **six**) are typechecked from JD.2 but bundled by nothing until JD.6 — zero bytes, and omitting the `include` entry fails `cd extension && bun run typecheck` with a confusing rootDir error. **The list-plus-interface drift check** (`JUDGE_DIMENSIONS` vs the `JudgeScores` interface: sorted-`Object.keys` equality over a populated fixture, because TS checks one direction only) is now asserted in BOTH judge suites — reuse the shape for any such pair. The two red-gate traps it cost are restated in the JD.3 block below.

### The JD lane's deviations register (D167–D170) and its pointer lines

- **D149** (JD.1) — full text elsewhere in this file (grep `D149` or `drafter.ts`). **Read it before any few-shot / winners / trust-marker work.** The clause that outlives it: **`posts_published.source` is `'scheduled'|'manual'` — the PUBLISH path, not authorship.** The only column that answers "who wrote this" is `scheduled_posts.source` (`'api'` vs `'drafter'`), reached through `posted_tweet_id`, which the publisher and the manual reconcile both stamp. JD.8 wrote this into `docs/composer-tab.md` in user-facing words.
- **D160** (GT.9) and **D162** (SC.9) — the closing-docs-sync doctrine, in §"Archived at JD.4" above. JD.8 was their last reader and both held: (b)'s `git log --name-only -- docs/` scoping found the one owed piece in a single command, and its warning that **a prompt-only task ships invisible** is exactly what JD.1 turned out to have done. (d)'s "ask what stopped binding *ever*" is what produced this section.
- **D165** (JD.4) / **D166** (JD.5) — full text in §"Archived at JD.6". What JD.8 consumed: `judge_invalid` was never shipped while `stale_verdict` / `nothing_to_apply` were (document the codes that exist); the re-judge is pinned to the stored model+provider (codemap §7 rule **36**); `MAX_JUDGE_TEXT` lives in `judge/prompt.ts`, not the route.
- **D167** (JD.6, UI doctrine): **the reset-on-edit rule is DERIVED, not an effect.** Task 6's How says "a `useEffect` on `text` resets to `idle` whenever `text !== judge.judgedText`". Shipped instead as one line of derived render state (`view = judged && judgedText !== text ? idle : judge`). Two reasons, the second being the real one: an effect runs AFTER paint, so the stale verdict paints for a frame every time you type — a number decorating text it never read, the exact thing §J4 exists to prevent. And derivation makes **undo restore the verdict**, because it genuinely describes that text again; the effect version would charge $0.003 to reproduce a number already in memory. **The general rule: when a plan says "clear X when Y changes", ask whether X is state at all** — if it is a pure function of Y, storing it buys a stale frame and a lost fact.
- **D168** (JD.6): **the panel shipped as a NEW file, against a task block that named `Composer.tsx`.** That file was 1406 lines and Wave 6's choke point (D141); the judge needs exactly three things from it — `text`, `setText`, a textarea ref — so `JudgePanel.tsx` takes them as props and Composer grew **13 lines** instead of ~300. The precedent is SC.4, which lifted `CoachChip` out of the same file for the same reason. **A plan's Edit list is a budget, not a file lock:** the constraint that mattered was *don't inflate the choke-point file*, and honouring the letter would have violated it. Also 6 files, not the block's 5 — `docs/composer-tab.md` was owed in the task's own commit, not by JD.8.
- **D169** (JD.6): **no provider picker shipped, despite JD decision 11's "so the panel exposes it".** (a) The house precedent is AI.8: `RewriteBody` carries `model?`/`provider?` and the Composer sends neither — Settings → AI owns the provider, and a second per-call control is a twin knob. (b) The task block's own Edit list and done-when never mention a picker; decision 11's real content is *"the route stamps `model`+`provider` on every row — the risk is documented and measurable rather than silently baked in"*, which the panel satisfies by RENDERING the grader. (c) **D166(b) makes a picker actively misleading on the apply path**: the override steers the WRITER only, because the re-judge is pinned to the stored grader — a control labelled "provider" that changes half of what the user thinks it changes is worse than no control. The typed API params exist, so adding one later is a UI change alone.
- **D170** (JD.7): **four divergences from Task 7's block, three of which are the same lesson — a measurement cell inherits its NEIGHBOURS' shape, not its plan's.** (a) **The loader takes the rows it is given.** The block says `loadJudgeRows()` selects own non-reply `posts_published` itself; SC.5's rule for that table is one load feeding every own-originals cell, so `loadJudgeRows(originals)` adds only the `draft_judgments` select and the hashing — "never a second query" now holds structurally, and `routes/digest.ts` pays nothing for a cell it does not render. (b) **The payload is named for its neighbours** — `cells`/`unjudged`/`totalPosted`/`totalMeasured`/`spread`/`profileVisitsSpread`/`spreadBands`, not the block's `bands`/`spread:{views,profileVisits}` — and the spread is SC.5's **highest ÷ lowest GATED band, naming the pair**, not top-÷-bottom. The two sections render side by side; a reader comparing them should not have to learn two vocabularies. (c) **An `approved`/`rejected` split the block never listed.** `deriveApproved` is the only other reading the verdict supports, and a 2-way split clears the gate at half the sample a 4-way one needs — on a cell fed by an on-demand button, the difference between an answer and a decade. Exactly the D152b clean/flagged move, one layer up. (d) **`JUDGE_VERDICT_ORDER` + `isJudgeVerdictLabel` went into `src/shared/judge.ts`**, which the Edit list did not name (D168 again): the ordered list is the same anti-twin export `JUDGE_SUB_DIMENSIONS` was, and the guard is what lets a plain TEXT column become a band honestly. **Two smaller calls:** the join filters `surface='post'` (nothing writes `'reply'` in v1 per decision 2, and a test pins that such a row EXISTS and is still excluded), and the hash is spelled `judgeTextHash(p.text)` rather than the block's `judgeTextHash(normalizeJudgeText(p.text))`, which D164(d) makes identical and a fixture proves.

### The JD lane's gotchas

- **JD.1 — the DB-fixture shape a shared in-memory suite needs:** `drafter.test.ts` is the exemplar — seed **400 days back** (clear of the brief/digest/monitor windows and of the spoken-for calendar bands), view counts ~9M so the rows outrank every other suite's deterministically, **`retired: true` on every `posts_published` row** (NT.7 — an un-retired row joins the *billed* daily pass), and teardown in a **`try/finally` inside the test** as well as `afterAll`, because files share one in-memory DB and `afterAll` alone leaves your rows visible to every later test in the same file. Where a foreign row could take a freed slot, filter the assertion to your prefix instead of comparing whole arrays. (`smoke-judge.ts` reuses the 400-day rule.)
- **JD.2 / JD.3 / JD.5 gotchas** — full text in §"Archived at JD.6" and §"Archived at JD.5". The three facts that outlived their readers: the **list-plus-interface drift check** (sorted-`Object.keys` equality over a populated fixture, because TS checks one direction only); **assert a hard-wrapped template through `normalizeJudgeText`**, never against the raw literal; and **`judgeTextHash('a  b') === judgeTextHash('a b')` by design** — whitespace-only edits are NOT edits, which is what makes JD.7's join work against pasted-from-X text, what JD.5's 409 `stale_verdict` deliberately lets through, and what `smoke-judge.ts` asserts as a *qualifier* (a double space must land on `nothing_to_apply`, never `stale_verdict`).
- **JD.6 — what a panel task actually gets to verify:** **(1) The $0 coverage for a panel is real coverage, and the bundle grep is the load-bearing half.** Repo convention gives panel surfaces no test file, so the gates are root+extension typecheck, lint, `cd extension && bun run build`, and then **`grep -F` on the built artefacts** — a shim string in `dist/sidepanel.js` proves Vite inlined the out-of-tree shim, a copy string proves the component is reachable, and a class name in `dist/assets/sidepanel-*.css` proves the block shipped. **Use `grep -cF 'literal'` with single quotes** — a `$` inside a double-quoted pattern reads as a false 0. Plus `grep -cE '^\s*import[ {]' dist/content.js` == 0. **(2) A `<textarea>` swap has no undo, so any async handler that calls `setText` needs the live value.** The apply call takes ~7 s and the box stays editable; `text` inside the handler's closure is the render-time value, so the swap must read a ref and refuse when the draft moved on. **This applies to every paid panel action that writes back into the editor.** **(3) An LLM-gated mount 404s, it does not 503** — `judgeRouter` is under `llmConfigured()` (§7.22), so an unconfigured install reaches the panel as `http_404`, not `llm_not_configured`; both map to the same sentence in `errorCopy`. (Inside a smoke that mounts the router directly on a bare Hono, the gate is absent and a keyless request 503s — which is why `smoke-judge.ts` can make that assertion at all.) **(4) The out-of-band `bun -e` check earned its ten seconds a fifth time.**
- **JD.7 — what a cell over a table two other suites touch has to prove:** **(1) Baseline EVERY count you assert.** `draft_judgments` lost its exact-count freedom the moment the Playbook joined it: the route describe snapshots five numbers in `beforeAll` through the loader itself and every assertion is a delta. That also survives `judge.test.ts` running first in the same in-memory DB. **(2) `judged_at`'s column default resolves to the same millisecond for a batch**, so a "newest per hash wins" assertion seeded without an explicit stamp is a coin flip that passes locally. Any "latest per key" loader has this shape. **(3) The whitespace-insensitivity of the join is only proven by a fixture where the two texts genuinely differ** — and for the surface filter, assert the reply-surface row EXISTS and hashes to a published original *and then* that it was still not counted: "it did not match" and "it was filtered out" are different claims and only one of them is the code. **(4) A local run proves wiring and NOTHING else** — `./stratus.db` has zero published originals, and prod has zero `draft_judgments` rows, so the honest first real read is `unjudged.posted === totalPosted` with every band at zero.
- **JD.8 — the last three facts, and the first one is the only surprise:** **(1) The `--live` decision is not a style choice, it is "what can a keyless run structurally not claim".** GT.9 and SC.9 shipped no flag because the only paid thing in reach was the call they existed to prove was not made. `smoke-judge.ts` ships one, because a keyless run can never show that the provider closed the JSON inside `JUDGE_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, that thirteen dimensions survive `parseVerdict`, or that the persisted `text_hash` is the one the Playbook probes — those are the three things `--live` buys and nothing else. **(2) Deleting fixture judgments by the HASH OF THEIR OWN TEXT is better than collecting ids.** `draft_judgments.id` is a random uuid, so an id list dies with the process and a run killed mid-way leaves rows forever; the hash of a fixture sentence is deterministic across runs, can never collide with a real draft, and covers the `--live` row for free without any id bookkeeping. Reuse this shape for any table whose PK is generated and whose natural key is content. **(3) `bun run typecheck` covers `scripts/**`, so NT.7's `app.request` trap bites here** and biome's `noUnusedTemplateLiteral` fires on a backtick string with no interpolation — `bunx biome check --write` on the changed file first, six tasks running now.

### Priors and narration that stopped binding

- **prior (JD.7 — the `judgeEffectiveness` Playbook cell; high, dep JD.4✓, $0, 10 files; suite 1842→1855 +13):** the phase's own falsification test, shipped in the same phase as the tool it grades. `buildJudgeEffectiveness` (§15 in `playbook.ts`) + `loadJudgeRows(originals)` + a `JudgeEffectivenessSection`, plus `JUDGE_VERDICT_ORDER`/`isJudgeVerdictLabel` in `src/shared/judge.ts`. Four deviations, all D170.
- **SC carry-forward (the static-coach facts, kept live from SC.9 to JD.8):** **(1) The coach band is a CONSTANT, not a weak feature** — 97% `top` over the prod corpus, `rework`/`almost` at zero, and the only `fix` rules that fire are `url_cost` and `answerable_question` (D154a); a consumer must split on a fix/flag count or report both sides. **(2) Cut points live in ONE place** — `scoreDraft`'s `toBand` owns `<45/45–59/60–84/85+`, and the tone maps live in `sidepanel/CoachChip.tsx`; re-deriving either grades a different number than the Composer showed. **(3) The exempt three (`substance`/`one_liner`/`other`) are ONE set with one meaning** — *no format detected* — shared by the cooldown and the reach band; consumers ship `exempt: true` with a pinned safe status rather than keeping a copy (§7.11). **(4) `latestOutcomes` is the house pattern and it is a SELECTED sample** wherever the re-read is selective (25 of 135 measured originals carry a second snapshot, and those are the winner re-reads at 10× the views); `routes/coach.ts::loadReachRows` is the one loader that inverts it, with a comment — codemap §7 rule **34**. **(5) Panel surfaces have no test file** (repo convention); `CoachChip` is a **hook-using** component and can never be called as a plain function. **(6) The four $0 verification recipes:** push a fixture through the SHIM rather than the canonical module; pull the prod corpus with `GET /x/data/posts_published?limit=200&offset=N` and run a new `build*` locally at `minN=1`; **replay a rolling window** over that corpus to tell a useful counter from a permanently-lit one; and one `POST /x/data/query` selecting each row's FIRST and LATEST snapshot before trusting any "latest snapshot" aggregate. **(7) Local `./stratus.db` has ZERO published originals** (prod lives on Hetzner) — every coach/judge route returns the empty-corpus path locally, which is what a fresh install shows, but it means a local run proves wiring, never calibration.
- **Wave-4 carry-forward, the Wave-5 registry carry-forward, the GR.10 smoke template and the UI.17 smoke-writing block** — all four were already pointer lines in STATE and their full text is in §"Archived at JD.3" / §"Archived at JD.2" / §"Archived at JD.4" / §"Archived at UI.17". They bound nothing by JD.8. The three residues worth one line each: a route whose success path SPENDS is tested from the refusal side (assert how far the request gets); **Bun auto-loads `.env`, so `env -u API_TOKEN` does NOT unset it** — assign `''`; and `bun run <script>` buffers stdout when it is not a TTY, so a hung script writes an EMPTY output file and `console.error` is how you bisect it.

## Archived at HM.5 (2026-07-28) — the Wave-7 HM lane (project humanizer, HM.1–HM.4), closing the lane

> The HM lane shipped and closed inside Wave 7; only RC.5 remains open and none of the blocks below binds it. D175/D178/D180/D182 and the three HM gotcha blocks are verbatim. What still binds an open task stayed in STATE.md: the RC-lane D176/D177/D179/D181 and the RC.4 gotcha. Grep here by task id (`HM.1`…`HM.4`) or by filename (`src/shared/humanize.ts`, `src/x/settings/humanizer.ts`, `src/x/routes/humanizer.ts`, `extension/src/sidepanel/HumanizerCard.tsx`, `extension/src/humanize.ts`); the load-bearing half is also in codemap §3.1/§3.3/§3.4/§5/§9 and in `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md` §"The project humanizer".

- **D175** (HM.1 — binds HM.2/HM.3/HM.4 and any future verbatim promotion into `src/shared/`): **four resolutions.** **(a) The split has exactly one genuinely shared private, and the plan asked the right question.** `pickFrom` is used by both `humanize` and the anti-repeat `pickItem`; it is now `export`ed from `humanize.ts` and **imported, not re-exported, by `engine.ts`** — so the engine's public surface is byte-identical to before the move plus the moved names, and nothing outside can start depending on a helper that was private yesterday. The six regex constants the task block flagged as maybe-shared (`LETTER_RE`/`SPACE_RE`/`LETTERS_ONLY_RE`/`LEADING_NON_LETTERS_RE`/`TRAILING_NON_LETTERS_RE`/`URL_ISH_RE`) turned out to be humanizer-only; `EMOJI_RE`/`VAR_TOKEN_RE`/`VAR_SLOT_RE` are render-only. Clean cut. **(b) `jitterOdds` is re-exported from `engine.ts` too**, though it is NEW rather than moved (the plan's Design says "engine re-exports all of it"). Costless and consistent; the only consumer today imports it from the shim. **(c) A `Write` of a file containing `\u` escapes silently emits the LITERAL characters.** Moving `EMOJI_RE` verbatim turned its two escapes into a raw ZWJ + VS16 in the source — behaviour-identical, invisible in every diff view, and `Edit` could not fix it because the old/new strings normalize to the same bytes; `perl -CSD -i -pe 's/…\x{200D}…/…\\u200D…/'` did. **A green suite does not prove a verbatim move was verbatim — `od -c` the lines that carry escapes.** (The same trap bit this task's STATE/codemap prose.) **(d) The plans' repo counts are already stale before Wave 7's first task**: the suite is **1863**, not the "1855+" every HM/RC done-when quotes, because `61f9601 close wave 6` landed out-of-skill in between. D174 already says to re-derive every count from the current code at implementation time — HM.1 is the proof it was not paranoia.

- **D178** (HM.2 — binds HM.3/HM.4 directly, and every future `app_settings` JSON store): **three resolutions.** **(a) The `llm/settings.ts` defaults idiom is UNSAFE the moment the settings object holds an ARRAY, and copying it verbatim would have shipped the bug.** `getAiSettings` returns `{...DEFAULT_AI_SETTINGS}` safely only because every field there is a primitive; `DEFAULT_HUMANIZER` carries `prefixes` and `suffixes`, so a shallow spread hands every caller the module's own array instances and one `.push` edits the process-wide default. All three return paths go through a `cloneDefaults()` that re-copies both pools, and a test mutates a GET response then re-reads to prove it. **Any future store whose defaults contain an array or a nested object inherits this — the exemplar does not cover it.** **(b) The strict/lenient split is a SEMANTIC difference, not duplication.** `parseHumanizerConfig` (the core) reads STORED rows and falls back per field; `parseHumanizerPatch` (the store) reads a human PATCH and must 400. The core's `parseChance` stayed private and the route's `validChance` re-states the same range check on purpose — exporting one predicate for two opposite behaviours would invite a future edit that makes a typo'd chance silently keep the old value. This is the deliberate exception to §7 rule 4c, and it is annotated at both sites. HM.1's D175a clean cut (`pickFrom` is the *only* shared private) survives intact. **(c) `saveHumanizerSettings` takes a FULL settings object; the route merges over `getHumanizerSettings()` first.** So a partial row is unrepresentable — a reader never has to know which fields some past PATCH happened to touch, which matters because HM.3 reads this config on a click path where a missing field would mean "no jitter" rather than "default jitter". **Also worth carrying: `0` and `1` are legal chances**, so every check here is range-based; a truthiness test would make "never fire this jitter" unsettable (the D177(b) trap in a different file).

- **D180** (HM.3 — binds RC.4 directly, HM.4 through the api namespace, and every future task that promotes a shared core the extension already mirrors): **four resolutions.** **(a) HM.1 promoted the ENGINE and left the TYPE behind, and the plan didn't know.** `extension/src/shared/types.ts` has declared its own seven-field `HumanizerConfig` since RL.1 (the per-list config on `ReplyList.humanizer`), so the plan's "import `HumanizerConfig` from `../humanize.ts`" would have been a name collision — and adding `HumanizerSettings` on top of the *local* copy would have made the panel run one module while typing against a hand-kept twin. Resolved by deleting the local declaration: `import type … from '../humanize.ts'` + `export type { HumanizerConfig }`, the JD.6 `JudgeVerdict` precedent, so the extension build now has exactly ONE declaration. Type-only under `verbatimModuleSyntax`, so nothing new reaches the content IIFE. **The generalizable question for any promotion-into-`src/shared/` task: does the extension already mirror the TYPE you just made canonical? Promoting the code and leaving the mirror is how a drift becomes silent — and it only bites at the first task that runs the engine client-side, which is never the promotion task itself.** **(b) A docs rule fired two tasks earlier than the plan expected.** The plan gives all docs to HM.5, but `docs/radar-tab.md:75` QUOTES the pick hint (`copied ✓`) and HM.3 changed it — the Wave-3 carry-forward rule ("reword a quoted string and you owe the doc in the same commit") is not a docs-sync rule, it binds whoever moves the string. HM.3 therefore shipped the hint-line correction **plus** a short §"Humanize picks"; HM.5 still owes the Settings-card half, the round-trip story and the smoke. D171a's shape again from the other side: the question is not "is there a docs-sync task later", it is "did I just change something a doc quotes". **(c) The plan names `RadarRow` but the prop has to cross `RadarGroup`, and the Clicked list renders `RadarRow` too.** Both get `humanizer`. A re-copy from the Clicked view IS a fresh pick (it re-runs `markClicked`+`confirmDraft`+clipboard), so jittering in Queue but not in Clicked would make the clipboard and the recorded `replyTextEdited` disagree for the same row. The CHECKBOX stays queue-only as planned — where you set the flag and where the flag applies are different questions. **(d) `humanize` is called ONCE per pick and the single result feeds both the clipboard and `confirmDraft`.** Calling it twice would be the natural-looking edit (`writeText(humanize(...).text)` next to `confirmDraft(id, humanize(...).text)`) and would silently record a DIFFERENT jitter than the one that got copied — the measurement story ("what actually went out") is only true because there is one draw. Also: `PICK_NOTE_MS` is 2500, not RD.2's 1500 — the note now carries a list of jitter names to read.

- **D182** (HM.4 — binds HM.5 directly, and every future editor over a NON-registry config): **four resolutions.** **(a) A dirty-gated form and a saves-on-click toggle cannot share one re-seed key, and the plan put them in one form.** Task 4 says "`enabled` checkbox included" alongside "gate Save on `dirty`" — but `enabled` is the flag the Radar checkbox flips on click, and the four toggles it sits under in *Behavior & privacy* all save on click too. A checkbox that silently waits for Save in that neighbourhood is a footgun, so `enabled` PATCHes optimistically and only pools+chances are dirty-gated. That split has a consequence the RL.9 exemplar does not: `stored` is a fresh object after the toggle's PATCH, so the re-seed effect had to be keyed on **`JSON.stringify(configOf(stored))` — the config half ONLY**. Keying on the whole settings object would make every toggle wipe half-typed pools, which is precisely the failure RL.9 hardened against. **Generalizes: when one card mixes save-on-click and save-on-Save fields, the re-seed key must cover only the fields the form owns.** **(b) Chances are held as typed STRINGS, not numbers.** The exemplar uses range sliders so it can keep numbers; the plan asks for `type="number"` inputs, and `Number('0.')` is `NaN` — a numeric state eats the keystroke mid-decimal and the field can never reach `0.15` by typing. State is the raw string, `parseChanceInput` does empty/NaN/out-of-range in one place, and the field's own `invalid_*` code renders under it. **Any future typed-number field in this panel inherits this.** **(c) The client mirrors the route's bounds because it structurally cannot import them.** `MAX_POOL_ENTRIES` 25 / `MAX_POOL_ENTRY_CHARS` 60 live in `src/x/settings/humanizer.ts`, which the extension may not import (§5 build isolation — only `src/shared/` is shimmed, and the humanize core carries no bounds). Hand-synced copy, annotated as one, the same standing arrangement as the settings types. The guards exist to turn a refusal into a message under the field; the server stays the authority, and a 400 that still gets through re-reads GET (TuningPanel pattern) — **typed text survives that re-read because a failed PATCH leaves `storedKey` unchanged**, which is a property of (a), not luck. **(d) A doc the change makes WRONG is owed even when a docs-sync task exists** — the D180b rule, firing a second time in the same lane. `docs/settings-tab.md:9` enumerates what General contains, and a fifth panel made that list incomplete; having opened the file for a one-word fix, the §"Reply humanizer" section went in too rather than shipping a reachable card with no user-facing page for a commit. **HM.5 should tick its `docs/settings-tab.md` item as already paid and spend the session on `docs/radar-tab.md`, `docs/replies-tab.md`, PHASE-HISTORY, the `17672d1` codemap fold-in and the smoke.**

- **HM.4: what HM.5 inherits, and the one string in `dist` that now appears three times.** `docs/settings-tab.md` is **done** (§"Reply humanizer" + the General enumeration at L9) — HM.5 owes `docs/radar-tab.md`'s Settings-card half + round-trip story, `docs/replies-tab.md`'s one line on the project-level sibling, `docs/PHASE-HISTORY.md`, `scripts/smoke-humanizer.ts`, the browser pass, and the `17672d1` Playbook.tsx fold-in. The smoke's $0 claim is structural, not a promise: nothing under `/x/humanizer` can reach `xFetch` or `askLLM`, so per D171c it ships **no `--live` flag** (there is no paid call a keyless run fails to prove). Note for whoever greps the build: `'come out changed'` is now in `dist/sidepanel.js` **three** times (ReplyLists' odds line, the Radar checkbox, this card) — the codemap's HM.1 inline check is a `>= 1` test, so it still holds. The card renders `Reply humanizer` twice in the bundle (the `Section` title + the doc-matching copy); neither is asserted by a test.

- **HM.3: what RC.4 inherits in `Radar.tsx`.** `RadarSection` now holds a second async state (`humanizer`) and a second mount effect; `RadarGroup` and BOTH `RadarRow` call sites (queue groups + the Clicked list) carry a `humanizer` prop — a new prop on `RadarRow` is now a **three**-site edit, not two. `draftReplies` is byte-untouched, so RC.4's `sendBatch(rows, scoreById?)` refactor starts from the plan's shape. **Do not add jitter inside `curateAndDraft` (D172)** — curated rows are drafted, then picked through the same `onPick`, so they are humanized for free; a second call site would double-jitter. `RADAR_KEYS` is still the two batch caps — RC.4 adds `x.radar.curatedCount` there and to `ServerConfig`/`SERVER_DEFAULTS`/`readServerConfig` (D179c). Also: `dist/sidepanel.js` now contains `'come out changed'` **twice** (ReplyLists' odds line + the Radar checkbox), so the codemap's HM.1 inline check is a `>= 1` test, not `== 1`.

- **HM.2: the `'humanizer'` row is invisible to the settings platform, and HM.4 must not reach for it there.** It is an `app_settings` key like `'ai'`, not a registry entry — `GET /x/settings`, the mirrored blob, `useSettingsEditor` and `x_update_setting` all skip it by construction. So the Settings → General card talks to `api.humanizer.{get,patch,reset}` (an `api.ts` addition HM.3 or HM.4 makes, whichever runs first), and a `SettingsGear` over it renders nothing. Same for HM.3's Radar read. Its counts are unchanged for the same reason: **14 groups / 61 knobs / 27 mirrored, prompt keys 16, MCP 23** — HM.2 minted none of them.
